> VO is **LOCKED** (Alex's own voice, recorded + spliced). Scene onsets are **MEASURED**. Words cannot change.
> Style: SIMULATE-62 / TOOL-65 lineage — **ONE framed dark Panel** ([[reel-never-dual-screen]]),
> **sprites act the story out**, colourful + grounded in a real street ([[reel-cinematic-legup]]),
> **ONE hero object per scene** ([[reel-declutter-single-hero]]).

## THE STORY — **MAPLE ST · The Three Empty Slots**

**Logline.** One golden-hour block of local shops, one clay-orange vendor cart, and a viewer-hero who services
the whole street off a single reusable stencil and a carousel rig — while a knockoff superhero hand-designs
post number one, a middleman's invoice ribbon gets measurably longer every scene, and a shuttered door at the
end of the block with THREE EMPTY BRASS NAMEPLATE SLOTS waits to explain what the money was actually for.

**World.** ONE continuous main street at golden hour. The camera walks it **strictly LEFT→RIGHT across all 15
scenes**, so the camera's x-position on the street IS the progress bar. Shops in fixed order:
SMILE DEPOT → alley gap → PLANET SWOLE → RE/MIX REALTY → PIZZA PEPPI → laundromat → empty lot → the closed
ROLLER SHUTTER with its brass plaque. Across the road, cool and grey: BLOATCORP MEDIA.
Low raking sun from **camera-RIGHT** in every scene; every shadow rakes LEFT. The street gets visibly warmer
and richer from S2 to S15. Only two exits from the street: S14's rain flashback (same geography) and macro pushes.

|---|---|
| SMILE DEPOT | `#C96A2E` on cream `#EFEBE3`, giant cream molar sign |
| PLANET SWOLE | `#5B3F86` + gold `#E7B24C` trim, dumbbell-and-planet ring |

(cart, spray, tag knots, pools, postbox, wax) so the protagonist's colour always reads as his.

Polished warm **BRASS** (`#F0CB63` / `#E7B24C` / `#8A5F22`) + the travelling-glint-and-high-chime pair is legal
**ONLY** on: S1's satchel flap counter · S5's stencil plate · S14's plaque + three nameplates · S15's wax stamp.
Everything else is re-metalled: S6 pole/collars → **chrome**; S7 hopper cowl → **oxidised copper**;
S10 rail/nosing/bell → **chrome**; S11 scale → **nickel silver** with copper pans; S12 plate/shutters → **copper**;
S13 grommets → **galvanised steel**; S15 finial + POSTS plate → **cast iron painted clay**.

### ⭐ THE OPEN LOOP
**Planted** physically in S1 shot C2, never in words: the lateral money-run halts (6 frames, residual drift, never
a dead stop) on the one closed, unlit, bolted frontage on a street where every other door is throwing cash at him.
Above it, a small brass plaque with **THREE EMPTY RECESSED SLOTS** that glint once. A banknote blows across them.
Nobody is standing there.
**Kept alive** at S5 lf96-103 — a 7-frame, 60px, un-glinted sighting of the one dark frontage down the block as
the camera whips past. No plaque detail, no camera hesitation, zero pre-spend. (Without this the loop goes cold
for 23 seconds and S13 has to *explain* rather than *re-arm*.)
**Re-armed** at S13: the ripped billboard uncovers a faded hand-painted arrow pointing straight at the shutter.
**Paid off ONLY at S14**, in that exact object: three rejected rain-soaked flyers become the three brass
nameplates, three bolts throw, the shutter rattles up — and the three slots were always the first three clients.
The machine was never the lock. The door was, and the door took a couple of weeks of knocking.

### INTENSITY CURVE
`8 → 4 → 6 → 6 → 6 → 7 → 7 → 8 → 7 → 7 → 8 → 8 → 9 → 10 → 7`
Peak at **46.05s ≈ 88.7%** (the third nameplate clack). S2 is the deliberate floor and its coil now lands at
7.57s, not 10.9s. S4 raised 5→6 so the S4-S12 run is genuinely monotonic.

### THE FOUR WAVES — one axis each (this was the reel's biggest repeat)
| scene | wave | axis |
|---|---|---|
| S8 | string lights ignite | **lateral L→R** (first, earns it) |
| S10 | till drawers open | **Z-axis, near→far up-street** |
| S12 | meter flags flip green | **from mid-block OUTWARD, both directions from one origin** |
| S14 | nameplates land | **vertical, top-down**, matching the crane |

## SCENE CARDS

### S1 · HOOK — "THE NUMBER CHANGES MATERIAL"
**LINE + DUR:** 0.00–5.57 (5.57s, 167 frames, lf 0-166). *"There are people making $30,000 a month posting on Instagram for companies that don't want to do it themselves, and they're not designing any of it."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** Someone is quietly taking $30,000 a month off this ordinary street — it is not a claim, it is already banked, he never touches it — and the one door still shut has three empty brass slots waiting.

**HERO OBJECT:** the courier **SATCHEL** of cash on the clay scooter rack — bridle leather, two brass buckles, embossed Claude starburst, a mouth that hinges like a **JAW** (opens, chomps, swallows, refuses), and a recessed **BRASS FLAP COUNTER** (satchel-local 150,150, 250×86) — the endpoint of the material morph. Body left196 top372 w470 h300 at f48 → left168 top326 w548 h372 by f118. **Brass is legal here** (ledger).

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, the LEFT (opening) end of the block, staged from **inside the satchel's mouth** so the street streams past above the leather rim. World authored 1180 wide anchored x=-84. Road top y636, kerb y624, pavement 624-690 (sprites groundY 684), near tarmac 690-792 (scooter contact y742). Shutter local x[548,878] y[300,640], 17 slats; **BRASS PLAQUE** x[628,800] y[252,300] with three 46×26 recessed wells at x 640/690/740, y264, `inset 0 3px 5px rgba(0,0,0,0.65)` + a 2px lit brass lip, 1px separators. **NO TEXT ON THE PLAQUE, EVER.** String lights strung but **UNLIT** — S1 is the dimmest the street ever is.

Cold, unlit, bolted, dead leaves and three unopened letters at its foot, the single desaturated object on a warm street. f149/f152 the travelling beam crosses the **BRASS PLAQUE** and the slots glint left-to-right; they read as three dark voids precisely because they are the only part that does NOT light up. f154 a single banknote tumbles across the plaque and does not stop. **SPARKY sprints the full 1012px past the shutter through f148-160 — the camera is near-still, so the dog is the motion.**
- **f152-167 (C3) SNAP PULL-BACK.** camZ 1.30→0.90 over 15 frames (out-quint) on a world authored 1180 wide, so no edge is exposed. The whole block: four doors still swinging, the bulging satchel, the SOLD sign still dragging, Canva a tiny caped heap in his own confetti, Bloatcorp cool grey, and at the right end one dark shuttered door. String lights stay UNLIT. On the exact frame of *"not designing any of it"* the shrink-wrapped tablet catches a hard specular so the wrinkles and coffee ring POP.

**CAMERA:** macro push (1.16→1.32) → lateral tracking dolly at scooter speed with camZ creeping 1.03→1.055, a 1.5px road buzz and 0.6° roll → hard decel + macro push to 1.34 → whip-pan to a residual drift → snap pull-back to 0.90. **Bleed solved backwards:** origin panel-local (384.6, 491); at peak 1.34 nothing load-bearing outside x[100,720]; at 0.90 authored x=0 maps to 50.6, hence the 1180-wide world at x=-84. Audit at f138, not on a static frame.

**LIGHT:** hard low golden key from camera-RIGHT (warm `#FFD79A`) against cool sky fill `#8FA7BE` from the left — the hero is back-rim-lit, blown warm edge right, cool slate bounce left. Shop shadows rake LEFT as `skewX(-38deg)` trapezoids travelling with P3. Two volumetric god-rays at x~218 and x~700 (three overlapping bars, `rotate(-14deg)`, `rgba(255,224,170,0.30)`→0, dust motes) STROBE the leather at f68/f92/f114. Every door-bang is a light event: a warm `Pool()` spills the interior onto the pavement. The colour story INVERTS at the end — the shutter is the only cold frontage, and the brass plaque is the one warm-metal object inside that cold patch.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P0 FG blur 6px, 1.6× (scooter silhouette 340×260 = 88,400px², hydrant, lamppost base, awning corner) · P1 HERO locked (satchel + flap counter + straps + rack + tablet; ~62% of panel, 86% at macro) · P2 street actors 1.0×, groundY 684, **one owner at a time at x858** · P3 shopfronts 0.72× (a 1012×310 band = 313,000px² continuously travelling) · P4 mid-far 0.22× (BLOATCORP; omitted if it falls outside x[-300,1312]) · P5 far 0.10× (skyline, water tower, clock tower at x~720 planting S11).

**ESCALATION:** opens PEAK-ADJACENT from frame zero — no establishing wide. Internally it escalates on the TRANSFORMATION: chalk (erasable, free, the material of every guru promise) → paper (real, countable, thrown by real people) → engraved brass (fixed, banked, permanent). Payment escalates per door: one folded note → a two-handed brick that CLANGS and recoils the hero 14px → a bundle with a SOLD sign that then drags → a greasy sack plus a refusal, on which the buckle POPS. Then the anti-escalation that creates the loop: the light goes cold and the one door that is not paying him glints once.

**GAG:** **CLAUDE NEVER TOUCHES THE MONEY.** Hands in pockets f48→f167; his only two actions in four seconds are one nod at f88 and setting a coffee cup down at f138 — on a design tablet still sealed in shrink-wrap with a retail sticker and a coffee ring on the plastic. The SATCHEL does all the work, and it SNAPS SHUT in Pizza Peppi's face when he offers pizza. Punchline: **CAPTAIN CANVA** — the only character actually designing, the only one not being paid — reaches the door exactly one frame after it slams.

**LIVING WORLD:** Bloatcorp's grid with one designer face-down (he never wakes, not once, in the whole reel). Laundromat drum turning, neon on a dying ballast. A seagull hits the laundromat glass at f100 and slides down it. A delivery trike crosses the far plane. Chalk dust in the key light. Pidgey rides the flap, thieves a chip off the chalk tray, leaves with one note. Bunting between two lampposts. Chalk nubs, confetti and one loose banknote keep travelling long after the characters that produced them have gone.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **$30,000** — ONE number through three materials, never legible in two states at once. Shop signage is ICON-FIRST at hero scale, wordmarks present only as raked sun-glared set dressing in P3 (which also pre-plants the S14 nameplate glyphs). **ZERO text on the plaque.**

**MUTE CHECK:** a giant chalk number is knocked over by a fat pigeon and its digits turn into flying cash; the cash pours into a leather bag that CHOMPS like a mouth while four shop doors bang open and hurl money at it; a buckle pops; a caped man with a ruler runs face-first into a door and his artwork explodes into confetti; the guy riding the bag never takes his hands out of his pockets; the counter locks in brass; then everything slows to a crawl on the one shuttered, unlit door, where a brass plaque shows **THREE EMPTY HOLES** and nobody is standing there. ✓

**TRANSITION IN:** **COLD OPEN.** Frame 0 is already a macro with camZ 1.16 and the push in motion. First audible cue is a chalk-grain riser, not a whoosh. Internal transitions: the 4-frame topple-WHIP at f44 (CutWipe idiom, so the street reveal reads as physically CAUSED by the bird) and the f146 whip-pan motivated by the departing brass specular. Hand S2 a left-side start.

**SFX:** L[0]=0.00 (panel-local == root here, but still emit `L[0]+local`; the cloned root cue map is DELETED). f0 warm street tone + chalk-grain riser · f26 wing flutter + wooden knock · f34-46 wooden A-frame CLATTER (**do NOT cap the dur** — this library's wood tail runs past 1.6s) + seven chalk-to-paper "fwip"s 2 frames apart · f48 whip-whoosh into a continuous scooter bed · **four DOOR-BANGS on a 14-frame metronome at f52/f66/f80/f94**, each with its own weight, and four SATCHEL CHOMPS 6 frames later, each a semitone lower as the bag fills · f72 metallic CLANG + fishtail scrape · f92 flat wooden SMACK + confetti flutter · f96 comedic spring-boing on the pizza refusal · f104 buckle POP · f106 SOLD sign on tarmac then a continuous drag-scrape · f129 tyre scrub · f130-140 six brass CLACKs right-to-left, decelerating and pitching down · **f138 the bed DIPS 6dB (not to silence — a hole here is a scroll cue) for one ceramic clink** · f149/f152 two high brass GLINT ticks · f158 sub-thump under the pull-back; f164 corgi paws + a distant horn. All under the per-frame VO sidechain duck baked from Alex's waveform (1.0 in gaps → 0.38 on speech, ROOT-indexed, −12 to −17dB).

**WHY THIS:** the only version where the transformation IS the argument of the hook is a number changing MATERIAL. Chalk is the material of a claim; paper is that claim in flight, thrown by real people; brass is it banked. One object, one number, three states, with the morph straddling the A→B cut so the eye cannot lose it. That pays four debts at once: frame zero is a stun; shot B's substance is the by-product of shot A, so a 3-second lateral run never has to justify itself; the open loop is handed off **for free**, because the final material is brass and the glint that leaves the counter travels to the one other brass thing on the street, which is EMPTY; and staging from INSIDE the satchel's mouth is what lets a lateral street run pass the 55%-fill rule that street scenes normally fail — the street stays parallax and never becomes the base object.

**REJECTED:** **PHONE → CASH**, the obvious creator morph. (1) A phone is the base of S2's tripod totem — burning it here breaks rule 2 before the reel starts. (2) It is the single most-seen visual in the AI-money category, so frame zero reads as a scroll-past. (3) It has no third state: phone→cash is a two-beat swap with nowhere to escalate, whereas chalk→paper→brass ends on the MATERIAL the open-loop object is made of. Also rejected: opening on Canva at 2am (better joke, but it buries $30,000 two seconds deep behind a character nobody cares about yet and opens at LOW intensity), and staging the money as a bank-balance screen (a card with a number on it; makes the money abstract at the exact moment it must be physical objects flying through the air).

### S2 · PROBLEM — "THE TRIPOD TOTEM"
**LINE + DUR:** 5.57–11.12 (5.55s, 166 frames, lf 0-165). *"Every business needs social posts, but they all hate making them, so they all pay somebody, and most of them are paying way too much for it."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** Four shops on one block are failing at the same job by hand, on one absurd tower they built together — and the tape holding it up is what somebody else is billing them for.

**HERO OBJECT:** the **TRIPOD TOTEM** — one jury-rigged phone rig ASSEMBLED ON SCREEN out of the wrong objects, one storey per shop, never cut away from and never reset: dumbbell base (gym) → office chair on castors → broom handle with a 3-tomato-tin collar and one fat matte-grey **GAFFER-TAPE WAD** (pizzeria) → a 300px SOLD-sign crossbar with Batbro's cape corner snagged (realtor) → a dental x-ray lightbox head at the summit with the taped phone under it and a browned-out ring light sagging off a bent mic arm (dentist). Final silhouette 550px tall (69% of panel) × 340px, base pivot (500,700), top y=150 — 3.7× taller than anything else in frame. **No other scene owns the verb COLLAPSE.**

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, same block, camera one shop-width further right — but golden hour has been switched OFF: a cloud bank rolls the street to overcast slate. This is the reel's tone floor. Facades cropped to their lower two-thirds in locked order: SMILE DEPOT x0-250 (spit sink at x215), PLANET SWOLE x250-500 (treadmill running with nobody on it), RE/MIX x500-740 (**a burst gutter joint above the awning, dripping steadily from frame zero** — the reel's only rain is S14's flashback, so the payload must have an on-screen cause), PIZZA PEPPI x740-1012 (3-step stoop). Alley slot x470-560 carrying a dead grey god-ray column until lf112. Pavement y676, kerb y690, tarmac 690-792. Owners at their own doors, groundY 690. **VITO owns the near-right pavement lane and never leaves it: x≥830, groundY 772, size 200** — a full plane in front of and below every other figure, so he can never occlude one. Far-left edge: a 30px unmoving sliver of cyan cape.

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-16 (A) MACRO DOLLY-IN.** camScale 1.55→1.42, origin (430,560), +2px handheld sway. Frame filled by a phone gaffer-taped to three dented tomato tins on Peppi's stoop, tins x400-470 y430-700, hot chrome specular on the left third. Phone screen is a blank slate rectangle with ONE blinking red dot — no UI. **The TAPE WAD at (470,430) is deliberately fat and matte: plant it hard.** Peppi holds a pizza board up with ONE hero slice. Bleed contract at 1.55×: content inside x[152,805] y[199,710].
- **lf16-39 (A-gag) THE SLIP + THE THEFT.** lf20 the top tin skids 24px; lf24-33 the stack buckles and the phone FACE-PLANTS into the pizza (wet slap, 3-frame ±5px shake). In the same arc Pidgey lifts the hero slice out of Peppi's claw mid-fall (lf28) so the board he is left holding is EMPTY — and he is still smiling at it. Sparky sprints the full 1012px on the far pavement behind the stoop, lf14-30.
- **lf39-52 (B) WHIP-PAN, STOREY TWO.** No cut. A green hand grabs phone-plus-tape-wad and the camera whips LEFT with it: 4 frames of streak blur motion-matched to the grab (430px of travel), landing at camScale 1.22 origin (500,520). The same phone is now taped to a broom jammed into a dumbbell. One storey has become two on a continuous object.
- **lf52-72 (B-gag) THE SAG + THE RIP.** BULK clamps a 190-dia RING LIGHT on a bent mic arm. It blooms hot white for 6 frames then **BROWNS OUT** — white → `#F7F3EA` → amber → a sickly 4Hz flicker — and the arm droops 34° like a wilted sunflower, its 340×180 gold Pool sliding off his chest onto the pavement (61,200px² at 7px/f). **He holds a full flexed pose in total darkness while the only well-lit thing in his own photoshoot is the floor, and does not adjust it.** lf66 he tries to tear a strip of tape and RIPS THE WHOLE ROLL IN TWO.
- **lf58-72 ⭐ THE EARLY MORPH (the S2 fix).** At lf58, as the ring light clamps on, a 16px strip of the same grey tape peels off the roll, whips right, and lands in Vito's paw **already paper-white with a red total bar on it** — a short 200px ribbon. He fans himself with it, unhurried, and the ribbon keeps paying out from the totem for the rest of the scene. **The tether between their effort and his bill is on screen from 7.57s, not 10.9s.**
- **lf72-87 (C) THE KICK-UNDER.** Sparky sprints the full width at ~14px/f in the road band (groundY 772, head-top y≈680, clear of every owner's feet at 690) and FACEPLANTS the dumbbell at lf80; the rig lurches 9° and Batbro kicks an office chair under it as a dolly. Behind, a 540×88 delivery van crosses the far road lf72-96. Two ≥40,000px² movers in the reel's weakest bucket, delivered by staging.
- **lf87-108 (D) CRANE UP — FOUR STOREYS, FOUR FAILURES.** camScale 1.22→1.06, origin drifting to (506,470). The totem tops out with the SOLD-sign crossbar and the x-ray lightbox throwing a hard COLD clinical rectangle. All four owners stand with arms still raised from feeding it a part: Flossula recoiling from his own lightbox glare, Bulk holding two half-rolls, Batbro tangled in his grapple line, Peppi still presenting the empty board. lf96 Flossula's phone arcs into the spit sink — one polite splash.
- **lf108-130 (E) THE LEAN + THE ONE WARM LIGHT.** camScale 1.06→0.98. The tallest object built so far spans y150-700 and starts a creaking lean toward camera. lf112 the SINGLE warm key snaps on in the near-right lane and finds **VITO** (x930→x880, groundY 772, size 200, pinstripes, clay tie, fedora, cigar curl) fanning himself at 3 fans/sec while everything behind him falls. **He is the only warm-lit object in the frame.** lf120 Pidgey lands on the lightbox — the last straw.
- **lf130-155 (F) TOPPLE, THEN TAPE BECOMES BILL.** The totem pivots at (500,700) and rotates 0→-68° over lf130-147 (in-quad) into a 3-frame bounce, tip landing at (395,782) in the EMPTY near lane. **187,000px² sweeping 68°.** Impact: ±7px shake decaying over 10f, two expanding dust ellipses, tins scattering, one rolling to the lens; the clipped awning dumps the gutter's held water on Batbro at lf149. **THE MORPH COMPLETES, SLOWED AND ANCHORED:** over lf134-156 (22 frames, not 12) the tape wad end stays PINNED to the fallen totem at (470,430) while only the far end travels — one object physically connecting the wreck to Vito's hand — widening **16px → 96px** (not 54), cross-fading matte grey → paper `#F7F3EA`, faint ruled ticks and a red total bar fading in (ZERO legible figures at any zoom). **3-frame hit-stop on the whole frame at lf150** as it slaps into his hand. They built the bill themselves, and it is an umbilical, not a dart.
- **lf155-165 (G) THE RIBBON TAKES THE STREET + FOUR WALLETS.** Vito fans himself, eyes locked on camera. The trailing ribbon (ledger: **120px**) lassoes the fire hydrant at lf158; the hydrant cap pops at lf163. An offcut flutters onto Bulk, flat on his back: he bench-presses it, RIPS IT IN HALF, and both halves settle back onto him, one on each side. He stares at the sky. lf156-165 the four owners reach for their wallets in a left-to-right rolling wave at 3-frame stagger, each hand stopping 12px SHORT of Vito's column. He does not look at any of them. Last frame: he tips the fedora at the viewer, ribbon stretched taut across the road like a barrier — the exact thing S3's cart rolls straight through.

**CAMERA:** three moves welded into ONE continuous operator take — no hard cut anywhere, which is what makes the totem legible as one growing object. (A) macro dolly-in 1.55→1.42 origin (430,560). (B) whip-pan left, 4 frames of streak blur motion-matched to the grab, settling at 1.22 origin (500,520). (C) crane up + pull back 1.22→0.98, origin drifting to (506,430), plus a ±5px shake at the faceplant and ±7px decaying at the impact. **Vito's arrival is deliberately NOT a camera move — the camera settles and he does the moving.** Bleed: at 1.55/origin(430,560) content in x[152,805] y[199,710]; at 1.22/origin(500,520) x[90,920] y[94,743]; because C ends UNDER 1.0 a backdrop layer sits at 1.06 behind everything. Audit at lf0 (peak).

**LIGHT:** the scene is the sun going out, which does the VO's last clause for free. An overcast slab rolls `rgba(58,92,132, 0→0.30)` across lf0-88; shop shadows soften and vanish; SMILE DEPOT's neon dies at lf66. Every remaining source is a FAILING one — the ring light browns through amber to a stuttering die and slides off its owner onto the floor; the x-ray box throws a hard cold rectangle Flossula physically recoils from; Peppi's neon drops out for 2 frames at lf18 and lf41; Bloatcorp is flat dead fluoro. Against all that, ONE hard warm clay/gold key snaps on at lf112 with a visible volumetric shaft through the alley gap, dust motes, bloom at its mouth — lighting **VITO alone**, with a travelling 260×120 amber Pool under his feet and a hot rim down his right side. Everyone paying is in shadow; the only man being paid is the only warm thing on Maple St. Every "empty" fill is slate-washed, never neutral grey.

**DEPTH PLANES:** 1 FG blur 2.5-3.5px, 1.35× (clay hydrant with gold cap, scooter wheel, torn awning corner, a bag tumbling lf100-140) · 2 HERO 1.0× (the totem growing y700→y150, its debris, the tape→invoice ribbon) · 3 street actors (four owners groundY 690 sizes 158-176; Vito in his exclusive lane x≥830 groundY 772; Sparky groundY 772; Pidgey) · 4 shopfronts 0.55× · 5 across-the-road 0.35× (Bloatcorp, **omitted if outside x[-300,1312]**) · 6 far city 0.18× (clock tower ticking once, **water tower held in the EXACT screen position it occupied on S1's final frame**, overcast slab, the delivery van).

**ESCALATION:** S1 ended on a working street at intensity 8, cash flying INTO the satchel. S2 inverts the direction of every force while raising the physical stakes. Same block, one shop right — but the four shops turn inward and fail the SAME task at once, and the failure gets literally TALLER every beat: one storey → two → three → four → until the tallest object built so far falls on them. S1's biggest mover was a scooter; S2's is a 187,000px² tower sweeping 68°. Where S1's escalation was money arriving, S2's is **MASS ACCUMULATING** — more junk, more tape, more storeys, less function — closing with money about to leave in the wrong direction. Intensity drops on TONE exactly as the arc demands while kinetic energy climbs monotonically into the topple, so the dip is a coil and not a dead bucket.

**GAG:** the whole tower is built out of things that are emphatically NOT camera equipment, and every part is traceable to the shop that donated it — you can read the block's inventory off one leaning object. Payoff: the roll of tape holding their disaster together becomes the invoice. Toppers: Bulk holds a full flexed pose in total darkness while his own ring light droops until the warm pool slides off his chest onto the pavement, leaving the pavement beautifully lit and him invisible, and he does not adjust it; he rips the tape roll in half, then the invoice in half, ending with two of each. And Pidgey lifts the hero slice out of Peppi's claw mid-fall, so the only thing the rig was ever pointed at is an empty board Peppi is still smiling at.

**LIVING WORLD:** Peppi's neon drops out at lf18 and lf41. SMILE DEPOT's OPEN dies the instant the cloud shadow completes. A lone treadmill belt runs with nobody on it; a protein tub tips off a shelf at lf74. A delivery van crosses lf72-96. Sparky faceplants the dumbbell then stands on the wreck with his tail up. The burst gutter drips from frame zero and finally dumps on Batbro. The clock tower ticks once during the crane. Bloatcorp's grid glows cold with one designer permanently face-down, and two windows go dark in sequence as the totem falls, as if watching. At the far-left edge a 30px sliver of cyan cape sits completely unmoving all scene — Canva, one shop behind, not looking up once.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **NONE.** Zero numerals, zero labels. S2 carries no spine entry and the arc's instruction to drop any agency price figure is honoured — the invoice is ruled ticks and a red bar, never digits, at any zoom. The phone screen is a blank slate with one red dot, never UI. Any implied self-timer is a sweeping ring, never a digit, so the numeral 3 stays reserved for S14.

**MUTE CHECK:** four shopfronts, four owners with their arms up, one absurd leaning tower of household junk with a phone taped to the top. It falls over. The tape holding it together stretches across the street, turns into a piece of paper, and lands in the hand of the one calm man standing in the only patch of sunlight — and four hands come out with wallets. ✓ The totem is 3.7× the next tallest object and sits in the central 40% of panel width, so there is zero ambiguity about where to look.

**TRANSITION IN:** from S1's snap pull-back — the widest frame so far — S2 punches to the tightest. Chassis CutWipe retinted for the weather: a 5-frame 216px gradient band travelling left-to-right (cream → `rgba(58,92,132)`, trailing `rgba(10,17,32,0.66)` slab, amber leading edge) plus a 2-frame dip to 0.10 black. **The cloud shadow IS the cut.** Same tarmac, same shop order, light one notch cooler, camera one shop right, water tower pinned to its S1 screen position.

**SFX:** emit as **L[1] + local** (L[1]=5.57). 0.00 overcast bed + distant traffic (−22dB) · 0.42 tin scrape · 0.62 three descending tin hits + wet phone slap · 0.72 wingbeat + indignant coo + short descending slide-whistle · 1.30 whip whoosh (4f, stereo R→L) · 1.80 brown-out buzz + fluorescent stutter sliding down over 0.8s · 2.20 tape RIIIP · 2.66 corgi skid + heavy dumbbell CLONK · 2.90 low crane riser, 1.6s tail — **do NOT cap `dur`** · 3.20 distant clock tick · 3.44 polite splash · 3.70 rising timber creak · **4.35 THE TOPPLE — multi-layer wood-and-metal crash; MEASURE the library file first and set dur+rel ≥ 90% of true length** · 4.42 dust whump + water slosh · 4.60 tape unspool zip, rising, 0.9s · 5.00 paper snap on the morph + a 3-frame hit-stop silence at 5.00-5.10 · 5.16 three lazy fan flaps · 5.28 hydrant clink + spurt · 5.20/5.30/5.40/5.50 four wallet snaps, quickening · 5.52 hat-brim flick. Per-cue volumes re-derived for THIS reel's onset density. VO duck ROOT-indexed.

**WHY THIS:** one continuous object for 5.55 seconds, never cut away from and never reset, with a single fat tape wad as the identity thread the eye tracks from lf8 to lf165 while the MEANING of the object changes four times — a stack, a rig, a monument to wasted effort, a bill. That does the VO's clauses structurally instead of illustratively: "every business needs social posts" = four shops feeding parts into one shared object; "they all hate making them" = the object gets worse the more they add; "paying way too much" = the object's own binding material becomes the invoice, so the cost is visibly made OUT OF the effort. The tone floor is the arc's designated scroll-away risk, so it is carried by a 187,000px² tower sweeping 68°, a full-width corgi sprint, a 47,520px² van and a 61,200px² light pool sliding — large movers by staging, not more props — and the thesis morph is pulled forward to 7.57s so the idea arrives before the swipe decision, not after it.

**REJECTED:** (1) a four-panel grid of phone screens, one per shop — a card-with-words composition, a head-on collision with S7's fanned deck, and critically it contains no transformation at all: four states side by side is not one state changing. (2) Cutting between the four shops as separate vignettes, or holding one sustained wide — the cut version buys four static tableaux and four base objects in one scene; the wide is a static hold that spends its reveal on frame zero, and you cannot perform a gag in a wide where every actor is 150px tall, which kills both the ring-light droop and the tape morph. Also rejected on rule 2: promoting VITO to hero here — his engine is the escalating wrong-tool ladder across S4/S8/S9/S13/S15, and basing a scene on him would pre-spend his S4 claw-machine reveal.

### S3 · PROMISE — "THE CART OPENS AND THE SHADOWS STAND UP"
**LINE + DUR:** 11.12–13.17 (2.05s, 61 frames, lf 0-60). *"Here's how you make the posts with Claude in under one minute."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** One folded bundle unfolds itself into a working shop, and the moment its light hits the road the street's own shadows stand up as paying customers.

**HERO OBJECT:** the clay-orange **VENDOR CART** — wheeled in as a strapped FOLDED SLAB and unfolded, in one unbroken mechanical cascade, into a lit street stall. Open footprint x180-560 / y452-702: rivet-banded body on a 172° `#E08A5F`/`#D2724E`/`#8E4126` gradient, cream beadboard inset, chrome kick-rail, 8-spoke carriage wheels r46 with gold hubs, a counter shelf sliding to x676 with a small bell, a **short off-centre stub pole at x358 that never rises above the canopy hem**, and a **SMOOTH ROUND STRETCHED PARASOL DOME** spanning x20-760, single-colour clay, scalloped hem, no visible ribs from below, underside carrying the Claude starburst. Hero bbox fills ~45% flat, ~57% at peak camZ 1.14. ⚠️ **Deliberately differentiated from S6:** S3 = smooth dome + stub pole; S6 = faceted 8-gore cone + full vertical pole + bulb rim. **The luggage tag is REMOVED from S3** — it belongs to S6 and S15.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, road level, mid-block, one shop right of S2 — the open tarmac between SMILE DEPOT and RE/MIX. Tarmac y648-792 in warm clay-tinted `#3A3330` with a cream centre line. Sun hard and low from camera-right so every shadow rakes LEFT, which lets **four customer shadows lie on the road from frame zero pointing at the cart** as innocent set dressing. Ground line y706, deep pavement lane y604, shopfront ground y648. Vito is ABSENT (arc containment). The shuttered end appears only as a 52px unlit sliver at the far right — no plaque, no glint. **Zero numerals:** no timer, no clock face, no readable hands ("under 1 minute" belongs to S7).

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-10 THE BUNDLE ARRIVES.** camZ 1.02, origin 50%/78%, roll -1.6°. YOU-CLAUDE enters from x-140 pushing a strapped folded slab (300×210, two leather straps, **the shrink-wrapped tablet bungeed on top with a coffee ring on the plastic and a cup balanced on it**) to x210 by lf10 at ~35px/f. This is the only scene where he visibly strains. lf9 BOTH BUCKLES POP. lf10 Pidgey drops off the molar sign, snatches one strap mid-air and exits top-right. Four raking shadows already lie at x724/842/940/1000, each a 190×46 quad `skewX(-62deg)` pointing LEFT at the cart.
- **lf2-14 ⭐ THE GAG STARTS EARLY (fix).** Canva enters the deep pavement lane from x-80 at **lf2** with T-square and mood board held up like credentials; Sparky enters the same lane from x620 at **lf6** at 43px/f; **the herding lands at lf14 (0.47s)** — a corgi dragging a superhero backwards out of frame while the cart is still unfolding its flanks. The gag runs UNDER the transformation instead of interrupting it, so S3 has a laugh in its first half-second.
- **lf10-22 IT GROWS A BODY.** Two side flanks swing down on piano hinges (0→96°, lf11-19) into chassis skirt and wheel chocks; body widens scaleX 300→380; two folding legs hit tarmac at lf20 with a clunk and 40px dust puffs. 3-frame body rock. camZ →1.07.
- **lf22-34 IT GROWS A MOUTH.** The front face splits like a jaw. The UPPER flap hinges up (rotateX 0→104°, out-cubic) revealing the serving window; its underside now faces the low camera and carries the scene's only label — **OPEN, 34px, on the flap's lower edge, no glint sweep** (fix: it must read as signage, not as a title card). The LOWER flap hinges down; the counter shelf slides to x676 with a cream cloth and a bell that bounces at lf33. camZ →1.11.
- **lf30-45 IT GROWS A ROOF AND FIRES THE LIGHT.** lf30-38 the stub pole seats and the dome's centre lifts to y240 as the camera cranes with it (translateY 0→-26, camZ →1.14). lf36-45 the stretched dome blooms open to x20-760 — a smooth skin, not ribs — with a 0.5-frame stagger around the hem so it reads as a spiral rather than a pop, overshoot 1.05 at lf44. Underside cream `#F7F3EA` with the 12-point Claude starburst r150 at (390,304) behind a 260px `rgba(255,226,164,0.42)` bloom. **lf43-48 THE LIGHT EVENT:** `Pool(390,748,w620,h150)` 0→0.85 spills warm clay across the tarmac, brass up-lights, one lens flare at lf45, the alley god-ray widens 1.3×. **NO shop, string or street light turns on** — that is S8's entire payoff.
- **lf42-53 THE LIGHT BECOMES A QUEUE.** lf42-47 LENS SNAP: camZ 1.14→0.94 over 5 frames (out-quart), origin sliding to 50%/62%. Not a cut — the cart never leaves frame. Then the shadows STAND UP, staggered: each quad shrinks width 190→0 and skew 62→0 while its owner rises at the shadow's ROOT (`transformOrigin 50%/100%`, scaleY 0.04→1.06→1.00, 3-frame fade, dust puff at each pair of feet). lf43-49 **FLOSSULA** at the counter (x724, groundY 724, size 240) — recoils instantly from the bright dome and lifts his mirror to shield his eyes, then checks his fangs (**recoil use 1 of 5**). lf45-51 **BATBRO** at **x884, groundY 712, size 196** (moved right per the merge fix). lf47-53 **BULK** at **x966, groundY 700, size 168, deliberately cropped 40% by the panel edge** — which also improves the queue's depth read.
- **lf50-60 THE BUTTON AND THE HOOK.** lf50 Batbro's grapple fires; lf51 his cape catches the **rim stay at (760,306)** (moved from 664 — at a 20° lean his upper body now sits near x820, ~96px clear of Flossula's head at x724); lf52 the line snaps taut and he stands frozen at a 20° lean for the rest of the scene, boots on the ground, tethered to the shop he is queuing at. **lf52 YOU-CLAUDE puts BOTH HANDS IN HIS APRON POCKETS** and leans on the counter — he does nothing from here to the end. lf54-60 settle: dome breathes 3°, bell rocks, camZ →0.965. **AND THE HOOK: the FOURTH shadow at x1000 is still lying flat, still pointing at the cart, and only begins to twitch at lf58-60.**

**CAMERA:** ONE unbroken low hero move split by a single lens-snap. (A) lf0-42 road-level dolly push, camZ 1.02→1.14, origin (506,618), translateX -18→0, crane lift translateY 0→-26 tied to the pole. (B) lf42-60 lens-snap pull-back 1.14→0.94 in 5 frames (out-quart), origin sliding to (506,491), settling to 0.965. **Bleed solved backwards at 1.14 about (506,618):** dome right rim x760→795.6 ✓, apex y228→173.4 ✓, counter tip x676→699.8 ✓; dome LEFT rim x20→-48.4 is a deliberate off-panel overfill with nothing load-bearing there. Queue sprites exist only from lf43, i.e. only at camZ ≤ 0.965: Bulk's right edge x1050→1019 (intentionally cropped). Backdrop over-rendered x-100..1112 / y-70..862.

**LIGHT:** hard low golden KEY from camera-RIGHT (sun disc 905,196 r46, bloomed) — the reason four raking shadows can lie on the tarmac pointing at the cart as set dressing. Cool slate FILL opposing it (`rgba(58,92,132,0.16)`), so the clay body carries a warm key edge and a cool rim, with a 5px cream rim down the right silhouette. One 46px god-ray at 14° out of the alley gap with visible dust, widening 1.3× during the bloom; a weaker second shaft across the RE/MIX roofline. The scene's ONE light event is the dome firing at lf43-48. Long raking ground shadow: 560×38 skewed ellipse at (380,752) plus a 44° hard bar running off-frame left. Bloatcorp stays cold grey.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P0 FG 1.22× blur 3px (hydrant x-10..76, striped bollard + van wheel arch x892..1012, kerbstone with a skittering leaf) · P1 HERO 1.0× (the cart in five states + YOU-Claude x600 groundY 744 size 236) · P2 street actors 0.62× (the four raking shadows and the three figures that stand out of them; plus a DEEP PAVEMENT LANE at ground y604 owned exclusively by Sparky and Canva during lf2-38 only) · P3 shopfronts 0.34× (SMILE DEPOT with stuttering neon, alley gap, PLANET SWOLE with a treadmill silhouette, RE/MIX as SHAPES only, the 52px unlit shutter sliver) · P4 mid-cool 0.20× (Bloatcorp, one designer face-down and motionless for all 61 frames) · P5 far 0.10× (sky gradient, clock tower silhouette, sun disc, two shafts, three birds).

**ESCALATION:** S2 bottomed out at 4 — four rigs COLLAPSING under overcast. S3 climbs to 6 by running that motion backwards and one rung past it: S2's tripod falls DOWN, S3's cart stands UP; S2's failures produced nothing, S3's assembly produces DEMAND inside the same two seconds. The internal ladder is strictly monotonic and each stage triggers the next — body → mouth → roof → light → people. The camera escalates with it: pushing in for the whole build, then snapping back to reveal the build had an audience. Colour escalates too: S2 was cool grey with one warm spotlight on the villain; S3 is full golden hour and lights Maple St's first warm pool. It is the first scene where a machine WORKS.

**GAG:** three, none needing a word. (1) **THE SHADOW QUEUE** — the customers don't walk in; four shadows that have been lying on the tarmac since frame zero STAND UP into three owners in 0.3s with a pitched-up three-thump ladder. (2) **SPARKY HERDS CAPTAIN CANVA** — the only character actually designing sprints in with a T-square held up like credentials and a corgi physically herds him back out, legs windmilling, mood-board pages fluttering off. (3) **BATBRO'S CAPE** — he grapples in to skip the line, hooks the dome rim stay, and spends the last nine frames standing in the queue at a permanent 20° lean, tethered to the shop he is queuing at, pretending it is normal, and nobody looks at him.

**LIVING WORLD:** Pidgey's one theft (a popped strap). SMILE DEPOT's neon on a 7-frame flicker. A treadmill silhouette running in PLANET SWOLE's window. The RE/MIX balloon bobbing. A bus flashing across the far cross-street, visible ONLY through the 22px alley gap at lf38-41. Dust turning in the god-ray. One Bloatcorp designer face-down for all 61 frames and not moving once — that is the joke — while a blind flaps beside him. A leaf skittering along the foreground kerbstone. The counter bell still rocking on the last frame.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **OPEN** — 34px, on the flap's lower edge, no glint sweep, arriving as part of the mechanism (its face is physically hidden until the flap opens). Nothing else.

**MUTE CHECK:** a man wheels in a strapped folded slab, it unfolds itself into a lit market stall on a sunny shop street, and the instant its light hits the road three shadows on the tarmac stand up into paying customers with a fourth still on its way — while a caped superhero who tried to cut in is dragged off by a corgi and a second caped guy gets snagged hanging off the umbrella. ✓ Cropping to the hero gives an unmistakable mushroom-topped barrow.

**TRANSITION IN:** motion-inverse match cut off S2, no wipe and no flash. S2's last frame is the totem COLLAPSING downward under a crane-up; S3's first ten frames are that energy backwards — a slab arriving at 35px/f and its buckles popping outward. The cut lands on the strap pop at lf9 so the audio transient carries it. Colour flips at the cut: overcast with one warm spot → full golden hour (intensity 4→6).

**SFX:** emit as **L[2] + local** (L[2]=11.12). 0.00 tarmac wheel rumble · 0.07 corgi paws entering the deep lane · 0.30 STRAP-POP double snap + wing flap · 0.40 metal flank CLUNK ×2 · 0.47 comic scuffle-and-herd (paws + fluttering paper) · 0.67 folding legs THUD + grit · 0.75 hatch whoosh + latch CLICK · 0.83 counter flap THUD, bell ting at 1.10 · 1.00 ratchet ZIP · **1.20 dome WHUMPF — measure the file, dur+rel ≥ 90% of true length, this is the scene's biggest sound** · 1.42 lens-snap WHOOSH · 1.45 bell shimmer on the starburst glint · **1.48 / 1.55 / 1.62 three THUMPs pitched UP in sequence as each shadow stands — the pitch ladder IS the warp-pipe joke and this is the loudest cue** · 1.68 grapple ZIP + cape TWANG at 1.74 · 1.92 a tiny Flossula hiss under a warm crowd murmur swelling to scene end. VO duck ROOT-indexed; verify the stem by solo-rendering `--codec=wav`, never by mix-minus.

**WHY THIS:** the promise line is the one place where nothing has been proven yet, so it has to be sold by a physical verb: one object changing state five times without a cut — slab → body → mouth → roof → light → queue, each stage's end triggering the next. That IS the promise rendered as mechanism-free result, it makes rule 3 unbreakable because the object is never finished, and it solves the queue without a walk-on: the customers are a transformation of the light the cart just made. The shadow stand-up is the load-bearing invention — the sun is already hard and low from camera-right, so four raking shadows cost one gradient quad each, are countable at mute, never occlude anything, and leaving the fourth flat and twitching hands S4 a hook for free. It also starts the reel's two longest gags in one beat: the starburst overhead (the machine works) and his hands going into his pockets (he doesn't).

**REJECTED:** the umbrella opening into the lens and blooming to white as the scene's ONLY beat — the prettiest single frame available, but it is one idea held for 2.05s with nothing becoming anything else, so it goes static at ~1.2s while the VO keeps talking, and a bloom-to-white burns the lights-snap-on energy S8 owns and would read as a scene transition, orphaning the queue. Also rejected: a giant VENDING MACHINE (no operator, so the does-less gag has nowhere to start; a lit grid of labelled compartments drifts to card-with-words; and it collides head-on with S4's glass-fronted claw machine on the very next scene); showing the four owners the workflow on a screen (UI/text, shows mechanism, and puts him to WORK at the exact moment the spine says he must start doing less); a food truck DRIVING in (arrival, not transformation; silhouette collides with S12's cargo trike). Peppi is deliberately kept out of the queue — no room without merging, and he is better spent on his S9 refusal and S15 acceptance; the fourth flat shadow does his job at zero build cost.

### S4 · STEP1 — "ONE GRAB"
**LINE + DUR:** 13.17–17.20 (4.03s, 121 frames, lf 0-120). *"One, pick one industry and stay there. Dentists, gyms, real estate, restaurants."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** You do not serve everybody. You reach in ONCE, take one industry out, and every other door locks behind you — and the guy who tried to hold all four is the one stuck inside the machine.

**HERO OBJECT:** the grimy street-corner **GRABCO CLAW MACHINE** — a 640×690 coin-op cabinet (x186..826, y58..748) whose vitrine is stuffed with four plush species (cream molars / purple dumbbells / cream-and-red little houses / gold-and-clay pizza slices). **THE TRANSFORMATION IS THE OBJECT:** a churning rainbow SOUP of four species → ONE PICK → three chute shutters SLAM → the bed DRAINS of three colours and REFILLS bottom-up as a solid ordered wall of identical molars → the single molar that came out PUFFS to car-sized on the pavement.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, one shop-width RIGHT of the cart — the corner pavement outside SMILE DEPOT. Kerb y726, tarmac to y792. Four chute mouths across the base: c1 x216-346, c2 x368-498, c3 x520-650, c4 x672-802, each with a brass roll-shutter in its lintel. Four marquee lamps at x244/372/500/628, top 118 — **one per industry, left-to-right in the VO's naming order** (molar / dumbbell / house / slice). Cabinet body slate-and-chrome; glass `#A6B8CC`→`#4C5F7C` with a `#F7F3EA` sheen. **Vitrine is split into two authored zones by a 6px chrome mullion at x=420** — see the occlusion fix. World authored from y=-260 and x=-140..1150.

**SHOT LIST**
- **f0-40 (A) CRANE DOWN INTO THE GLASS.** A claw-carriage SHADOW BAR (1012×230, `rgba(6,10,22,0.62)`, 6px blur) sweeps y-240→830 across f0-8 — the chassis CutWipe rotated vertical, so the join reads as the camera craning down THROUGH something. camY -196→0 (out-cubic) + camZ 1.02→1.07, origin 50%/26%: we start on marquee, rooftop and clock tower and settle looking DOWN into the vitrine. f4 the internal fluoro stutters on twice, throwing a cold cyan-green trapezoid onto the plush bed. The bed CHURNS: 26 plushes drifting ±14px and rotating ±16° on seed-phased orbits, the whole 580×140 mass rocking. Deliberately noisy and unreadable: TOO MANY INDUSTRIES. All FOUR marquee lamps lit. f12 Pidgey lands on the crown and pecks twice. f4-18 Sparky sprints the far pavement in the LEFT gutter only (x-140→190, groundY 604) and vanishes behind the cabinet. **f26 the cabinet SHUDDERS 3px with a muffled thump from inside — unexplained.** That is the jam being planted.
- **f18-40 (A2) ONE COIN.** FLOSSULA (x104, groundY 730, size 196, flipped) shoves a fistful of cash up at the slot with one paw while shielding his eyes with the other. YOU-CLAUDE (x882, groundY 726, size 186, shrink-wrapped tablet under his off arm) does not take the fistful — he raises ONE paw and drops ONE coin. CHUNK. The cabinet judders and the whole heap resettles in a staggered 4-9px sag (165,000px² of hero surface moving). The chrome trolley traverses x680→300 at ~15px/f, claw swinging 8° on lag. A 120×420 specular parallelogram sweeps the glass.
- **f41-62 (B) MACRO / THE PICK.** HARD CUT to claw macro — no wipe, the light change justifies it, and it lands on the word "one". camZ 1.36→1.28, origin 46%/40% (pivot 466,317). **All shot-B content authored inside x[150,850] y[95,650]; audit overflow at f41.** Both near sprites deliberately cropped out; only Claude's paw enters the right edge, resting on the joystick without him looking at it. Bed re-rendered at 1.9× so only ~9 plushes are in frame, one of each species readable. f45-53 pincers open 26°, a cyan targeting glint slides the chrome, the claw drops 60px and the surrounding plushes squash aside. f53-58 pincers SNAP on ONE cream molar — CLACK — with a 3-frame hit-stop on the entire bed, then every non-picked plush SAGS a notch.
- **f62-76 (B2) THE LIFT.** As the molar clears the mass a warm clay-gold key rakes it from camera-right while everything beneath drops to cold cyan — the chosen-one read done purely in colour temperature, zero text. The heap sags into the hole and a dumbbell, a house and a slice visibly tumble AWAY off the claw: he HAD them and let them go. f66-72 a dumbbell is stuck to the molar by one thread, dangles, drops off. **f74 a pinstriped sleeve and a polished shoe flash in the glass reflection upper-left — 3 frames before the reveal.**
- **f77-89 (C) SNAP PULL-BACK / VITO IS INSIDE IT.** camZ 1.28→0.975 over 12 frames (out-cubic, 2-frame overshoot) settling to 1.00 by f99, origin 50%/60%. Every pixel travels ~22px/frame. The street rushes in — and **VITO** is folded into the vitrine's **LEFT BAY, x236..400 / y210..380**, behind the chrome mullion at x420: pinstripes crushed, cheek flat against the pane, arms wrapped around a dumbbell + a house + a slice, one polished shoe out of the chute-2 mouth, his invoice pressed flat to the glass as a **shield** (wrong-tool escalation #2). Ribbon ledger: **180px**, puddling out of the coin return. He is lane-locked to x[186,410] at a z BELOW the hero glass. Sparky is now on top of the marquee next to Pidgey, eating a plush slice he did not previously have. Simultaneously at f77, on *"Dentists"*: chute 1 flares warm, the molar thunks down and lands at x281, ground 726. **Chute 1 stays OPEN.**
- **f88-113 (C2) THREE CLUNKS, THREE THEFTS.** On *"gyms"* (f88), *"real estate"* (f100), *"restaurants"* (f112): shutters 2, 3 and 4 SLAM in sequence, dropping 84px in 5 frames with a 2px bounce, pitch descending — ~85,500px² at ~17px/f. **Each slam has a visible physical CAUSE two frames earlier:** the matching plush is YANKED bodily out of Vito's arms (dumbbell f86, house f98, slice f110), whipped down through a drain grille, and that ENTIRE SPECIES drains from the bed in a 6-frame cascade. Vito plays three stages of loss and ends hugging air. In lockstep, marquee lamps 2, 3 and 4 DIE — a 2-frame filament flicker each, bloom collapsing to a dull slate lens. Only the MOLAR lamp stays lit; only chute 1 stays open. f104 PIDGEY plucks Vito's sunglasses off his face through the gap and leaves; Vito cannot free a hand.
- **f96-120 (C3) THE WALL, THE RELIGHT, THE GROWTH.** As each species drains, the bed REFILLS bottom-up with identical cream molars in three steps — **constrained to x430..796 / y300..510** (the right two-thirds only, capped 80px below Vito's chin) — landing on a solid ordered grid of ~24 identical molars. That 366×210 mass climbing ~12px/f is the scene's guaranteed large mover. f96-120 the camera continues Maple St's rightward walk (camX 0→-76), which is what OPENS the right third. f114 the marquee snaps to a single warm clay-orange with bulbs chasing the rim, internal fluoro warming cyan→gold, warm Pool flooding the pavement. **f112-120 the won molar PUFFS scale 1→4.6 (out-back, two settle bounces) into a car-sized 210×236 plush centred x700 (spans x595-805), ground 726 — and YOU-CLAUDE is re-grounded at x965, size 186, deliberately edge-cropped, 190px of clearance restored.** f114-120 **FLOSSULA (recoil use 2 of 5, ESCALATED, not repeated):** the relight hits him, he throws the cape up — and the car-sized molar lands beside him, four times his height. He looks up at it, looks at camera, and slowly **sits down on the kerb.** Final frame: one lit lamp, one open chute, a wall of identical molars, one enormous molar on the pavement, Claude's paws back in his apron pockets with the tablet still shrink-wrapped, and one man in a pinstripe suit inside the glass holding nothing at all.

**CAMERA:** three moves, none reused elsewhere, plus a closing rightward walk. (A) the reel's ONLY vertical crane-down: camY -196→0 (out-cubic) with camZ 1.02→1.07, origin 50%/26%. (B) macro push on the claw: camZ 1.36→1.28, origin 46%/40%; **bleed solved backwards — at 1.36, x=850 maps to 988 and x=150 to 36, so ALL shot-B content lives inside x[150,850] y[95,650], audited at f41 (peak), never on a static frame.** (C) snap pull-back camZ 1.28→0.975→1.00 (out-cubic, 2-frame overshoot), origin 50%/60%, then the CAMERA WALK camX 0→-76. Drift CAPPED at camZ 1.00 (1.03 would throw the giant molar past the IG safe line).

**LIGHT:** two light worlds separated by one pane of glass, and the payoff is them swapping. INSIDE: a grimy cyan-green fluoro tube throwing a hard volumetric trapezoid `rgba(120,190,180,0.22)` onto the plush bed, hot speculars smeared along the glass. OUTSIDE: low raking golden sun from camera-RIGHT laying a long skewed cabinet shadow LEFT across the pavement, plus a god-ray wedge through the alley gap with dust motes and grate steam. **The lighting is the script, three times:** (1) f62, the sun rakes the LIFTED molar alone in warm clay-gold while the entire bed drops to cold cyan; (2) f88/f100/f112, three of the four marquee lamps DIE in sequence, leaving one lens burning — a second, independent, mute-legible read of the same idea; (3) f114, the marquee snaps to a single warm clay-orange, the fluoro warms cyan→gold, and a warm Pool floods the pavement. The machine changes light colour because it changed what it does.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P1 FG blur 3px 1.3× (hydrant, chained scooter wheel, SMILE DEPOT's awning corner clipping top-right) · P2 HERO (the cabinet, marquee with 18 individually-socketed bulbs, vitrine with a diagonal sheen band and street reflections, gantry rail, four chutes, control deck, rust drips, a taped hand-written sign torn in half) · P3 street actors (all grounded with `<Actor>`, contact shadows WIDER than each sprite) · P4 shopfronts blur 2.5px 0.55× (SMILE DEPOT filling the left gutter, PLANET SWOLE right, a fire-escape balcony carrying CANVA) · P5 far blur 4px 0.3× (Bloatcorp, clock tower, water tower, hazy skyline — **colour-mixed toward the sky, never alpha-faded**) · P6 atmosphere (god-ray wedge, grate steam, the cold fluoro trapezoid, tarmac heat sheen).

**ESCALATION:** S3 proved DEMAND but nothing has been decided. S4 steps up on five axes at once. SCALE: S3's cart was person-sized furniture; this is a 640×690 cabinet, the first object that dwarfs the people and needs a crane to be seen. MACHINERY: the reel's first thing with a mechanism that acts on its own, starting the monotonic capability ramp. IRREVERSIBILITY: the first choice that gets physically LOCKED — three steel shutters slam and stay down. VILLAIN CONTACT: the first time Vito is caught inside the machinery and then stripped of all four prizes one at a time; the losing streak that runs to S15 starts here. COLOUR: the reel's temperature flips on screen for the first time.

**GAG:** **THE MACHINE WAS JAMMED BECAUSE VITO IS INSIDE IT.** The pull-back finds him folded into the left bay hugging one of each of the three industries he refuses to give up, invoice pressed flat as a riot shield — and the gag pays itself off THREE MORE TIMES as each shutter slam is caused by the matching plush being yanked bodily out of his arms, so by the last clunk the greediest man on Maple St is hugging nothing at all, alone in a glass box. Stacked on top: the prize that comes out is CAR-SIZED, and Flossula, having recoiled from the relight, ends up sitting down on the kerb under a plush molar four times his height. Quietest layer: **YOU-CLAUDE never plays.** He drops one coin, puts both paws in his apron pockets, and never touches the joystick.

**LIVING WORLD:** Sparky sprints the LEFT gutter only and never re-emerges on that side — then turns up on the marquee eating a plush slice. Pidgey pecks the crown, then steals Vito's sunglasses. **CAPTAIN CANVA** is one storey up on the fire escape in the far-left lane (x96, groundY 452, size 108), kneeling over one small sheet on a tiny easel, running a spirit level across a hand-drawn grid — still on shop ONE, moves the level 4px in the entire scene, never once looks up at the machine. SMILE DEPOT's neon stutters twice on a bad transformer. String bulbs sway. A delivery trike crosses at y646. Steam wisps through the god-ray. One Bloatcorp silhouette face-down for all four seconds. A forgotten plush house sits abandoned in the coin-return cup, and a strip of peeling gaffer tape sags across chute 3 — the chute that is about to be shuttered anyway.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **GRABCO** — a chipped enamel knockoff plate on the marquee, 148×30 at (232,116), **rendered at FULL opacity in chipped cream enamel with wear painted as darker flecks and a scuffed lower edge** (never alpha — no content below 0.85 anywhere in this reel). That is the scene's only legible text. **ZERO numerals.**

**MUTE CHECK:** a claw machine crammed with four obviously different kinds of stuffed toy; a claw takes exactly ONE; three of the four exit doors slam shut and stay shut; everything that isn't the chosen thing drains away and the cabinet refills as a wall of the same one thing; that one thing lands on the pavement and grows car-sized; and behind the glass a man in a pinstripe suit is stuck because he tried to hold all four. ✓ The four industries are carried ENTIRELY by silhouette (molar / dumbbell / house-with-sold-flag / pizza wedge) laid out left-to-right in the VO's exact naming order, so the caption is CONFIRMED by the picture without the picture repeating a word of it.

**TRANSITION IN:** off S3's dome bloom, a hard VERTICAL SHADOW WIPE — the claw carriage's 1012×230 shadow sweeping y-240→830 across f0-8, so the join reads as the camera craning down THROUGH something rather than as an edit. The fluoro stuttering on at f4 lands inside the wipe. Geography stays continuous: SMILE DEPOT's awning is visible at frame-left in both S3's tail and S4's shot C, one shop further right. **The scene's first HARD cut is deliberately saved for f41 so it lands on the word "one".**

**SFX:** emit as **L[3] + local** (L[3]=13.17); delete any inherited root cue map. 0.00 crane servo whirr + low metal hum bed · 0.13 shadow-wipe whoosh + fluoro stutter-ping ×2 · **0.87 muffled jam-THUMP from inside the cabinet, unexplained** · 1.00 brass coin CHUNK + judder · 1.13 gantry traverse panning L · 1.37 hard-cut transient · 1.53 pincer servo + pneumatic hiss · 1.87 pincer CLACK (hardest hit of the first half) · 2.07 cable wind-up whine · 2.57 pull-back whoosh + a soft comedic boing on Vito's reveal · 2.60 chute thunk · **2.93 / 3.33 / 3.73 three brass shutter CLUNKs descending in pitch, each preceded 2 frames by a plush-yank WHIP — measure the file, dur+rel ≥ 90% of true length or the metal tail chops** · 2.95-3.93 low granular drain rumble · 3.47 pigeon flutter + sunglasses TING · 3.80 marquee relight thunk + bulb hum swell · 3.87 plush inflate PFOOMP with two settle bounces · 3.97 a small hiss, a cape flap and a defeated kerb-sit thump. Onset density is moderate and back-loaded — re-balance per-cue volumes against THIS scene, never transfer them.

**WHY THIS:** a claw machine is the only everyday object whose entire cultural meaning is already "you get ONE grab, so choose" — the imperative is pre-loaded into the toy, so the scene never has to argue, it just has to run the machine. It discharges five constraints by geometry rather than hope. (1) The four named industries become four plush SILHOUETTES in the VO's exact order, so the graphics confirm the caption without repeating one word. (2) "And stay there" has no natural image — three lamps dying and three steel shutters slamming makes permanence physical, audible and irreversible in 0.4s, and the bed refilling as a wall of identical molars states the reuse thesis S5 inherits. (3) The VO names the industries LAST, which fights any design that converges early; putting the names on four CHUTES makes the naming beat and the commitment beat the SAME beat. (4) It is transparent, so the villain hides in plain sight inside a prop the audience has been staring at, and the jam planted at f26 gets its cause at f77 then pays off three more times. (5) It is real grimy street furniture, so it lives inside Maple St without inventing a new world.

**REJECTED:** a four-symbol SLOT MACHINE or prize wheel landing on one industry. Fatally, it means LUCK — the exact opposite of "pick one industry and stay there"; a claw is a chosen, single, expensive, irreversible reach, whereas a wheel is something that happens TO you, and this is the reel's first teaching scene so it would install the wrong idea. It also fails the transformation spine (a wheel's climax is a STOP, not a morph — no interior for the rejected industries to drain to, no volume for the chosen one to accumulate into a wall) and a flat spinning disc is opaque, so there is nowhere to hide Vito. Also rejected: a four-drawer FILING CABINET (a TEXT object demanding four written industry names in a scene budgeted for one word) and a diner pie-case (no grabbing mechanic, and a glass display case sits visually too close to S3's cart one scene earlier).
