### S5 · REUSE — "THE STENCIL COOLS, THE WALLS DON'T"
**LINE + DUR:** 17.20–22.12 (4.92s, 148 frames, lf 0-147). *"One industry means one style and one workflow you reuse forever, and every client after the first one gets easier."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** You cut ONE brass plate, once — and then that same unchanged plate turns every wall on Maple St into an identical finished post, costing less effort every time, until it costs none at all.

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE BRASS STENCIL PLATE** — a milled hand-held spray stencil whose negative space IS the poster layout (rounded outer frame, corner starburst wedge, three thick rounded bars, a centre glyph well). 460×540 world px, 1372×1030 at macro overfill, ~168×120 in his paw. **Brass is legal here** (ledger): 5-stop `#FFF6D8`→`#F6DFA6`→`#E7B24C`→`#B8813A`→`#6E4A1E`, brushed grain, milled edge with 8 rivet dimples, bevelled aperture lip, travelling specular on every camera move. **It is BORN in Shot A and never changes again — everything else changes around it.** The only hand tool in the reel, and the only object the camera physically travels THROUGH.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, the third leg of the block. Build as ONE WORLD LAYER 1360×1060 at left:-174 top:-134, translated by `worldX`, so the Shot-C pull-back to 0.80 never shows a seam. World anchors (facades 460 wide, 36px gaps, ground y706, mural band y300-560): **SMILE DEPOT** 300-760, mural centre 530 (`#C96A2E` on cream) · **PLANET SWOLE** 796-1256, centre 1026 (`#5B3F86` + gold) · **RE/MIX REALTY** 1292-1752, centre 1522 (`#C44A3A`/`#F7F3EA`/`#3A5C84`) · **PIZZA PEPPI** 1788-2248, centre 2018 (chrome + muted-green neon). Alley gaps at 760-796, 1256-1292, 1752-1788. Shot A happens on the cart's fold-down anvil counter. Across the road at ground y556: Canva's stepladder lane and Bloatcorp. Low sun at panel x~820. ⛔ **Facades use the locked palette — SMILE DEPOT is `#C96A2E`, never clay `#D2724E`** (clay belongs to the hero chain).

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-10 (A) POUR.** Macro, near-black. A clay crucible tips in and pours molten brass into a graphite mould filling x[210,830] y[300,610]. Splash beads bounce and cool mid-air. The pour is the ONLY light source: warm Pool (506,540) r260 op 0.58 uplighting Claude's face from beneath at (760,240), tongue out, flickering the brick behind. camZ 1.03→1.09, origin `46.4% 54.3%`.
- **lf10-20 (A) IT SETS.** The surface SKINS OVER: a hard colour boundary travels left-to-right at ~62px/f, white-hot → amber → solid brass with a cool `rgba(120,160,220,0.12)` rim. A travelling boundary, never a crossfade. Heat shimmer above y300; sparks up through a god-ray. **TRANSFORMATION 1: liquid becomes tool.** Glow ledger **G = 1.00**.
- **lf20-34 (A) THE CUT + PIDGEY.** A cutter head traces the poster layout — a bright `#FFF3C8` cut-line running frame, bars and starburst wedge. Sparks fan in 16 parabolas; three brass swarf ribbons peel and curl off, cooling white→amber→brown as they fall. camZ 1.09→1.62. lf24-32 Pidgey lands on the top bevel and squares up to his own warped reflection in the polished brass (flipped copy, `skewY(4deg)`); he pecks twice, the reflection pecks back, he puffs up and shuffles off. lf28 a swarf curl flicks up and clatters off the lens (5-frame shard 0.4→2.6 + a 2-frame flash).
- **lf34-38 (A) THE SLUG DROPS.** The poster-shaped centre slug DROPS OUT with a clang. Implement as a full-panel brass rect with an SVG `<mask>` carrying the layout path in black, so the street genuinely shows through. **HOLD 4 frames.** Cleanest single "becomes" beat in the reel.
- **lf38-42 (A) THROUGH THE APERTURE.** camZ accelerates 1.62→2.65 about the aperture centre (506,392) and the camera passes physically THROUGH the hole. The golden street layer behind the mask ramps 0→1 and scales with the push; the plate's lift smears into a horizontal whip-blur. **Bleed: at 2.65 about (506,392), authored content must sit inside x[321,691] y[244,543]** — only the deliberately overfilling plate lives outside. Audit at lf41.
- **lf42-68 (B1) SHOP ONE — TWO PAWS, SLOW (26f).** Emerge at camZ 1.04. `worldX` lf42:130 → lf50:110 → lf60:104 → lf68:60, so SMILE DEPOT's mural centre lands at panel 640 for contact at lf50 and the camera all but STOPS for an 18-frame pause. Claude pinned at panel x=330, groundY 706, size 236, the shrink-wrapped tablet with its coffee ring on the scooter rack behind him. **He DISMOUNTS.** Ritual, full length: contact THUMP + dust puff at (640,430) → TWO paws bracing the plate flat, feet planted, tongue out → three deliberate spray passes (lf52/57/62), a 120×90 mist cloud drifting into the god-ray → peel-flash → mural 1 revealed by an 8-frame clip-path wipe in dental orange, over-spray fuzz, two drips, the molar glyph popping LAST with a 1.25→1.00 punch. He even taps the corners. **FLOSSULA (recoil use 3 of 5)** throws his cape up and HISSES at the bright new mark, lowers it, checks his fangs, then grins at his own wall. **Glow ledger G 1.00 → 0.70** — a hot amber flash visibly travels plate→wall at contact.
- **lf68-88 (B2) SHOP TWO — ONE PAW, ROLLING (20f).** Camera accelerates: `worldX` lf68:60 → lf76:-260 → lf80:-386 (contact) → lf88:-400. Pause is now 8 frames. **He does NOT dismount** — one paw stays on the bars, ONE paw slaps the plate on and swipes the can across in a single move, **and he is looking at CAMERA, not at the wall.** ONE spray pass, mist half the length, mural 2 resolves in 5 frames (purple + gold, dumbbell glyph). **BULK** flexes at the wet logo, spins to high-five Claude — who has already rolled past — and his hand meets AIR; on the follow-through he RIPS his own vest and both halves flap for the rest of the scene. A 1.5° camera roll punches into the cut. **G 0.70 → 0.46.**
- **lf88-103 (B3) SHOP THREE — A WRIST FLICK, COFFEE IN THE OTHER PAW (15f).** `worldX` lf88:-400 → lf92:-700 → lf96:-882 (contact) → lf103:-900. Pause is 4 frames. He does not look at the wall: a wrist flick at lf93, the plate kisses (640,428) for 3 frames, one pass, mural 3 is just THERE with a 2-frame pop-in. **His free paw now holds the COFFEE CUP that was standing on the shrink-wrapped tablet** — the coaster gag paying off mid-scene, mute-readable. **BATBRO** grapples in, lands badly in a skid at lf97 and passes through the drifting mist, so the starburst prints ON HIS CAPE; at lf101 he twists, sees it, and is delighted. Horizontal motion-streak overlay scaled to speed; FG plane whipping at 1.45× worldX; the world-1752 god-ray flares. camZ eases 1.04→1.10. **G 0.46 → 0.24.** Across the road, Canva is STILL on shop one, up a stepladder, spirit level against a hand-ruled pencil grid, adjusting by a millimetre.
- **⭐ lf96-103 THE LOOP SIGHTING (fix).** As the camera whips toward Peppi, the far end of the block is momentarily visible down the street in the P4 shopfront plane: **the one dark, unlit frontage on a fully golden street, 60px wide, no plaque detail, no glint, no camera hesitation, gone in 7 frames.** Zero pre-spend; keeps the door alive at 20.5s so S13's arrow lands on a live question.
- **lf103-124 (C) THE FLING AND THE DOG.** lf103-108 windup; lf108 RELEASE with a hard whip-blur streak, then both paws go straight back on the bars. `worldX` →-1080 → -1318; camZ 1.10→1.14 (PEAK). The plate spins rightward (0→1080°) from (470,565) through apex (760,300) to wall contact (700,430), scaling 1.00→1.18, glinting at lf112/117/122. **The whole arc sits in an upper corridor y[300,470] that contains no sprite.** lf112 SPARKY launches from the kerb at x=600, clamps the plate at (712,340), is carried then DRAGGED bodily right, hind paws skidding a dust ribbon in a dedicated foreground lane at y~745; he loses grip at lf120, tumbles once, and lands **bolt upright and perfectly dignified in a grey dust cloud, shaking out clean** (⛔ NOT permanently orange — no downstream scene can carry a repainted dog).
- **lf124-131 (C) IT PRINTS ITSELF.** lf124 CONTACT: the plate SLAPS Peppi's chrome panel (mural centre → panel 700) **with nobody touching it.** 2-frame white contact-flash Pool at (700,430), mist burst, mural 4 blooms in chrome + muted green with the slice glyph — identical layout, fourth palette, no touch-up. **G = 0.06: the plate is stone cold and the wall lights up anyway.** TRANSFORMATION 3 — the heat he spent once has finished spending itself. Peppi applauds with a pizza and a slice slides off it. ⚠️ **PARENTING RULE:** during every contact window the plate must be parented to the SHOPFRONT plane (0.86×) so it sticks to the brick, and re-parented to the hero plane (1.00×) on lift. Authoring its x on the hero plane through a contact is this scene's #1 bug and reads as the stencil sliding off the wall.
- **lf131-142 (C) BOOMERANG + SNAP PULL-BACK ONTO THREE IDENTICAL MURALS.** The plate peels off and boomerangs LEFT, diving low to SKIM the tarmac at y~650 with a dust wake — entirely inside the ground band, crossing no figure — on the arc (700,430) → (620,520) → **(495,560)** (moved from 455: Claude's right edge sits at ~448, so 455 left only 7px of clearance; 495 restores 47px). Simultaneously camZ SNAPS 1.14→0.80 with `worldX` easing -1318→-1016. Three finished murals resolve at panel centres 109 / 506 / 903 — the identical layout in three different palettes in one frame, outer two slightly clipped. **That single frame is the proof of "one style" with zero text.**
- **lf142-147 (C tail) THE NO-LOOK CATCH AND THE CANVA BUTTON.** lf144 the plate pops up into a paw he raised WITHOUT LOOKING (3-frame scale pop 1.30→1.00 + glint) while he sips the coffee, other paw in his pocket. He ends the scene holding the exact same object he was holding at 1.40s, with four more walls done. **THE BUTTON:** the pull-back reveals CANVA raising his spirit level in triumph over his ONE finished pencil square, bubble dead centre, beaming — standing in front of SMILE DEPOT'S WALL, which Claude painted 90 frames ago. His square is a twentieth the size of the printed mark beside it. He deflates. Pidgey lands on his shoulder and drops one feather on it. lf145-147: at the far-right edge three flat CRATES thud onto the tarmac — the carousel rig arriving, the hook into S6.

**CAMERA:** three moves, none reused, and **the ACCELERATION IS THE ARGUMENT.** (A) macro push THROUGH the object — 1.03→1.09→1.62→2.65, origin shifting to the aperture centre (506,392), plus 3px handheld drift; the only through-object transit in the reel. (B) accelerating lateral track implemented as a `worldX` translate on the single world layer at near-constant camZ 1.04-1.10, so there is no scale-bleed risk at all; peak traverse ~46px/f between contacts 2 and 3. **THE THREE CONTACT PAUSES SHRINK 18f → 8f → 4f → 0f, and the shot lengths shrink 26f / 20f / 15f, so the EDIT gets easier at exactly the same rate he does.** (C) snap pull-back tracking the returning plate, camZ 1.14→0.80 while `worldX` eases -1318→-1016. Bleed at peak 1.14 origin (506,459): key graphics x[76,936] y[70,737] — which is why Peppi is authored at x=900 not 940. Audit at lf41 and lf124. **HERO STAGING RULE:** Claude's sprite centre stays ≥210 panel px LEFT of the active mural centre at all times (330 vs 640 = 310), so his head is always in front of plain brick.

**LIGHT:** two regimes and one continuous energy transfer. SHOT A: the pour is the ONLY source — pure bottom-uplight, warm radial Pool r260 op 0.58, brick flicker keyed to the pour, near-black surround, cool `rgba(120,160,220,0.12)` rim from top-left, plus a cutter-head practical travelling with the cut so the brass is lit BY ITS OWN CUT. SHOTS B/C: full golden hour, hard low raking key from camera-RIGHT rim-lighting every sprite's right edge against a cool `#3A5C84`-at-22% left face; long shop shadows raking left as skewed parallelograms animated with `worldX`, LENGTHENING as we track. **VOLUMETRIC:** three god-ray shafts fall through the alley gaps and, because we are tracking, sweep the frame on a rhythm that lands on each application; **when the atomised spray mist enters a shaft, the shaft's brightness DOUBLES for 6 frames** — mist reveals light. **THE GLOW LEDGER (the scene's spine):** the cut-line emits amber at G=1.00 when born and each application spends it down — 1.00 → 0.70 → 0.46 → 0.24 → 0.06 — with a hot amber flash visibly travelling plate-to-wall at every transfer, **while EVERY mural lands at full saturation regardless.** Canva's lane is deliberately FLAT and cool (Bloatcorp fluoro, no key) so the two men are lit like different films. ⛔ **NO street, string or neon light ignites in S5** — the block-wide snap-on is S8's entire payoff. Warmth evidence at lf103 is carried by shop INTERIOR windows brightening and the god-rays widening; **the strung cable stays visibly dark against the sky in every frame.**

**DEPTH PLANES:** P1 FG 1.45× blur 5px near-black (kerb furniture sweeping past — hydrant, lamppost base, awning corner, and the parked scooter's rack with the SHRINK-WRAPPED TABLET catching one glint; also Sparky's tarmac dust ribbon at y~745) · P2 HERO 1.00× sharp (the plate, the volumetric spray cone, Claude, Sparky, the cans; the plate temporarily re-parents to P4 during every contact) · P3 street actors 0.94× groundY 700-712 (Flossula, Bulk, Batbro, Peppi, kerb clutter, plus a 540px delivery truck crossing lf60-92 dragging a moving shadow bar across the facades) · P4 shopfronts 0.86× (the four facades, awnings, windows with business visible inside, **the 60px dark shutter frontage at lf96-103**, and the four murals accumulating) · P5 across-road 0.06× groundY 556 (Canva's pinned stepladder lane and Bloatcorp) · P6 far 0.02× (clock tower, water tower, wires, the low sun disc, a hazy warm band — brick, plaster and window grids all the way back, never a flat fill).

**ESCALATION:** S4 was a vertical crane-down onto a static cabinet and ONE claw drop. S5 multiplies that single choice laterally across the whole block: the camera flips from vertical to the reel's FIRST horizontal accelerating traverse, the machine shrinks to a hand tool he personally owns, and the output goes from one to four. Internally it escalates on EFFORT rather than spectacle, on two axes at once: **the street gets faster (pauses 18f→8f→4f→0f, shots 26f→20f→15f, traverse peaking ~46px/f) while the operator does less (two paws and three passes → one paw and one pass while looking at camera → a wrist flick with a coffee in the other paw → thrown away entirely).** The output never varies. It ends strictly higher than it started because the tool comes BACK to him unasked — and hands S6 the exact state it needs: the hand tool has just proved it can work without him touching it, so the only step up left is a machine that runs without him at all.

**GAG:** the escalating laziness IS the gag and it lands four times before the punchline. Tongue out, both paws, three passes, tapping the corners → one paw, one pass, looking at camera (and BULK's high-five meets AIR, ripping his own vest on the follow-through) → a wrist flick while his free paw holds the coffee that was standing on the shrink-wrapped tablet (and BATBRO gets the starburst printed ON HIS CAPE and is delighted) → **PUNCHLINE:** he FLINGS the plate without looking; SPARKY intercepts it mid-air and is DRAGGED down the tarmac by the jaw before landing bolt upright and perfectly dignified; the plate prints itself on Peppi's wall with nobody touching it and BOOMERANGS back into a paw he raised without looking, while he sips the coffee. **THE BUTTON:** Canva raises his spirit level in triumph over ONE finished pencil square — in front of a wall Claude painted 90 frames ago.

**LIVING WORLD:** Flossula hissing at his own neon, which stutters at lf47 and lf63. Bulk's silhouette curling a dumbbell in the window at one rep per 12 frames before he comes out. Batbro's SOLD sign leaning unattended under a fresh cape-shaped tear in the awning. A 540px delivery truck crossing lf60-92, dragging its shadow bar across the facades and bouncing the awning scallops. The laundromat neon flickering on its own broken rhythm. Pidgey riding the overhead wire the length of Shot B, stealing the paintbrush off the cart at lf88, ducking so hard as the plate whips past that his hat falls off, and returning at lf145 to land on Canva's shoulder. Sparky sprinting the full panel width at lf76 delivering paint can #2 in his mouth. A slice sliding off Peppi's applauding pizza and nobody picking it up. One Bloatcorp designer face-down the entire scene. **Vito appears exactly once, at lf70, as a distant far-plane silhouette at x=940 fanning himself, ribbon ledger 220px — no interaction, no crossing.**

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **NONE.** Zero numerals, zero readable wordmarks. S5 owns no spine figure and leaks nothing. The four shops are identified by GLYPH + PALETTE only — molar / dumbbell / house-with-balloon / slice — which also pre-loads the three engraved nameplates that pay off in S14. The murals stay pure colour-block and shape, deliberately proportioned so they can never be misread as a line of type at any zoom. The only recurring graphic is the Claude starburst, which is a shape. GATE THE HOW is satisfied structurally: you see the RESULT on four walls and never a legible setting, dial or instruction.

**MUTE CHECK:** metal is poured, it hardens, a shape is cut out of it and the middle drops away — then that one plate is pressed on a wall and a poster appears; slapped on a second wall, a poster appears; flicked at a third, a poster appears; thrown at a fourth, and a poster appears with nobody touching it at all. The frame widens onto three walls carrying the identical poster shape in three different colours, the plate flies back into his hand while he drinks coffee, and across the road a caped man is still measuring his first drawing on a wall that was already painted. ✓

**TRANSITION IN:** hard MATCH CUT ON METAL into MACRO DARKNESS — the biggest combined luminance and scale contrast in the reel. S4 ends wide, bright and grimy-fluoro on the pull-back with the chutes CLUNKING shut on Vito; that metal slam cuts straight into near-black brick lit only by a molten pour, so the wide-to-macro discontinuity is motivated by a matching metallic impact. A 4-frame spark shower fires across the cut line, and the first frame holds the same warm amber value the closed chute ended on. The cut lands on the VO's word "One". A SECOND internal cut-to-bright happens at lf38-42 — dark-to-gold twice in five seconds, without ever leaving the street.

**SFX:** emit as **L[4] + local** (L[4]=17.20). Measure every file before setting `dur`; tails need dur+rel ≥ 90% (the spark crackle, the frisbee whoosh and the boomerang are the ones that chop). 0.00 crucible pour glug into a low hiss (dur 1.0) · 0.36 metallic TICK on the set-boundary · 0.67 cutter whine + spark crackle (dur 1.1, **do NOT cap**) · 0.93 brass ping off the lens · 1.13 slug-drop CLANG · 1.27 deep whoosh through the aperture, pitch rising with camZ. **THE RITUAL, three times, same three cues each time but each SHORTER and pitched slightly higher so the ear hears "faster" before the eye does:** contact THUMP → spray PSHT → peel SHRK at 1.67/1.73/1.90/2.07 (spray dur 0.60), 2.67/2.73 (dur 0.35), 3.10/3.13 (dur 0.15). Plus 2.10 vampire hiss-gasp · 2.73 cloth RIP + air-whoosh high-five miss · 3.23 grapple zip + thud · 2.53 claw scrabble. Shot C: 3.60 doppler frisbee whoosh · 3.73 bark + claw-skid + dust (dur 0.8) · **4.13 metal CLACK print + neon buzz — the loudest cue, placed in the VO comma gap after "reuse forever"; verify against the MEASURED gap first, and if it is under 0.20s move the clack to 4.20** · 4.37-4.73 returning boomerang whoosh panned right-to-left with the plate · 4.80 leather CATCH pop · 4.82 pencil squeak + level ding then a deflating slide-whistle under the feather · 4.85-4.92 three crate thuds planting S6. ~22 onsets in 4.92s — re-tune for THIS reel's density. VO duck ROOT-indexed, never panel-indexed.

**WHY THIS:** a stencil IS the idea with zero abstraction — a template you cut once and reuse forever, where the reuse is visibly faster each time and the output is provably identical. It is the only candidate that is simultaneously a real recognisable street object, a thing the camera can physically pass THROUGH (a move nothing else in the reel has), and throwable, so the escalation and the biggest laugh arrive in the same beat. The transformation chain gives the eye three unbroken "becomes": LIQUID→TOOL when the slug drops, BRICK→POSTER four times, and underneath both, **HEAT→COLOUR** — the glow ledger spends the cut-line from 1.00 to 0.06 across four applications while every mural still lands at full saturation. That last transfer is the second half of the VO line rendered as physics: the effort is paid once, up front, and every client after the first collects it for free. "Gets easier" is then carried by the CAMERA rather than by added props, so nobody has to say "faster". And "one style" is not asserted — it is proven by three identical layouts in three different palettes in one frame at lf142.

**REJECTED:** a RUBBER STAMP AND INK PAD — the obvious reuse object, losing on five counts: it implies small paper output when the scene needs the whole street; it reads bureaucratic and grey against golden hour; the camera cannot travel through it, so Shot A collapses into a flat close-up; it has no atomised spray to walk into a god-ray, killing the best light beat; and it cannot be thrown, which costs the punchline, the large mover and the Sparky gag in one stroke. Also dropped: a CONVEYOR BELT spitting duplicates (base-object collision — a conveyor is a machine and S6's rig is this reel's machine; it wants an interior; and worst, a belt moves the WORK past a fixed camera, which kills the lateral-camera-as-progress-bar device the whole reel is built on); a FOUR-UP WALL GRID (reads as a graphic layout, nobody performs anything, and a fanned array of finished slides is S7's base object); a literal split-frame Claude-vs-Canva race (a dual screen by another name — absolute no; the 0.06× across-road lane delivers the same A/B contrast inside ONE panel); and a PHOTOCOPIER (too literal, characterless, and it visually argues "copy-paste", which reads as spam rather than as a style).

### S6 · STEP2 — "THE CRATE BUILDS ITSELF"
**LINE + DUR:** 22.12–25.00 (2.88s, 86 frames, lf 0-85; hard cut at lf47). *"Two, build the workflow once. I have a carousel workflow linked to my bio."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** He builds it ONCE — and "building it" is one toe-tap on a flat-pack crate that erects itself into a lit fairground carousel while his hands stay in his pockets for the remaining 72 frames.

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE CAROUSEL RIG**, authored as ONE object in continuous state-change: a banded clay-orange **FLAT-PACK CRATE STACK** that unfolds into the erected gold-canopy carousel. Every finished part is visibly the crate part it came from — LID → 8 canopy gores; WALLS → octagonal deck; CORE → 3-stage telescoping **CHROME** pole (⛔ not brass — material ledger); STRAPPING → guy-ropes. Full extent x186-826 / y196-706 ≈ 62% of the panel. ⚠️ **Silhouette guard against S3:** S3's canopy is a smooth round stretched dome on a stub pole; S6's crown is a **faceted 8-gore cone** with gold seam piping, a 12-tab scalloped valance, a 20-bulb rim, and a full vertical pole at architectural scale.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, ~40% along the walk — camera in the middle of the road exactly ONE shopfront right of S5, between RE/MIX REALTY (x120-470) and a LAUNDROMAT sliver (x470-640, warm drum window, one sock stuck to the glass) with PIZZA PEPPI entering at the right edge (x640-1012). Far pavement ground y606, kerb lip y690, tarmac 690-792, hero ground y706 (deck rim seats at y660). Bloatcorp across the road far-left x0-150 (**at this camera position it is inside x[-300,1312], so it renders**). Low sun disc camera-RIGHT at (952,286) behind the alley gap. **STREET LIGHTS DISCIPLINE (load-bearing for S8): the strung café lights stay UNLIT.** This scene's glow is one isolated island of warm — the rig is the FIRST object in the reel taller than a shopfront and the FIRST that makes its own light. **Peak-camScale authoring bounds solved before coordinates:** Shot A origin (506,681) at 1.085 → author inside x[54,957] y[54,783]; Shot B origin (506,348) at 1.06 with worldY +52 → x[44,968] y[-29,717]. Audit at lf47 and lf58.

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-8 ARRIVAL, ZERO STATIC FRAMES.** The crate stack is ALREADY in motion at frame zero, carrying S5's frisbee energy: three stacked crates (deck 300×164, brass-free chrome corner caps, Claude starburst on the lid) fly in from off-panel-left and SKID x250→x452 at ~34px/f, dust plume 620×180 (≈111,000px²), corners sparking, until a THUD against the kerb at lf8 with a 6px shockwave ring and a squash 0.84→1.00 over 5f. Pidgey rides the lid through the skid, wings out. Sparky bails, all four legs splayed (groundY 868 so his legs crop off the panel bottom).
- **lf8-14 THE ENTIRE LABOUR.** YOU-CLAUDE walks in to x150 and at lf12 gives the crate corner **ONE TOE-TAP**. At lf14 his hands go into his pockets and **DO NOT COME OUT for the remaining 72 frames.** The amber strapping POPS at lf14 and spins off-frame right.
- **lf14-26 LID BECOMES CANOPY (flat).** The lid splits along 8 hinges and falls outward FLAT onto the tarmac — a gold metal flower opening r90→r300 in 12 frames (≈280,000px² arriving, rim edge 17px/f). Gores land in a running order (gore i starts lf14 + i×0.6f) so the opening sweeps rather than pops. Pidgey is carried out on gore #3, wings spread, indignant, and hops clear at lf24.
- **lf22-34 WALLS BECOME DECK.** The four crate walls hinge DOWN and lock into an octagonal deck: top ellipse cx506 cy660 rx260 ry44, front face y660-692, fluted ribs every 45°, chromed edge band, contact shadow ellipse rx 0→330 sweeping LEFT (second free large mover). scaleX 0.12→1.00 with a 1.03 settle.
- **lf26-47 CORE BECOMES POLE + ROPES.** Chrome core telescopes out of the crate centre: stage 1 tip y640→y430 (lf26-34); stage 2 tip →y300 (lf34-44), each seating with a visible collar and a hard specular flare. Guy-ropes whip out to pegs at (208,762)/(316,758)/(700,758)/(852,746), launching lf30/34/38/42; each is an SVG catenary whose sag animates 46→0 with a 2-frame overshoot to -6 (the SNAP), each landing hammering a peg in from y-90 over 3f with a 5-ray spark burst. **PIDGEY'S CAUSAL GAG (nobody sees it but the audience):** at lf36 he waddles to the LEFT-FRONT peg, cocks his head, and YANKS IT OUT in one hop; that rope hangs slack for exactly 3 frames, then snaps taut upward. **BATBRO SUBVERSION (fix — 2nd of his 4 identical landings replaced):** he grapples in at lf38 and **lands PERFECTLY**, boots square, cape settling, and dusts himself off with visible pride — and is then yanked feet-first up the rope anyway at lf60 because his cape is caught in the peg Pidgey pulled.
- **CUT lf47** — mid-transformation, on the word "carousel", 2-frame whip blur on lf45-47, so the morph carries across it and reads as a re-frame of one continuous action.
- **lf47-60 IT TOPS OUT.** Stage 3 locks, pole tip y300→y196, finial seats with a CLACK and a 228→220→228 overshoot. The 8 flat gores are dragged up by their tips — the flower CLOSES to a cone at lf54, then UNFURLS into the full canopy (r300, apex 506,216, rim ellipse rx300 ry190, alternating CLAY and PAPER gores with gold seam piping) over lf54-60. The scalloped valance drops in the same running order lf58-66, masking the pole midsection. Batbro's snagged rope goes taut and he is hauled feet-first from (826,690) to off-frame (868,60) by lf74 at 26px/f, size ramping 190→150, SOLD sign still gripped, legs pedalling.
- **lf60-74 THE BRAND BEAT + THE LIGHT BEAT + CANVA.** lf64: the 8 starburst FRAGMENTS printed one-per-gore rotate into alignment and complete a single **CLAUDE STARBURST rosette** on the crown — the transformation's punctuation mark and the only branding in the scene. lf62-74: the opening canopy CUTS the low sun and throws **8 hard god-ray shafts** fanning between the gores, sweeping the tarmac and climbing the RE/MIX facade with dust igniting inside each shaft, while the canopy's soft shadow (≈800×400, edge travel 11px/f) sweeps right-to-left across the shopfronts. **CAPTAIN CANVA** — who stepped in at lf50 to press his T-square against the rig and check it was plumb, nodded with satisfaction, and had his cape clamped by the rotating collar at lf56 without noticing — now comes round the near-left rim **UPSIDE DOWN** through his dedicated zone x200-420 / y210-320, cape over his own face, T-square still gripped at a perfectly level professional angle, one eye squinted along it. Sparky sprints the full panel width lf62→lf86 UNDER the deck. **lf70-80 eight chrome ride-poles drop from the valance to the deck, each carrying a DELIBERATELY BLANK 4:5 PORTRAIT PANEL (92×115, PAPER face, clay frame, rounded corners, the same clay corner notch S7's cards carry), hung in a row like a rack of prints** — so on mute the machine reads as "a thing that holds a row of posts", not as a fairground ride, and it visually rhymes with the deck that fires out three seconds later. **The machine is built but has produced nothing.**
- **lf74-85 THE LIGHTS RUN, THEN THE TAG.** 20 rim bulbs light in a chase, one every 1.5 frames left-to-right, warm `#FFE9B8` cores with 60px blooms, each dropping a Pool on the deck; a broad clay Pool (506,690) 760×160 ramps 0→0.85, plus a comedy up-light on Canva's already-inverted face. The canopy settles with a 14px gore-ripple. The chase closes at lf82 with a 2-frame warm-white bloom. **lf80-85 THE TAG (enlarged per fix): a 140×96 cognac-leather LUGGAGE TAG drops from the finial on a chrome chain to (600,290), carrying a debossed two-oval CHAIN-LINK charm — pure shape, zero text — and swings ±16° damped. The camera is settled and NOTHING ELSE IN FRAME MOVES for these 6 frames; a 40px specular band sweeps it for a hard GLINT at lf83.** Pidgey reclaims the finial and has to shuffle to stay put. Claude gives one lazy thumb-flick toward the tag without looking, hands otherwise still pocketed. **LAST FRAME: full rig lit and turning, tag swinging, nothing produced yet — the hole S7 drops the napkin into.**

**CAMERA:** TWO moves, cut at lf47 mid-transformation. SHOT A low-angle dolly push from road level: camZ 1.015→1.085, origin `50% 86%`, plus world translateY 0→-18 tilting up as the pole telescopes — the low angle is staged, not faked (we see the crates' front faces, the deck's front face, and the canopy's dark UNDERSIDE). SHOT B crane up + slow orbit: camZ 1.06→1.012 (the crane pulls back as it rises), origin `50% 44%`, world translateY 0→+52 so the ground sinks away. **The orbit is sold three ways rather than by faking a 3D rotation:** the canopy rim ellipse ry animates 190→232; the P3 shopfront plane slides x -34→+22 while the P4 skyline slides +8→-6 (counter-parallax); every gore's stripe phase and every ride-pole's angle advances +14° with a depth squash. Neither move duplicates a neighbour: S2/S5/S9 own whip-pans, S5/S7 own macro pushes, S1/S4 own snap pull-backs. Bleed checked at both peaks: widest element is the open canopy x206-806; the finial at y196 projects to y239; Bloatcorp and the shopfront walls are the only elements permitted to run off the edge.

**LIGHT:** two-source and hard. WARM KEY from camera-RIGHT: the sun disc rakes gold down the right edge of the pole, the right gores and the deck rim, throwing every shadow LEFT and long — the rig's cast shadow stretches to x50 and GROWS as the rig grows. COOL FILL from sky-left: slate `#3A5C84` rim on all left-facing planes, plus cold blue-grey spill from Bloatcorp's grid so the two sides of the street read as different temperatures. **VOLUMETRICS ARE CAUSED BY THE TRANSFORMATION:** the rising pole cuts the alley god-ray at lf26 and throws a sweeping shadow bar; then at lf62 the opening canopy occludes the sun and fans 8 HARD shafts through the gaps between gores with dust igniting inside each (0→0.36 → 0.18). The skid dust, peg sparks and canopy dust all sit inside those beams so the air reads real. **THE LIGHT EVENT:** at lf74 the rig becomes the reel's first self-lit object. ⛔ **DELIBERATELY WITHHELD: the street's string lights stay DARK** — withholding it here is what makes S8 land. Far plane is desaturated by MIXING toward the sky colour `#5E6C82`, never by dropping opacity.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P0 FG silhouette blur 4px, brightness 0.5, static (the parked scooter's tail with the SHRINK-WRAPPED tablet still on the rack, coffee ring visible, plus a clay hydrant; bottom-right the blurred vendor-cart awning corner catching a spark) · P0b **Sparky's dedicated lane** (full panel width, size 168 at groundY 868 so his legs crop off the bottom — a big near-camera silhouette whose sprite top sits BELOW every other figure's feet) · P1 HERO (the crate→carousel in all states plus its cast shadow; ~62% of panel; nothing else may share this band) · P2 street actors in fixed lanes (Claude far-left kerb x150 size 240; Batbro on the right guy-rope; Canva at ground x640 in Shot A then bolted to the near-left rim zone x200-420 / y210-320 in Shot B; Peppi leaning out of his neon doorway with a spatula) · P3 shopfronts (RE/MIX with tethered balloon, laundromat sliver, PEPPI, **UNLIT** string lights, Bloatcorp) · P4 far (hazy skyline, clock tower at x700 planting S11, water tower, colour-mixed toward the sky).

**ESCALATION:** S5 was a HAND TOOL — palm-sized, human-powered, and he still had to ride the street shop to shop. S6 turns the tool into ARCHITECTURE and three things step up at once. SCALE: the first object taller than a shopfront, filling 62% of the panel and forcing the camera to crane. LIGHT: every prior scene was lit only by the sun; this is the first object that makes its own — precisely the ammunition S8 detonates block-wide. LABOUR: the does-less rung drops from a thrown stencil to a single toe-tap, then to hands-in-pockets for 72 straight frames, and the point is PROVED rather than asserted by physically converting the one man still working by hand into a component of the machine. Nothing is static for a single bucket. It opens on a dead crate skidding in the dirt and closes on a lit, turning rig that has produced ABSOLUTELY NOTHING — the hook straight into S7.

**GAG:** he builds it with his shoe, and the only man still doing the work becomes part of the machine. **CANVA** steps in to be helpful, presses his T-square against the rig to check it is plumb, nods with satisfaction, and the rotating collar clamps his cape. He does not notice. Fourteen frames later he comes round the front of a lit carousel UPSIDE DOWN, cape hanging over his own face, still holding the T-square perfectly level, still squinting along it, checking plumb while inverted with total professionalism. Nested underneath, a three-character causal chain nobody narrates: **PIDGEY** yanks a peg out for no reason → the rope hangs slack for 3 frames then snaps taut → **BATBRO**, who for once landed *perfectly*, is hauled feet-first out of the top of the panel anyway, SOLD sign still in hand, legs pedalling. Two capes airborne, one man in a cap with his hands in his pockets, and the machine building itself between them.

**LIVING WORLD:** **VITO** (x86, groundY 640, size 92, deep background lane — he never enters the hero band) holds his rolled invoice up against the rising pole as a **TAPE MEASURE** (a NEW wrong tool) and it comes up embarrassingly short; ribbon ledger **260px**, wrapped once around the lamppost behind him. Behind the Bloatcorp glass the cubicle grid turns its heads toward the new light in a running wave at lf76 — except the one designer face-down asleep, who never moves. Peppi leans out with a spatula and the P of his sign flickers twice. The RE/MIX balloon is dragged sideways by the canopy's downdraft at lf60 and snaps back. The laundromat drum spins faster in sympathy, one sock plastered to the glass, and a stack of folded towels topples at lf8 from the crate impact. A paper cup rolls the full width of the gutter. A parked trike has one wheel still slowly turning from the impact. A bus crosses the far background gap lf0-24. The block is visibly one notch warmer than S2 — but the string lights stay dark.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **NONE.** Not one word, label or numeral. Mandatory: the VO and captions carry "carousel workflow" and "linked to my bio" over these exact frames, so printing either on the canopy, the valance or the tag would repeat the spoken words. The artifact is named by FORM and by the 8 gore-fragments resolving into a single CLAUDE STARBURST rosette at lf64 — an ident, not a word. The bio link is an embossed two-oval chain-link charm, pure shape. **DELIBERATELY DESIGNED AND CUT:** a "CAROUSEL" word assembling across the valance as the gores opened — it looked great and it is banned. The step number "Two" lives in the caption only.

**MUTE CHECK:** a flat-pack crate skids across a shop street and thuds against the kerb. A guy in a cap taps it once with his foot and puts his hands in his pockets. The crate unfolds itself: the lid becomes a canopy, the walls become a deck, the core shoots up into a pole, ropes stake themselves down, and a lit carousel **hung with a row of blank portrait panels** stands where the crate was — while two caped idiots get carried into the air by it and one goes round upside down still holding a ruler. ✓ Every finished part is visibly the crate part it came from; nothing appears from off-screen and nothing is concealed. The toe-tap and the pockets are the whole sentence.

**TRANSITION IN:** hard match-on-action off S5, no dissolve and no wipe device. S5 ends on the flung stencil boomeranging into his paw, spinning clockwise at speed; S6's frame 0 already has that lateral energy — the crate stack flying in from off-panel-left with the same clockwise tumble — so the spin match-cuts across the boundary and there is no zero-velocity frame. The skid dust doubles as the CutWipe light band sweeping lf0-6. The camera has advanced exactly one shopfront right. The rhyme lands at the end: S5's last frame is a small disc spinning in his hand; S6's payoff is a giant disc turning over his head.

**SFX:** emit as **L[5] + local** (L[5]=22.12); delete any inherited root cue map. 0.00 crate skid + gravel · 0.27 wooden THUD with a chrome ring, sub-thump layered · **0.40 toe-tap tick — tiny and dry, but it MUST be audible under the VO because the whole build hinges on it** · 0.47 metal strap POP · 0.53-0.87 eight rapid hinge CLACKs falling into a descending run · 0.87-1.13 deck panels locking, four soft seats · **1.00 / 1.47 / 1.93 three telescoping CLACKs ASCENDING in pitch** closing on a finial CLANK · 1.00/1.13/1.27/1.40 four rope WHIPS each resolving into a peg CLANK with a spark tick · 1.20 STAKE_TINK for Pidgey's peg · **1.80-2.00 canvas WHUMPF — measure the file, dur+rel ≥ 90%; this is the scene's biggest sound and a chopped whumpf reads as a slap** · 2.00 ROPE_ZIP for Batbro plus a distant off-panel yelp at 2.20 · 2.13 soft bell shimmer on the rosette (a bell, not a sparkle) · 2.47-2.80 bulb-chase twinkle, ten descending ticks · 2.73 bloom SHIMMER · **2.77 tag CHINK on the glint — small and clean, deliberately tiny after the swell, the last sound before S7.** If a fairground organ is used at all it is a TWO-NOTE STAB ~0.6s, never a tune, and never over "carousel workflow". Speech is dense here, so weight cues into the 0.27-0.40, 1.53-1.60 and 2.60-2.83 gaps rather than turning anything up. VO duck ROOT-indexed; verify by solo-rendering `--codec=wav`, NEVER by mix-minus.

**WHY THIS:** "build the workflow once" has one honest visual answer — the thing has to assemble itself in front of you, in one unbroken move, out of parts you can account for. Authoring it as a flat-pack crate means the eye follows every conversion, so the audience never takes the machine on faith, which is what makes the claim land instead of reading as a pitch. Three constraints solve for free: the transformation IS the large mover (a 500px metal flower opening, then a 280,000px² canopy climbing 330px); the canopy occluding the sun IS the volumetric beat; and the toe-tap IS the does-less rung between S5's thrown stencil and S7's single napkin. GATE THE HOW is structural rather than policed — exterior, crown, deck, finial and tag, never a dial, a setting or the hopper-to-chute mechanism, which is deliberately withheld for S7. The blank ride-panels are the mute-read fix: without them a muted viewer watches a merry-go-round being built and receives no information; with them the object reads as a rack that holds posts. And it ends on a genuine hook: a beautiful lit machine that has not yet made a single thing.

**REJECTED:** the carousel ALREADY STANDING and turning with the four shop logos riding the horses, a finished post flying off each time one passes the front. (1) Nothing becomes anything — a rotation is a STATE, not a transformation, and in 2.88s a steady spin is a static hold dressed as motion. (2) Four riding logos means four labelled objects at once, straight into the no-text rule, and the mute test collapses because the scene only reads if you can read the logos. (3) It pre-spends S7 — a carousel already firing out finished posts leaves the napkin-to-deck payoff nowhere to go. Runner-up rejected: a CONVEYOR / assembly line with Claude turning a crank — it collides head-on with S7's hopper-and-chute rig, it breaks the does-less ladder (the rung after a frisbee throw must be smaller than a throw), and a visible crank is a direct gate-the-how violation. Also rejected: building the "carousel" as a giant Instagram carousel UI (an unnecessary container inside the panel, a card with words on it) and painting CAROUSEL across the valance (the caption already speaks it over those frames).

### S7 · LOAD — "THE RING SURVIVES"
**LINE + DUR:** 25.00–28.56 (3.56s, **101 frames after donating 6 to S8**, lf 0-100). *"You load it once, and it turns one idea into a finished on-brand carousel in under a minute."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** The worst scrap of paper on Maple St goes in with ONE finger and comes back as a block-long ribbon of finished, obviously-same-brand cards — **with the same coffee ring still on card one** — before a wound-up timer completes a single turn. Same paper, upgraded. The machine isn't the point; the RATIO is.

**HERO OBJECT:** ONE tracked piece of **PAPER** changing state: the coffee-ringed **NAPKIN** (300×210 at macro) that becomes a fanned 16-panel **CARD DECK** ("paper dragon") sweeping 640px along the pavement. The **Ø96 COFFEE RING** is the identity token that proves the deck IS the napkin — established in macro at 0.4s, reappearing in the same top-right corner of card one at 2.45s. The only paper-stock hero in the reel. ⚠️ **RULE-2 DEFENCE (tightened per critique):** S6's rig is reduced to two body parts, and the **hopper cowl is authored at 240×175 at (600,180)** — 58% of its original size, pushed to the upper-right third — while the napkin holds 300×210 across the lower-left two-thirds. **Crop-test lf46, lf52 and lf62: if the cropped hero is a copper funnel rather than a piece of paper, shrink again.** The cowl is a doorway, never the subject. **Cowl is OXIDISED COPPER `#9C5B3A`/`#6E3A24`, not brass** (material ledger).

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, ~62% along the block — the kerb between the laundromat and PIZZA PEPPI. Kerb/ground GY=690. The rig stands at x620-930 (canopy top y286, chute mouth 170×120 at (200,430) in shot C framing). The clay CART occupies x330-520 with the still-**SHRINK-WRAPPED tablet** on its counter at (470,556), rot 8°, coffee ring on the plastic, a cup set down ON it at lf6. **A dedicated FOREGROUND GUTTER LANE y706-772 (z=30) is reserved exclusively for paper and Pidgey — no sprite pixels ever enter it.** Bloatcorp x40-330 y380-560 across the road, cubicle grid lit, one designer face-down. Far pavement groundY=572. A god-ray cuts through the gap between rig canopy and Peppi's awning and lands squarely on the gutter lane, so the deck unfurls INTO the light. ⛔ **String lights stay unlit and unmentioned** (S8's payoff, per the fix).

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-12 (A1) THE ONE IDEA.** RACK-FOCUS IN from S6: the gold canopy arc sits blurred across the top (3px→8px over lf0-6) while the napkin snaps sharp — no cut in the street's geography. Counter plane fills y430-792. HERO: the NAPKIN, 300×210 at left 356 top 452, rot -6°, PAPER `#F7F3EA`, scalloped torn edge, a grease patch going translucent so warm bokeh burns through. On it ONE clay-ink scribble (circle + wedge + arrow, stroke w9 with a wet-ink shine, raised as a ridge with its own micro-shadow) — **a scribble, ZERO letters at any zoom.** Top-right at napkin-local (196,14): the **Ø96 COFFEE RING**, `#7A3F27` at 0.50, 22px torus. **THIS IS THE TOKEN — it must read at this size.** A pen finishes the last stroke and taps twice. At (676,486) a **brass EGG TIMER Ø120** (brass is legal only on the ledger's four objects, so this dial is **chromed with a cream face**): knurled crown, red sweep hand parked at 12, a small `1 MIN` arc at the top. The napkin sails in on an arc and DROPS IN at lf48 in a puff of lint. PIDGEY lands on the cowl rim at lf52, peers in, takes a puff of steam in the face at lf56, leaves indignantly.

- **lf82-96 (C2) THE PAPER DRAGON.** THE BIG MOVER. The fan front sweeps 552px in 16 frames (~35px/f; sweeping mass ~640×150 = 96,000px²). **Faces are PURE colour-block — one big field per card in Peppi's palette (chrome silver / neon red / cream / deep green) — with the sequence repeating every 4 cards so the whole ribbon reads as ONE brand system at a glance, and the SAME clay corner notch on every card.** Any "text" exists only as soft blurred rounded rects at 22% opacity behind 3px blur, illegible at every zoom. At lf92-100 a single white SHEEN BAND sweeps left→right across all 16 cards in one pass, binding them into one object. Peppi does not chase it (that would cross Claude — forbidden); he crawls 40px on his knee, which is funnier. The deck front passes CANVA (deep-left, groundY 624) BELOW and IN FRONT of his ground line, never touching his pixels. He does not look up.
- **lf96-100 (C3) THE THEFT, THE REPLACEMENT, THE TIMER.** PIDGEY dives to the deck's LAST card, snatches it at lf97 and labours out toward the top-right. **Claude does not look up.** At lf98 the chute fires a REPLACEMENT on a HIGH arc (apex y340, safely above the deck's top edge at y442 so it never covers a landed card) and it SEATS into the vacant slot at lf100 — the theft is repaired before the thief clears frame. **FOREGROUND bottom-right: the EGG TIMER returns at Ø200, (640,596)-(840,796), cropped by the panel bottom — its red hand has swept about two-thirds of one revolution and CLACKS to a stop SHORT of the `1 MIN` mark. It never rings; it gives one dry DING and Peppi flinches on his knee.** HOOK INTO S8: at the extreme right a slack red RIBBON strung low across the pavement (x820-900, y626, catenary sag) is brushed by the last card's corner, quivers, and catches one glint. Never explained.

**CAMERA:** three moves, the last two joined continuously. (A) macro dolly push 1.05→1.16, origin 50%/55% sliding to 57% x, opening on a RACK FOCUS from S6's canopy. (B) lateral whip-pan, translateX +520→0 over 9 frames with a horizontal smear and 3° skewX, easing hard into a settle micro-push 1.12→1.06, origin 40%/45% — the camera effectively goes THROUGH the throat. (B3→C) crane down, continuous, NO cut — translateY -132→+4, camZ 1.10→1.02 with a 6-frame overshoot settle, then a slow 1.02→1.04 push onto the cropped timer. **Overflow solved backwards (origin x=506):** shot A peak 1.16 → load-bearing coords inside x[84,928] (the tablet at x760-960 is the only element allowed past that edge and its crop is deliberate); shot B peak 1.12 → x[69,943]; shot C peak 1.04 → x[35,977]. Audit at peak, never on a static frame.

**LIGHT:** hard low golden key from camera-RIGHT throughout, cool slate fill bouncing off Bloatcorp's glass. SHOT A: a 28° specular band rakes the counter laminate; the napkin's fibre texture throws 3px shadows LEFT; a cool rim on its left edge; the grease patch blows out to warm bokeh. **SHOT B: the hopper interior IS the scene's light source** — dark→full amber on entry, throwing an upward Pool onto the canopy underside and a downward wash onto the watching faces (light as the reveal, never mechanism); a 200px 14° volumetric GOD-RAY through the canopy/awning gap at 0.20 screen blend, full of paper lint. SHOT C: the chute mouth PULSES on each card burst; the god-ray lands squarely on the gutter lane so the deck unfurls INTO the light; hot cream specular rim on every right-facing card edge, cool slate rim on the shaded left faces; 16 long parallel shadows raking left. **Bloatcorp stays deliberately cool grey — the only cold thing in frame.**

**DEPTH PLANES:** P1 FG 1.35× blur 5-6px (shot A the counter lip and curling steam; shot C the cart's clay awning corner, the hydrant with a snagged length of Vito's ribbon fluttering, and the cropped Ø200 egg timer) · P2 HERO 1.00× (napkin → copper cowl → the 16-card deck in the reserved lane y706-772; always 55%+ of the panel) · P3 street actors 0.92× (Claude at the rig pole, Peppi, Pidgey, Sparky, the cart with the shrink-wrapped tablet coaster) · P4 shopfronts 0.68× (PEPPI's chrome facade and neon slice, the laundromat with its spinning drum, awnings, the rig pole and canopy scallops) · P5 far 0.40× (Bloatcorp with one designer face-down; Canva's easel step at groundY 624) · P6 sky 0.18× (warm haze, the hazy clock tower at x120, one god-ray shaft).

**ESCALATION:** S6 BUILT the rig and left us craning UP at an empty canopy — a machine that had not yet done anything. S7 makes it EAT and OUTPUT, and inverts the geometry: we end craning DOWN at a pavement buried in its product. Three axes at once. (1) **INPUT QUALITY COLLAPSES while output quality rises** — the thing fed in is the worst object in the reel so far, a grease-stained napkin from the one character everybody has refused, and what comes out is longer than the shop. (2) EFFORT DROPS AGAIN: his entire contribution is ONE FINGER leaving a pocket for four frames. (3) SCALE TRIPLES EVERY BEAT: a 300px napkin → a 240px cowl → a 640px ribbon colonising the whole street floor — and the machine reloads a stolen card without anyone noticing. It ends handing S8 a fully operational business with nothing left to do but cut the ribbon.

**GAG:** **THE ONE FINGER**, then **THE THEFT**. Peppi holds out his greasy napkin braced to be refused again; Claude never takes it — one finger comes out, TAPS it, the napkin flicks away spinning, the finger goes back in the pocket, and Peppi's hand is left frozen mid-air still shaped around a napkin that isn't there — **and when he offers the pizza with his other claw, the hatch flap snaps shut on it.** Payoff bit at the tail: Pidgey walks off with the end card and Claude does not even look up, because the chute has already fired a replacement that seats into the gap before the pigeon clears frame. Support: the shrink-wrapped tablet gets a cup set down ON it; Peppi, not trusting any of this, wound up a pizza timer and when it clacks the needle has barely moved and he flinches at it from his knee; Canva is still on post number ONE with a spirit level while sixteen finished pieces hit the pavement behind him.

**LIVING WORLD:** Sparky sprints the full panel width twice — blurred through the bokeh in A2, then along the far pavement right→left in C — always fully behind everything. The laundromat drum spins through its window. Steam curls off the cup and crosses frame. Bloatcorp's grid glows with one designer face-down the entire scene. Peppi's neon slice buzzes and flickers twice, then commits. **VITO** stands small in the deep plane at x=430, size 94, holding his ribbon up beside the unfurling deck to compare lengths and visibly losing — silhouette only, **no props escalation, and he leaves before S8 so his binoculars land fresh**; ribbon ledger **300px**, a snagged length fluttering on the foreground hydrant. Canva nods twice over his spirit level. Dust jumps off the cowl on the clunk; paper lint hangs in the god-ray. The parked scooter's amber indicator blinks in the blurred foreground.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **1 MIN** — a small arc on the egg-timer dial, established on the counter in A1 and returned to, cropped and enlarged to Ø200, in the C3 foreground where the hand clacks to a stop SHORT of the mark. That is the scene's entire text. The napkin scribble has zero letters at any zoom; all 16 card faces are pure colour-blocks with only blurred 22%-opacity rects standing in for copy; the rig carries no dials, labels or readable settings. **Number-spine containment holds: "under 1 minute" appears ONLY here**, and the kerbside parking meter the arc originally suggested is deliberately NOT used because S12's base object is the row of meters.

**MUTE CHECK:** a man holds out a greasy, coffee-stained napkin with a scribble on it → another man taps it with ONE finger without taking his hands out of his pockets → a glowing funnel swallows it and turns once → a long ribbon of clean, matching, colourful cards fans out across the sunlit pavement, **and the FIRST one still has the same coffee stain** → a timer's hand clacks to a stop short of one full turn while a pigeon steals a card and is instantly replaced. ✓ Worst input, biggest output, no time, no effort — the whole comparison is physical, and the hero silhouette is unmistakably PAPER.

**TRANSITION IN:** RACK FOCUS, not a hard cut. S6 ends craning UP and orbiting the newly lit canopy; S7 opens with that same canopy still in frame across the top, blurring out 3px→8px over lf0-6, while the napkin snaps sharp — the eye is handed from the machine to the thing about to go into it with no break in geography, and the energy inverts (S6 craned up and out, S7 pushes down and in). A 3-frame warm flash on the bulb chase covers the scale mismatch: the biggest object in the reel handing off to the smallest, which states the scene's idea before a word lands. **NOTE: S7 donates its final 6 frames to S8's recoil (see FIXES) — the deck unfurl tail is trimmed, never an S8 element added.**

**SFX:** emit as **L[7] + local** (L[7]=25.00). 25.30 pen_tap ×2 (v0.22) · 25.47 cup on plastic wrapper, dull knock (v0.20) · 25.55 metal wind-up ratchet, 6 clicks (v0.26) · 25.87 dry paper rustle (v0.28) · 26.03 finger_tick, tiny and dry (v0.24) + paper_whoosh (v0.32) · 26.05 **spring-boing on the pizza refusal (v0.22) — the same cue as S1 f96** · 26.27 WHIP swoosh (v0.46, **dur+rel ≥ 90% of true file length**) · 26.60 flutter + soft hollow thump into the hopper (v0.30) · 26.63 amber flare riser (v0.26) · 26.87 copper CLUNK (v0.42, full tail — measure first) · 27.00-27.35 single drum whirr, one revolution, rising, loop-free (v0.24) · 27.13 bulb_zap ×5 panned L→R (v0.20) · 27.55 light-rush riser down the chute (v0.30) · 27.68 card fire #1, hard snap (v0.44) · **27.73-28.28 accelerating card riffle cascade, pitch and density rising with the fan front — the payoff sound; let the tail run LONG at a lower level rather than a short loud chop** (v0.36→0.44) · 28.33 timer needle clack (v0.38) · 28.42 pigeon flap + card pluck (v0.30) · 28.50 replacement card snap (v0.40) · 28.54 ribbon glint ting, tiny, hooks S8 (v0.18). This VO is near-continuous so the duck sits mostly at 0.38 and only the last two cues get a real gap. Verify by SOLO-RENDERING the SFX stem `--codec=wav` — NEVER by subtracting stems.

**WHY THIS:** the line has two jobs — "you load it ONCE" (effort) and "one idea → a finished on-brand carousel" (transformation) — and one tracked object does both. **The COFFEE RING is the load-bearing invention:** a distinctive, mute-legible mark established in macro at 0.4s, so when it reappears on card one at 2.45s the viewer knows without a word that this is the SAME paper, upgraded. Causality proved, not asserted — the failure mode of every cut-away version. The ONE FINGER is the effort half as physical comedy, and casting Peppi as the giver turns an abstract capability claim into a status reversal you can watch on two faces. The instant-replacement tail converts a finished demo into a running SUPPLY, the exact belief S8's ribbon-cut and S13's hammock need already established. The staging buys the hard constraints for free: gate-the-how is automatic because the mechanism is sealed inside an opaque glowing cowl (there is nothing to copy-paste, only a mouth and a chute, and the light IS the reveal); rule 11 is over-served because the hero IS a 640px moving mass; and the reserved gutter lane with all sprites grounded at y≤690 makes no-occlusion a geometric guarantee rather than a per-frame check. "On-brand" is proved graphically — a repeating 4-colour rhythm plus the same corner notch on 16 cards reads as one system in a single glance, which no label could do.

**REJECTED:** (1) a GIANT PRINTING PRESS interior — the obvious physical read of "workflow", dying four ways: it is S6's rig wearing a hat (silhouette collision); its entire visual language IS exposed mechanism, so any belt path or dial becomes a copy-pasteable how-to; a machine interior leaves Maple St and breaks the street-as-progress-bar spine; and it makes the machine the hero when the hero has to be the RATIO. (2) DEMO TO THE QUEUE — four reacting sprites plus Claude plus the rig blows the spacing law inside 1012px, the reaction splits four ways so no single face carries the surprise, and it re-uses S3's queue as repeat rather than escalation. (3) Claude typing the idea in himself — kills the does-less ladder and drifts toward the HOW. (4) A screen/app UI with a progress bar — makes a device the hero, shows the HOW, and is exactly the flat sticker-card look the cinematic rule exists to kill. (5) A before/after of a bad post vs a good post — an implicit split-screen, absolutely banned. (6) The KERBSIDE PARKING METER for the "under 1 minute" beat — it pre-spends S12, whose BASE object is the row of meters; the counter-top egg timer keeps the number, belongs to Peppi's world, is established as a tracked prop in shot A rather than dropped in late, and collides with nothing.

### S8 · BUILT — "THE RIBBON BECOMES THE LIGHTS"
**LINE + DUR:** 28.56–30.27 (1.71s, **57 frames — 51 authored + 6 donated from S7's deck tail**, lf 0-56). *"That's the whole business, and it's already built."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** Nothing gets constructed here — a finished thing gets RELEASED. The one red line holding the block closed is cut, and that exact ribbon flies up into brackets that were **already on the walls** and becomes the street's lights.

**HERO OBJECT:** the oversized ceremonial **SCISSORS** closing on the taut red **RIBBON** — and the ribbon's two severed halves, which are the SAME object at every later stage: taut red line → severed → two recoiling arcs → threaded scalloped festoon → knots budding into bulbs → the block's lit garland. **ONE object, six states, no cut.** Blades 150×340 each, chrome `#F3EEE0`→`#6E7480` with a 4px hot specular and a 60px travelling glint; clay `#D2724E` enamel bows; **chrome** pivot disc r26 (not brass). Ribbon: 46px body `#C44A3A` with a `#E0705C` highlight and `#8E2F26` underside, satin weave, spanning x86→946 at y430 with a catenary sag to y452.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, wide across the whole block — the furthest-right the camera has walked, directly in front of the stretch the rig serves. Authored WIDE at camZ 1.00. Ground GY=700, kerb y686, tarmac apron 700-792. Facades y250-560 in fixed order: SMILE DEPOT x20-232 · PLANET SWOLE x244-452 · alley gap x452-496 (god-ray) · RE/MIX x496-712 · PIZZA PEPPI x724-930. Far-right sliver x950-1012, half-cropped and in cool shadow, is the roller shutter with its brass plaque at (985,372). **Bolted to the facades at y~316 are SEVEN iron garland BRACKETS at x = 86/236/386/506/656/806/946 — visible and conspicuously EMPTY from f24. They were always there; that is the "already built" beat stated in set-dressing rather than words.** Bloatcorp x690-900 y280-420. Nothing is constructed in this scene: the rig is turning behind Peppi, the cart with its ring-stained tablet is standing, S7's deck is still unfurled on the pavement at x40-230. **Only the lights change.**

**SHOT LIST**
- **f0-16 (with the 6 donated frames) MACRO, THE BITE.** camZ 2.58→2.70 — a slow TIGHTENING push so the macro is never dead; transformOrigin locked on (506,430) for the whole scene. Visible authored window x[319,693] y[271,589], **filled edge to edge**: two enormous chrome jaws converging on the 46px ribbon, hard specular raking DOWN the steel and crossing the bevel at f6, ribbon rim-lit from behind so the fibres glow through. Blade tips travel 8px/f authored = 21px/f on screen. **Standing on the TOP blade, absurdly enlarged by the macro, is PIDGEY, pecking at a sequin on the satin, entirely oblivious that the blade he is on is closing.** Ribbon fibres visibly stretch under Canva's pull; two threads pop at f8. f15 Pidgey's head snaps up, shock face. Backdrop behind the jaws is a blurred(6px) warm shopfront plus one god-ray — no empty quadrant.
- **f16 THE CUT.** Jaws meet at (506,430). ONE frame of white specular bloom, r90, `mixBlendMode: screen` — the only pure white in the scene — plus an 8-spoke spark starburst and 14 red fibre flecks. The ribbon parts. The pull-back begins on this exact frame.
- **f16-28 RELEASE, RECOIL, CATAPULT (the donated frames land here).** camZ 2.62→2.06, a deliberately SLOW release so the recoil is legible. The halves whip apart along arcs: LEFT about Canva's grip at (110,418), RIGHT about Flossula's wrist at (672,452). **CANVA is slingshotted backwards out of frame LEFT**, both fists still locked on the ribbon, cape snapping, T-square spinning out of his grip; exits x<0 at f24. The owners' hands enter from below. The scissors OPEN wide and swing DOWN and out through the foreground plane, carried by Bulk lowering them — fully below y792 by f38. **The tool leaves downward, the ribbon goes upward; the only exit in the scene and it crosses nobody.** Pidgey flaps up with a scrap of ribbon in his beak. Blur wrapper drops 5.4→2.2.
- **f28-36 THE HOOK-UP (the morph beat, now 8 free frames wide).** camZ 2.06→1.62; blur to 0. Both flying halves STRAIGHTEN instead of falling: they rise to the seven brackets at y316 and thread through them, settling into a classic scalloped street festoon — one continuous line from (86,330) through 236/386/506/656/806 to (946,322), sagging ~70px between brackets. **The shared centre anchor is the x506 bracket: the point where the blades severed them is the point that rejoins them.** ⭐ **THE RIBBON STAYS RED** (fix — the recolour to cord is DELETED): 24 small clay knots appear along it, 4 per sag, and the bulbs will bud straight out of the red satin. A red-corded festoon is more legible as "the same ribbon", which is the entire point of the morph, and deleting the crossfade buys the whole window for the thread-and-sag geometry. The empty brackets are visible for a beat before they fill.
- **f36-48 IGNITION WAVE (lateral L→R — the reel's ONE pure lateral wave).** camZ 1.62→1.20. Each knot BUDS over 3 frames into a 30×42 teardrop bulb (`#E7B24C` body, `#F6E4A0` core, brass cap) and ignites with a 70px bloom. **The wave runs x86→x946 across f36-48 = 72px/frame, FASTER than the camera.** Behind each lit section a Pool blooms on the facade and a second on the tarmac. Each shop's sign wakes as a SHAPE as the wave passes — tooth, dumbbell, balloon, slice — and a folded pennant garland unfurls 130px down that shop's awning over 4 frames (24,700px² each at 32px/f) with a 14-piece confetti burst. **f42: BULK**, handed the slack severed half, absently rips it in half out of habit and stands holding two useless pieces. **f45: FLOSSULA's** own section lights above his head, he recoils and throws his cape over his face — **the only creature on Maple St who hates the lights that just made him money** (recoil use 4 of 5). Sparky sprints x-60→1080 in the road plane at 54px/f, racing the wave and losing.
- **f48-56 WIDE SETTLE + THE COPYCAT.** camZ 1.20→1.045→1.005→1.000, decelerating into the authored wide with a 3px translateY jolt at f52 (never a scale undershoot — the floor is hard-clamped at 1.000). Full block lit, the strand and its 24 bloomed bulbs a luminous band across the entire panel width; sun flare kisses top-right. f48: the pull-back reveals **VITO** watching through **binoculars made of two rolled-up invoices** (wrong-tool #4); he lowers them, ribbon ledger **340px**, snagged on the foreground hydrant. **⭐ f49-53 THE SECOND LAUGH (fix — the plaque glint is DELETED and replaced here):** the wave reaches the far-right bracket at (946,322) and STOPS — the shutter sliver stays unlit, the one dead frontage the lights do not reach, **and the camera does not slow for it and nothing tinks.** Half a beat later, across the road, **BLOATCORP's entire cubicle grid lights up in a copycat wave — except every one of their windows is the same dead fluorescent green, and one designer stands up to admire it** (a different sprite two seats over from the permanent sleeper, who never wakes). Loop re-armed by absence, not by decoration. f56: Claude dead in frame, paws in pockets, head turned to camera; the cart awning's price tag swings into the bottom-right, still blank — the hook into S9.

**CAMERA:** ONE unbroken accelerating macro-to-wide dolly-out. No cut, no sub-beat — a cut would fracture 57 frames. Single transform on the scene wrapper, `transformOrigin: "506px 430px"` held for the entire scene. camZ = `IV(lf,[0,13,16,28,36,44,51,54,57],[2.58,2.70,2.62,2.06,1.62,1.20,1.045,1.005,1.000])` with out-cubic across the f16→f51 span so the retreat reads slow-slow-then-rushing. **camZ NEVER goes below 1.000**, so `origin+(x-origin)*maxScale ≤ 996` is satisfied by construction and no authored element can be thrown past the edge; the audit instead runs at f13 (peak 2.70) — visible authored window x[319.4,692.6] y[271.4,588.6], confirm it is filled corner to corner and nothing legible sits outside it. Second instrument: a blur wrapper on every plane except P2-HERO, `IV(lf,[0,16,28,36],[6,5.4,2.2,0])`, so the rack-focus and the dolly resolve together. The camera is itself the guaranteed large mover from f16 (a point at window edge travels ~24px/f at f36); f0-f16 is covered by the closing blades (150×340 = 51,000px² authored each, tips at 21px/f on screen).

**LIGHT:** hard low golden key from camera-RIGHT; the light state INVERTS mid-shot. f0-16 (macro): one blown specular rakes DOWN the near blade and crosses the bevel at f6, a cool `#A6B8CC` rim on the far blade so the two read as separate metal, and the ribbon lit from BEHIND so the satin glows translucent red where the weave is thinnest — the cut point is the brightest pixel in the panel. f16: ONE frame of white bloom, the only pure white. f16-36: neutral golden hour, god-ray through the alley gap at 18°; shopfronts still sit in cool ambient shade (`#B8987A` blue-shifted 8%) because the lights are not on yet. f36-48: **the ignition wave is its own TRAVELLING light source** — a moving warm front, each lit section dropping a Pool on the facade (`rgba(255,226,164,0.85)`→`rgba(231,178,76,0.34)`→transparent) and a second on the tarmac, plus a 70px halo per bulb, **so every sprite's cast shadow re-angles as its section lights and the shadows swing left-to-right in the same wave.** f48-56: the block is warmer and richer than at any prior point and STAYS that way through S15; pavement carries seven overlapping warm pools. **COLD HOLDOUTS, deliberate:** Bloatcorp's copycat grid is fluorescent grey-green, and the shutter sliver stays dark.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P0 FG blur 4px 2× warm-dark silhouette (the hydrant at x62 with Vito's ribbon snagged and wrapped twice, the scooter's rear wheel and mirror x900-1000, an out-of-focus awning corner top-left). **The scissors EXIT downward through this plane across f28-38 and are fully below y792 by f38 — the tool's only exit, and it crosses no figure.** · P1 near-FG confetti/atmosphere z30, **restricted to the x-bands [0,86] and [946,1012] only** — the outer sixths where no sprite ever stands, so it buys depth without ever crossing a face · P2 HERO always sharp (the scissors, the ribbon, and every later state of that same object; y271-589 at macro ≈ 82%, a full-width luminous band y280-470 at the wide ≈ 58%) · P3 street actors groundY 700 via `<Actor>` with contact shadows 1.12× sprite width: CANVA x96 size 200 (far-left lane, braced two-fisted on the left ribbon end, exits f24) · YOU-CLAUDE x300 size 236 (hands in pockets, head already turned to camera) · FLOSSULA x672 size 210 (right ribbon end looped round his wrist) · BULK x892 size 268 (both paws on the bows — he is the one actually cutting). **Spacing: 96→300 = 204 (need 185) · 300→672 = 372 · 672→892 = 220 (need 203). All clear.** · P4 shopfronts y250-560 with the seven empty brackets · P5 far z1 (sun disc at (905,150) r52, skyline, clock tower, water tower, two birds) · P6 road plane (Sparky at groundY 776 size 84 — his sprite top computes to y692, just under the pavement figures' ground line, so he clips only their shadow ellipses; **this is how a full-width runner satisfies rule 4**).

**ESCALATION:** S7 ended crane-down on a paper deck along one stretch of pavement — one shop, pavement level, horizontal, medium. S8 steps up on four measurable axes. (1) SCALE OF MOVE: camZ 2.70→1.000 is the largest camera traverse to this point, executed in 1.71s. (2) SCOPE: the first time the WHOLE BLOCK acts as one system in a single beat — S4 was one claw, S5 four walls one at a time, S6 one rig, S7 one deck; S8 is seven brackets, four facades, four awnings and 24 bulbs firing together. (3) **PERMANENT STATE CHANGE:** the first irreversible world change. The street's lights come ON here and stay on through S15, which is what makes "visibly richer and warmer by S15" literally true. (4) REGISTER: every prior scene was a task completing; this is a civic CEREMONY — first confetti, first bloom-white frame, first crowd sound. And the labour ladder drops another rung: a hero who does not even hold the scissors. Intensity 7→8, delivered in the shortest scene, so density per second spikes rather than duration. It also pays a structural debt: S6 erected the rig, S7 ran one job through it, S8 is the ribbon coming off — the only ordering in which "it's ALREADY built" can be true.

**GAG:** **CAPTAIN CANVA IS HOLDING THE FAR END.** Both fists locked on the ribbon, heels dug in, tongue out, taking a ceremonial ribbon with total professional seriousness — the only character doing physical labour and the only one not being paid. At f16 the tension releases and he is slingshotted backwards out of frame like a snapped elastic band. **And the half he was gripping is the half that becomes the LEFT garland span: his labour literally hangs the lights, and he is not there to see them come on.** SECOND (the reason the macro is worth having): for the first sixteen frames the audience is looking at an enormous unidentifiable PIDGEY standing on the top blade, pecking at a sequin, oblivious that the blade he is on is closing — **the joke only resolves when the camera pulls back.** THIRD: **the Bloatcorp copycat grid lights up in dead fluorescent green** and one designer stands up to admire it. FOURTH (f42, background): Bulk absently RIPS the slack severed half in half out of pure habit. UNDER ALL OF IT: Claude's paws never leave his pockets and his head is turned to camera for the entire cut. He did not cut it, did not hold it, did not hang it.

**LIVING WORLD:** Pidgey on the blade f0-15, then flapping up with a scrap of ribbon (one theft per appearance) — **bare-headed here; the traffic-warden cap is not until S12.** Sparky sprints the full panel width in the ROAD plane at 54px/f across f36-56, racing the light wave and losing by a full shop length. The laundromat/PEPPI neon flickers twice at f42 and then commits, half a beat behind the wave. A delivery bike wobbles right-to-left at y640 BEHIND all the actors (figures occlude it; it never crosses in front of anyone). The alley god-ray picks up a dust plume when the pennants drop. A single confetti disc lands on Flossula's cape at f45 and he flicks it off without looking, still checking his fangs. Two birds cross the far plane.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **NONE.** No numeral, no label, no legible word on the hero at any zoom. Spine containment is clean. Shop identities read as glyph SHAPES only as the wave passes — tooth, dumbbell, balloon, slice — never as wordmarks; any facade lettering stays at background-plane scale. The brass plaque at (985,372) has no text on it and never will.

**MUTE CHECK:** giant scissors cut a red ribbon, the ribbon flies up and becomes the street's lights, the whole block switches on. ✓ GRAND OPENING — it's done, it's open, it's working. The read needs no prior scene, because a ribbon cut is one of the few universally legible "this is now OPEN" images, which is exactly why it can carry an abstract line with zero text. **The one thing the viewer must NOT read is "somebody is building something":** nothing is under construction, no tool touches any structure, the brackets were already bolted to the walls, and the cart, the rig and S7's deck are all standing untouched at f0. That absence is the whole scene and it is carried entirely by the pull-back.

**TRANSITION IN:** **MATCH-CUT ON THE RED.** No black, no wipe, no camera reset. S7's crane-down ends with the deck's last slide — a clay-red colour block on a shallow left-to-right diagonal — sweeping through frame centre. S8's f0 opens macro on the RED RIBBON at the identical screen position, identical diagonal angle and near-identical hue, so for about 4 frames the cut is invisible and the viewer believes they are still looking at a card. A 2-frame horizontal motion-blur streak in the same rightward direction carries the eye through the scale jump. Tightest join in the reel. ⚠️ **The 6 frames S8 needed came from the tail of S7's deck unfurl (already applied above). NEVER add an element to S8.**

**SFX:** emit as **L[7] + local** where **L[7] = 28.56** (a scene-local `at` here typechecks, renders and is SILENT). +0.00 low chrome tension hum / satin creak under load (v0.20, dur 1.0) · **+0.53 THE SHEAR: heavy metal snip layered with a taut-string PLUCK as the tension parts (v0.46) — the scene's accent and the only cue that should punch through Alex's line; do NOT truncate the string tail (dur+rel ≥ 90% of the measured file)** · +0.56 elastic recoil ZIP (v0.30, dur 0.8) · +0.60 pigeon flap flurry (v0.20) · +0.63 Canva yelp dopplering off frame-LEFT (v0.24, dur 0.9 — doppler chops if capped short) · +1.00 T-square THUNK · +0.96 soft rope-through-iron creak as the strand threads · **THE IGNITION LADDER (the signature sound): four light-switch CLUNKs each with a bulb-glass tink at +1.20 / +1.33 / +1.46 / +1.59, panned hard L progressively to hard R, pitched up one step each so the wave is audible as a rising four-note ladder** (v0.28, dur 0.6), riding a shimmer riser from +1.15 · +1.60 banner FWOOMP + confetti pop (v0.26, dur 1.0) with a low warm crowd "ahh" under it · +1.62 a dry fluorescent BUZZ-ON for the Bloatcorp copycat, deliberately ugly (v0.18) · +1.66 single sub bloom on the wide settle (v0.34, dur 1.6, tail allowed to ring under S9's head). ⛔ **No plaque glint tick — that cue is deleted.** VO is near-continuous (10 words in 1.71s) so everything but the SHEAR rides at duck floor, which is correct. 13 onsets in 1.71s — re-level against the VO after a solo `--codec=wav` render.

**WHY THIS:** the line forbids showing construction, so the scene had to find the one image that means "finished, and only now being let out". A ribbon cut is exactly that — the shop behind it was fitted, stocked and lit days before anyone touched the scissors — and it is the most universally legible "this is now OPEN" image in existence. Making the ribbon BECOME the festoon is what turns a good image into the right one: there is only ever one object on screen, in six states, so the eye never has to be told what is transforming, and the geometry closes the argument — the halves part at x506 and rejoin at the x506 bracket, so the instrument of severing is the point of connection. **The seven empty brackets, visible for a beat before they fill, carry the thesis in set-dressing: the hooks were always on the walls, and the only thing between the street and being lit was one red line.** Starting inside the jaws buys a free scale shock — for half a second the scissors are architecture — and the shock IS the payoff line. At 1.71s a cut would fracture the beat, but one accelerating dolly delivers five beats inside an unbroken move. The Canva catapult stages the reel's thesis as two bodies with no words. And the loop is re-armed honestly by ABSENCE: the light wave runs the entire block and dies one frontage short, leaving the shuttered door dark — pointed at by omission, not by a decorative tink that would spend S13's ammunition for nothing.

**REJECTED:** the "machine assembles itself" morph — the rig's crates, pole, canopy and bulbs flying together into a finished unit. (1) SEMANTICS: it shows something BEING built at the exact moment the VO says it is ALREADY built, so the image argues against the line. (2) RULE 2: S6's hero is that same rig being erected, so this makes the rig the BASE of two scenes. (3) It is inward-looking — it keeps the camera on one machine when the job of this beat is to prove the thing serves a whole BLOCK, which is what justifies the retainer logic in S12. Also rejected: a giant BRASS BREAKER LEVER thrown at the end of the street — a legitimately good "already built" object, but a lever is a MECHANISM ("here is the switch you flip", drifting toward the how), it duplicates S12's kinetic idea, it has no room for a second party holding the other end under tension so it cannot carry the Canva catapult, and brass is now ledger-restricted. Also rejected: making VITO the one catapulted — funnier in isolation, but he must survive intact to be flattened by the billboard at S13 and deploy the parachute a second too late; the joke only truly works on the character who is genuinely trying. Also rejected: an unfurling grand-opening BANNER with lettering — text on screen, duplicates the caption verbatim, and a slower flatter move with none of the elastic release 1.71s needs. Its one good idea, the pennant unfurl, survives demoted to background dressing.
