### S9 · STEP3 — "THE PRICE GETS PHYSICALLY SMALLER"
**LINE + DUR:** 30.27–32.73 (2.46s, 74 frames, lf 0-73; SHOT = lf<54 ? A : B). *"Three, price it so it's an easy yes. $20 a post."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** The price was not argued down, it SHRANK. A cold chained agency slab so heavy it bows the awning sheds its two literal zeros — they clonk onto the tarmac and roll away like wheels — and becomes a small warm cardboard tag a man can flick with his little finger. **$20 is the SIZE of the object, not a claim about it.**

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE PRICE TAG** on the cart awning, authored as ONE object in unbroken metamorphosis: it opens as a 560×420 riveted slate-grey chained **SPLIT-FLAP PRICE SLAB** (left 268 top 176) and ends as a 168×214 cream/clay eyeleted cardboard **SWING TAG** on a single string (left 430 top 300). Same object, one continuous shrink, no cut. Nothing else hangs, and nothing else transforms by destroying two-thirds of itself.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, the PIZZA PEPPI / laundromat stretch — macro-close for the first time since S5's stencil. AWNING front rail x40..980 at y104 h22, clay + cream scalloped valance hanging 34px, free end propped by a clay pole at x560 down to y700. Ground y700, near kerb y748, counter top y560. SCREEN-LEFT behind the counter: **YOU-CLAUDE, hands in pockets, x88 groundY676 size178**, with the SHRINK-WRAPPED TABLET flat beside him at (150,548), plastic sheen plus a coffee ring. PAVEMENT front-of-queue: **COUNT FLOSSULA x258 groundY716 size196**. Behind him a distance-scaled queue at size 118-132, groundY 654, x 60/132/196 — three sprites max at readable scale. SCREEN-RIGHT entering low off-frame: **BULK's one enormous arm**, band y578..650. FAR-LEFT SIXTH, deep plane: **CANVA** at an easel on shop ONE, size 105 groundY 648, hand-lettering ONE tiny price card with a spirit level. Across the road in its own building's shadow: **BLOATCORP** — which becomes SHOT B's frame. Slab bg `linear-gradient(158deg,#7A828F,#3F4753)`, 4 rivets, recessed readout window x296 y240 w500 h268 bg `#10151D`, `inset 0 8px 18px rgba(0,0,0,0.65)`, with a **CLAY `#D2724E` 6px felt gasket at its lip** (carrying S8's ribbon-red across the cut). Four split-flap digit modules at x 372/470/568/666, top 258, w92 h232. Two 14px black chains to the rail at y126; **the rail BOWS +54px under the weight**, valance sagging, clay pole visibly flexed. Final tag: cream `#F7F3EA` paper grain, clay 6px border, **chrome** grommet r14 at (514,300), 3px jute string to the rail.

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-13 (A1) THE WALL.** Open ALREADY mid-macro-push (camScale 1.02→1.07, origin 506,396) with S8's confetti still falling through the blurred FG. The hero is a SLAB: 560×420 riveted slate-grey, four rivets, two heavy chains, the rail bowed +54px and its valance sagging, the pole flexed. **All four flap modules run FAST and UNREADABLE** — vertical blur plus a travelling seam highlight, a 500×268 = 134,000px² field of motion. **The readout sits RECESSED IN COLD SHADE while the whole street around it is golden: the number is a cold hole punched in a warm street.** A long hard slab-shadow rakes LEFT. Flossula is already backing off, one hand raised, mirror lowered. Pidgey perched on the rail directly above.
- **lf14-20 (A1b) ⭐ THE FALSE LANDING — NO LEGIBLE FIGURE (fix).** The flaps clack to a stop right-to-left, one every 2 frames at lf14/16/18/20, weight rising with each hit. **Only the leading `$` resolves; the four digit modules stay at 40% vertical blur.** On the last clack the slab DROPS 6px on its chains, the rail bows a further 9px, dust puffs off the valance, and Pidgey is launched off in a flurry. **⛔ `$2,000` and `$200` are DELETED from the reel** — the slab's SIZE, its two chains, the bowed rail and Flossula keeling over deliver "far too expensive" with zero legible figure, and the mute check already says so. The gag is funnier because he faints at a number he cannot even read. camScale →1.10.
- **lf20-30 THE SETUP HOLD (5 frames) + THE TIMBER.** The blurred readout holds lf20-25 only, then the first zero begins unbolting at lf26. **lf22 FLOSSULA KEELS** (recoil family, use 5 of 5 — the peak): pivot at his heels (258,716), rotate 0→-86° over lf22-30 on a stiff-plank ease, cape flapping up over his own face, hand mirror spinning out and skittering 60px across the flagstones. **He falls LEFT, AWAY from the hero, and the fall TERMINATES AT x148** (moved from x96 — Claude's sprite spans x44-132, so x96 put the falling torso through him; x148 clears it entirely). The three-sprite queue leans back in unison 11°. A 196px sprite carving a 90° arc: the scene's first monster mover.
- **lf26-39 (A2) IT DROPS ITS ZEROS.** **THE MORPH BEGINS AND NEVER STOPS.** lf26: the RIGHTMOST digit module (a zero) pops four bolts with four sparks, tips forward off its mounts, falls, and **CLONKS onto the tarmac at lf36 — being a 0, it ROLLS off right like a wheel.** The instant it leaves, the casing SHRINKS to fit: w 560→452, left 268→322, bottom 596→566, all continuous, edges travelling ~9px/f **so the HERO ITSELF is the large mover.** lf30-42: BULK's arm reaches in low from the right and tries to flick the slab with a **THUMB** at (770,566) — too big, it just thuds and the whole slab swings like a gong. Material crossfade starts: slate → `#F7F3EA`/`#E8DCC6`, radius 10→18, rivets fading, the recessed dark window bleeding out to printed paper.
- **lf40-48 (A2b) AND THE OTHER ONE.** lf40: the second zero unbolts and drops, CLONK at lf47, rolls LEFT — Sparky is already sprinting the near kerb after it. Casing continues w 452→300→168, h 420→282→214, centre x settling 548 then easing to 514. The chains crossfade out while a single 3px jute string draws in from the rail to the grommet. **The rail UN-BOWS as the weight leaves: +54 → +12, and holds, coiled.** lf42-48: Bulk gives up on the thumb and **swaps to a PINKY**, his contact point tracking inward with the shrink — (770,566) → (700,545) → (636,516) — **his own finger is a live readout of how small the thing has got** (this is the ONLY place the thumb→pinky gag is played as a setup; S12's version is replaced). camScale at PEAK 1.18 at lf46.
- **lf49-53 THE CLACK (the VO says "$20" here).** Bulk's pinky FLICKS the tag's bottom-right corner at (636,516) and THAT is what lands it. One big warm wooden CLACK: **the readout snaps to $20, hand-inked, clay underline, on cream board — the FIRST legible figure in the scene.** The tag swings +26° settling to a lazy ±7° pendulum; the rail SPRINGS from +12 sag to -18 up-bow and rattles. Camera RELEASES — a 4-frame snap pull-back 1.18→1.06. The warm CLAY POOL floods on cue: radial at (548,700), 720×260, 0→0.9 over lf49-56, reaching the far kerb. **FLOSSULA SPRINGS UPRIGHT** lf49-54, -86°→0 with a +8° overshoot, wallet already open, one twenty peeking, fangs bared in a grin — then immediately recoils from the new warm light at lf52-56. **His body has run the same morph as the object, on the same timeline.**
- **lf54-57 (B) THE WHIP.** SHOT CUT. Whip-pan right across the road: four frames of horizontal smear, the whole field translating ~250px/f under a 40px blur streak. Cart, awning, tag, Sparky and Canva all smear out left; Bloatcorp's glass box slides in and settles at lf57 on a 3-frame overshoot. **Light temperature flips MID-SMEAR: golden → flat cold slate.**
- **lf58-65 (B) THE COPY TRIES IT.** VITO's own board outside the Bloatcorp revolving door: a chrome A-frame carrying the SAME class of split-flap board, x380..700 y200..540 — deliberately smaller in frame and **NEVER legible at any frame.** Its digits spin the WRONG WAY, upward, into a grey unreadable blur; Vito (x790 groundY700 size250) has both hands on the crank, cranking harder, ribbon ledger **380px**, trailing off-frame left past the hydrant and doubling back. lf62-66 the board SWELLS — scaleX →1.09, scaleY →1.14, seams straining, one bolt pinging out. Camera creeps 1.03→1.09 with a 2° handheld drift.
- **lf66-73 THE JAM, THE SPRING, THE SMOKE.** lf66: JAM. Every digit freezes mid-flip on half-cards — a nonsense unreadable state — and the board judders under a 4-frame decaying shake (±7px x, ±5px y). lf68: a coiled SPRING fires 200px out of the top on a whip trail; **PIDGEY snatches it mid-air and leaves with it.** lf69-73: grey smoke pours from the seams in three overlapping billows 200-340px each, rising 9px/f, orange emissive at their base — **and the ONLY volumetric light in the shot is a single thin god-ray hitting that plume, so the failure is the sole lit event.** Vito fans it with the invoice (wrong-tool #5) and the ribbon whips across frame x-400→x700 (~63,000px² of moving paper). Behind the glass **ALL 30 CUBICLE DESIGNERS STAND UP IN UNISON to stare at the smoke, rising 26px together over six frames — one stays face-down asleep.** Frame-left, PIZZA PEPPI holds up his pizza box with a fresh wet **red X** stamped on the lid, shoulders down (refusal #4). Last 6 frames: the warm clay light from across the street creeps onto the left quarter of Vito's pavement. Hook into S10's tills.

**CAMERA:** TWO MOVES, OPPOSED. SHOT A = the tightest MACRO DOLLY PUSH since S5's stencil: camScale 1.02 → 1.07 → 1.10 → 1.11 → **PEAK 1.18 at lf46**, origin (506,396). **The camera pushes IN at exactly the rate the hero shrinks, so for a full second the object HOLDS APPARENT SIZE while physically destroying itself** — the eye reads the shrink off the growing world and feels it before it can name it. ⚠️ Because that defeats itself on a 6-inch screen if it is the ONLY cue, the shrink is triple-encoded (see WHY THIS): size, material/temperature, and Bulk's tracking contact point. Then a 4-frame SNAP PULL-BACK 1.18→1.06 on the clack as the release. SHOT B = WHIP-PAN (4 frames, ~250px/f + 40px blur, 3-frame settle overshoot) then a creeping push 1.03→1.09, origin (500,430), plus a 4-frame decaying shake at the jam. **Bleed solved backwards:** at peak 1.18, screen x = 506+(x-506)×1.18, so key graphics sit inside x[78,934] y[61,731]. Checks — tag at lf46 x464..632 → 456..654 ✓; slab at lf0 x268..828 → 225..886 ✓; slab bottom y596 → 632 ✓; **Flossula's fall terminates at x148 → screen 84 ✓** (his head, the readable part, never goes below x186); Bulk's elbow is intentionally off-frame, his FINGER never exceeds x770 → 817 ✓. **Audit at lf46, not on a static frame.**

**LIGHT:** SHOT A: the street's golden key from camera-RIGHT rakes the slab face at ~18° so the riveted casing throws a long hard shadow LEFT — and critically **the readout is RECESSED IN SHADE (`#10151D`), so the expensive number is a COLD HOLE in a warm street.** Three god-ray shafts at x300/620/880, 34px wide, 8° tilt, with suspended dust. **As the casing warms and shrinks, its cast shadow shortens and softens with it — the light does the storytelling on its own.** Warm bounce kicks UP off the cream counter onto Flossula's and the queue's undersides so the timber reads even in silhouette. ON THE CLACK the CLAY POOL floods, a clay rim-light appears on the tag's left edge, its paper lifts `#F0EADC`→`#F7F3EA`, and a foreground string-light bulb pops on top-right — the street is measurably warmer at S9 than at S2. **SHOT B INVERTS EVERYTHING:** Bloatcorp sits in its own building's shadow — flat cold slate, desaturate 25%, no key at all, only a sick green-white fluorescent underglow bleeding out of the lobby. One thin god-ray, and the SMOKE PLUME is the only thing it lights. From lf69 the burning board's orange seam-emissive underlights Vito's face and the belly of the smoke — **the expensive side of the street is grey until it catches fire.**

**DEPTH PLANES:** z58-64 FG blur 8px 1.6× (the scooter's rear wheel and mirror cropped bottom-left, a hanging string-light bulb top-right, S8's leftover confetti through the first half-second, and **SPARKY** size 210 groundY 748 sprinting the near kerb lf36-56 chasing a rolling zero, cropped by the panel edge at peak zoom) · z40-46 HERO sharp 1.0× (the transforming object, its chains-then-string, the awning rail and valance, the flexed pole, its cast shadow; owns x[268,828] shrinking to x[430,598]; **Flossula stands at z46 just in front of the slab's bottom-left corner as an 88×29 sliver of STABLE overlap — never a crossing**) · z24-32 actor plane groundY 676-716 (Claude with his hands in his pockets and the tablet coaster; Bulk's arm entering low from the right; Peppi and the three-sprite queue. **Spacing enforced: Claude x88/r89 to Flossula x258/r98 = 170px vs the 159px merge floor; contact shadows authored WIDER than each sprite**) · z14-20 shopfront plane blur 1.5px 0.35× (the cart body and counter, PEPPI's chrome + neon facade, the laundromat's buzzing sign and turning drum, awnings, kerb, and the S6 rig's silhouette still turning down the block) · z8-12 far plane blur 4px 0.15× (Bloatcorp — **which becomes SHOT B's hero plane after the whip** — Canva tiny at his easel, parked traffic) · z2-6 sky/haze (hazy clock tower and water tower, a gull crossing lf8-30, warm gradient).

**ESCALATION:** S8 was one continuous ADDITIVE pull-back — a ribbon cut and a block's lights snapping on, the widest and most generous frame in the reel. S9 inverts every axis at once: widest → tightest (the first macro since S5), additive → **SUBTRACTIVE** (the hero destroys two-thirds of itself), celebratory → transactional. Three firsts land here. (1) The first time a NUMBER is attacked rather than a workflow. (2) The first time money leaves a wallet ON CAMERA in direct reaction to a figure — S10's tills are the consequence, so the next scene's KA-CHING is already loaded. (3) **The first time Vito's machinery physically BREAKS** — until now he has only been inconvenienced; here his equipment jams, blows a spring and catches fire, stepping his ladder from "annoyed" to "losing". It also ends higher than it starts: it opens on a cold chained slab that repels a customer and closes with warm light crossing the road and reaching the enemy's feet.

**GAG:** **THE PRICE FALLS BY LITERALLY DROPPING ITS TWO ZEROS** — they unbolt, spark, clonk onto the tarmac, and being zeros they ROLL AWAY LIKE WHEELS; Sparky chases one the full width of the panel and Pidgey inspects the other. Around it: Count Flossula, a vampire dentist, keels straight over backwards **at a number he cannot even read**, stiff as a felled plank, cape flying up over his own face, mirror skittering across the flagstones — then springs bolt upright at $20 with his wallet ALREADY open, and immediately recoils from the warm light. Bulk cannot flick the tag because his thumb is too big, so he switches to a PINKY, **and his contact point tracks inward (770 → 700 → 636) as the object shrinks, so his own finger is simultaneously the joke and the measurement** — and his pinky flick is what lands the $20. Across the road Vito cranks his board the WRONG WAY until it swells, jams on half-flipped cards, fires a spring that Pidgey steals in mid-air, and starts smoking, which he fans with an invoice. Peppi's pizza gets a red X stamped on the lid. Far left, Canva is still hand-lettering ONE price card with a spirit level.

**LIVING WORLD:** Pidgey perches on the rail lf0-21, is launched off in a puff of feathers by the slam, drops down to inspect a rolled zero, then reappears across the road to snatch Vito's spring at lf68. Sparky sprints the near kerb lf36-56 after the other zero, cropped by the panel edge. The laundromat neon half-flickers on a bad ballast. A gull crosses the far sky lf8-30. A foreground bulb pops on at lf50 as the clay pool floods. S8's confetti is still falling through the blurred FG in the first half-second. The queue leans back in unison at the slab and forward again at $20. Behind Bloatcorp's glass the whole grid swivels then stands up as one to watch the board burn — **and one designer stays face-down asleep through the entire thing.**

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **$20** — the final and only legible numeral, hand-inked on the tag, from lf49 to the cut. ⭐ **This is the S9 spine fix: the slab's false landing is a blurred four-module state with only the `$` resolved, so `$2,000` and `$200` never exist as readable figures anywhere in this reel.** Vito's board across the street is NEVER legible at any frame — spinning blur, then a jammed half-flipped nonsense state — so **no agency figure ever enters the number spine.** No other words, no labels; shop signage stays parallax-small and out of focus, and Bloatcorp's nameplate is an embossed logo SHAPE. Captions carry no numeral over lf14-30 either.

**MUTE CHECK:** a huge cold grey chained slab hangs off an awning, so heavy the rail is bowed — a customer backs away and falls flat on his back. Two round modules unbolt and roll off down the street like wheels. The slab shrinks and warms as they go, sheds its chains for a string, and ends as a small cream cardboard tag a man can flick with his little finger — at which point the fallen customer springs up with his wallet already open and warm light floods the pavement. Whip across the road: the grey copy of the same machine swells, jams, fires a spring and smokes while thirty office workers stand up to stare. ✓ **Size = friction.** Nothing needs reading, and the beat survives a muted, thumb-scrolled viewing because the verdict is delivered by a body, not a caption.

**TRANSITION IN:** **MATCH-CUT ON FALLING CONFETTI.** S8 ends wide on the freshly lit block with confetti in the air; S9 opens on THAT SAME confetti, now in the macro foreground blur plane, with the camera already mid-push past it onto the slab. Same street, same continuous time, same block position — only the lens has tightened. Secondary carry: **S8's ceremonial-ribbon red is picked up as the CLAY felt gasket at the readout well's lip**, so the first colour we see is the last colour we left on. No cut-flash, no wipe.

**SFX:** emit as **L[8] + local** (L[8]=30.27). 0.00 split-flap chatter bed, low and sustained under the VO at −19dB · 0.47/0.53/0.60/0.67 four hard flap CLACKS rising in weight, the last layered with a chain-drop clunk and a wing flurry · 0.73 stiff body-fall thud + cape flap + a small metal mirror skitter · 0.87 and 1.33 two bolt-pop sparks · 1.20 and 1.57 zero CLONK on tarmac plus a wooden ROLL that dopplers off frame · 1.40 heavy dull gong-thud (Bulk's thumb) · **1.63 THE CLACK — big warm wooden hit with a small grommet ring, the loudest cue in the scene** · 1.67 awning boing + a low warm swell + wallet flap · 1.80 whip whoosh, 4 frames, pitched down into the settle · 2.07 metal strain / rivet ping · 2.20 grinding JAM under the shake · 2.27 comic metallic spring BOING + pigeon flap · 2.33 steam hiss into a low fire crackle **with a long tail at a LOW level so it reads big**, plus a wet rubber-stamp THUNK (the red X) · 2.42 thirty chairs scraping as one dry unison scrape. ⛔ **MEASURE the library before capping any `dur`** — the spring, the roll and the fire tail need dur+rel ≥ 90% of true file length. Per-cue volumes do NOT transfer; re-level against THIS scene's density (very dense at 0.47-0.73 and 1.63, sparse at 1.93). The only real VO gaps are at ~0.58 and ~1.48, so place the two hero cues there.

**WHY THIS:** "price it so it's an easy yes" is a claim about friction, and friction has a physical analogue the eye reads instantly: **SIZE**. So the scene never asserts the claim, it PERFORMS it — the object destroys two-thirds of itself, and the two things it sheds are literally the two zeros, which then roll away into the living world as props. **Three separate readouts run the same morph on the same timeline**, so a mute viewer gets it three times over: the object's SIZE (560×420 → 168×214), its MATERIAL and TEMPERATURE (riveted cold slate + chains → warm cardboard + string), and the CUSTOMER'S BODY (flat on his back → upright with his wallet open). Bulk's contact point tracking inward means even the comedy is a measurement. The camera pushes in at the rate the hero shrinks so the change is FELT before it is named — but because that alone would leave a phone viewer with only the numeral, the shrink is carried by material and by two bodies as well, which is also exactly why the expensive figure can be deleted without costing the scene anything. Casting is right too: Claude must be the least active body here because the claim is about someone else's nervous system. And SHOT B is not a second scene, it is the transformation's **CONTROL GROUP** — the identical machine across the road attempts the same move, spins the wrong way, and blows up. Proof by mirror, in 20 frames. It also discharges the flagged small-prop risk: the shrinking hero (~9px/f), Flossula's 196px 90° timber, the 250px/f whip, the 340px smoke billows and the unfurling ribbon put a ≥40,000px² mover in every one-second bucket without adding a prop.

**REJECTED:** a CASH MORPH — a thick 40-page agency contract stack compressing into a single twenty. (1) OBJECT COLLISION: S10's hero is a till drawer full of twenties and S11's is a banded $1,000 brick on a scale; a cash-based hero here would make three consecutive scenes whose base object is money. (2) WRONG EMOTIONAL READ: a stack shrinking to one note reads as "you get paid less", which inverts the claim — the point is that $20 is frictionless for the BUYER. Slab-to-tag reads as "less resistance", which is what the VO argues. (3) NOTHING TO ACT ON: a note cannot be flicked with a pinky, hung from an awning, or backed away from, so Bulk, Flossula and the bowed rail all disappear and the scene collapses into a prop shot with no bodies in it. Also rejected: letting Flossula haggle for a beat — "the number nobody argues with" means there is structurally no negotiation available, so the reaction has to be involuntary. Also rejected: staging the open INSIDE the flap well as a separate full-bleed macro layer — a beautiful 8 frames, but it hides the one thing the scene needs established in shot one, how BIG and how HEAVY the slab is, and the bowed rail is the whole setup for the shrink.

### S10 · EASYYES — "ONE DRAWER, THE WHOLE BLOCK"
**LINE + DUR:** 32.73–35.10 (2.37s, 71 frames, lf 0-70). *"That's nothing to a business, and it's the number nobody argues with."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** $20 is so far beneath a business's notice that the money moves while nobody is even looking at it — and the reflex runs the whole street.

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE CHROME-AND-CREAM CASH-REGISTER TILL DRAWER** at Smile Depot — and its transformation: one closed drawer slab becomes **ONE CONTINUOUS OPEN DRAWER** spanning the full 1012px panel, with four shop cabinets riding on its single **chrome** rail (⛔ not brass — material ledger; chrome is already the register's bezel language). Unique silhouette in the reel. It is also the only object on Maple St whose entire meaning is "accepted, no argument" — **a drawer opening IS a yes.** The counter is ground plane, never hero.

**SETTING:** inside the open serving front of SMILE DEPOT, shot over Flossula's caped shoulder from the pavement side — camera ~62% along the block, between S9's cart awning and S11's kerb, so the walk keeps advancing and **the shuttered end is NEVER reached** (that would pre-spend S14). Counter slab top y=612, front face y[612,792] running off the panel bottom, oak with chrome nosing. Register world x[186,734] y[172,612]. Right gap x[740,1012] holds the dental chair (chair x[786,946], patient sprite cx 846, groundY 470, size 150). Flossula is a foreground-left OTS silhouette x[-70,258]. Through the window across the road: Bloatcorp's grid with one designer face-down. Shot B rides right past PLANET SWOLE, RE/MIX and PIZZA PEPPI, all three tills on the same rail. Golden hour, **string lights lit since S8** swaying above.

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-11 (A) OTS MACRO PUSH BEGINS.** camScale 1.020→1.052. A 5-frame horizontal whip-smear inherited from S9's pan eases out (0.9→0 by lf5) and we land tight over Flossula's caped shoulder — the smoke wisp from his jammed board drifts through the top-left corner and dissipates by lf9. **THE REGISTER IS A CLOSED SLAB:** body x[186,734] y[172,612]; scrolled chrome pediment with two finial knobs; a glass amount tower x[300,620] y[238,300] holding two blank cream cards; a 5×3 key bank of ivory-capped keys x[596,800] y[300,368]; drawer front slab x[192,728] y[498,598]; **the drawer SEAM is a single 2px dark line at y=498.** A clay-green banknote (132×62, portrait blob + guilloche, **NO digits**) slides right along the counter lane y=660 from x=250 to x=470, pushed by Flossula's foreground arm. **HIS HEAD NEVER TURNS** — eyeline locked hard right at the chair, tiny mirror up in his other claw checking his fangs. LARGE MOVER: cape 300×340 = 102,000px² sweeping 8px/f. A delivery truck's shadow bar begins its sweep across the tiled back wall.
- **lf11-22 THE SEAM BECOMES A LIGHT SOURCE, AND THE EJECT.** lf11-14 the seam CRACKS into a gold hairline — a div at y=496 growing 2→9px with a 6px bloom and a Pool beneath. **lf14-22 THE EJECT:** the drawer front (536×100) translates y 498→552 (54px in 8f = 6.75px/f, 53,600px² LARGE MOVER) and scaleX 1.000→1.045 for perspective widen, with a 3-frame overshoot bounce; a trapezoid TROUGH unfolds behind it, x[196,724] y[500,556], carrying 5 coin-tray dividers and a chrome note-clip bar. **GOLD POURS UP OUT OF IT:** hot bounce on the case underside, an elongated gold ellipse thrown forward onto the counter x[150,780] y[600,690], 0→0.52. lf16-22 **FLOSSULA RECOILS from the bright thing** — silhouette flinches 26px left, cape flares 210→300 wide, gold rim burning its right edge — **and STILL does not break eye contact.** lf16 PARALLEL, in a dedicated clear lane: **THE PATIENT'S ARM shoots dead horizontal**, hand travelling x 846→760 at y=494, holding note #2 pinched between two fingers, bib fluttering once, cotton-wool cheek bulge visible. Nothing else about him moves. Nobody looks at him.
- **lf22-24 (A tail, trimmed by 8 frames per fix) THE NOTE LANDS.** The counter note lifts and arcs up-right into the trough under the clip. The amount flag pops up in the glass tower carrying a **MOLAR SILHOUETTE** — no digits, ever. The note settles flat on a banded brick already in bay 3 — a sliver on a small mountain, and nobody reacts. **THE PATIENT'S ARM IS STILL EXTENDED, UNTAKEN.**
- **⭐ lf24-29 THE WHIP (fix — the hard cut is REMOVED).** The morph is no longer asserted across a one-pixel-tolerance cut. Instead: a 5-frame **WHIP-PAN on the existing rail** — content translateX with a 40px horizontal blur streak, **camScale held dead flat at 1.115 throughout** — so the drawer's chrome front lip (x[192,728], y[546,566]) is **continuously on screen and cannot break.** Aligned to the motion axis it reads SOLID while everything else smears; its edges extend left 192→-40 and right 728→1052 (43px/f each end), the specular becoming one white-hot continuous line with a 26px warm under-glow. Rail area 1012×46 ≈ 46,552px² LARGE MOVER. **Rail alignment is now a continuity constraint the renderer enforces, not a hand-authored coincidence.**
- **lf29-40 (B) THE RIDE BEGINS — Z-AXIS, NOT LATERAL (fix).** Four shops resolve into ONE drawer. ⭐ **The chain now runs TOWARD CAMERA in depth rather than laterally** (S8 owns the pure lateral wave): cabinets 1-4 sit at scale 1.00 / 0.78 / 0.60 / 0.46 receding **up-street**, and they open **near-to-far**, so the mover is a Z-axis chain. lf29-35: SMILE DEPOT's cabinet (scale 1.00) is already open. lf33-40 **PLANET SWOLE's cabinet (430×320 = 137,600px² at scale 0.78) RISES 42px off the rail in 6 frames** (7px/f), a gold wedge opening in the gap, **DUMBBELL** flag popping in its tower. **BULK** (x=330, groundY=546, size=250) **opens it by BUTTING it with the dumbbell he never stops curling** (⛔ the pinky is S9's and S12's — not repeated here), the other arm still mid-rep (90px travel). SPARKY enters the blurred FG at y[600,780], 260×180 = 46,800px², crossing the full 1012 at ~34px/f — ahead of the camera, so the drawers appear to pop in his wake.
- **lf40-52 THE CHAIN RECEDES.** lf40-46: RE/MIX's cabinet (400×300 at scale 0.60) lifts — **BATBRO's SOLD sign is jammed in the trough and flips up out of it, spinning end over end, and his cape catches the drawer pull so he is dragged 40px sideways, upside down.** HOUSE flag pops. Third CHUNK, a semitone up and quieter. In the deep across-the-road lane (0.34×, y[300,420], below hero z, never crossing the rail): **VITO shoves a forty-page concertina contract down the diner counter; it unfolds 260px and takes out the sugar shaker in a white puff.** Ribbon ledger **420px**, wrapped twice around a lamppost. Peppi shrugs, palms up, beaming. Coins spill the lip at x≈240/560/880 and bounce down the counter face. lf56-66: **PIDGEY** lands on the rail, lifts exactly one note out of Peppi's trough and exits up-right; Peppi shrugs at that too. Final 4 frames, camScale floors at 1.005: **one unbroken 1012px band of warm gold lighting the entire block FROM BELOW**, four cabinets riding it in depth, every drawer open, nobody arguing — and the eye now asking how much that pile actually is, which is exactly S11's scale.

**CAMERA:** TWO moves welded by one object — the drawer's chrome front lip. (A) lf0-24 OTS MACRO PUSH: origin `52% 54%` (526,428), camScale `IV(f,[0,24],[1.020,1.115])` — a real push, riding over Flossula's blurred caped shoulder. (B) lf24-70 **WHIP** (translateX + 40px blur, camScale HELD FLAT at 1.115 for the whole 5 frames — no cut) into a LATERAL RIDE + PULL-BACK: camScale `IV(f,[29,70],[1.115,1.005])`; floor at 1.005 so no sub-1.0 gap exposes the panel edge. World translate WX two-segment: [24,31] → 0→-560, [31,70] → -560→-1240, i.e. the street sheet moves LEFT at ~21px/f so the camera reads as continuing the left→right walk. ⚠️ **MATCH LAW (now enforced by the whip rather than by hope):** the chrome lip is authored at IDENTICAL panel-local y[546,566] on both sides of the whip and camScale does not change across it. **Bleed solved backwards per the camera-scale trap:** at peak 1.115 with origin x=526, authored x must satisfy 526+(x-526)×1.115 ≤ 996 → x ≤ 947, and ≥ 68. Register body x[186,734] → [146,758] ✓. Patient hand furthest reach x=760 → 787 ✓ (inside IG-safe 906). Origin y=428: peak maps y=612 → 633 ✓, y=172 → 143 ✓, y=792 → 834 (deliberately cropped — it is the ground plane). **Audit at lf24 (peak camScale), never on a static frame.**

**LIGHT:** low raking golden-hour sun from camera-RIGHT: warm gold key on the register's right edge, cool slate fill left, one god-ray through the shop window in shot A with dust motes. **The scene's real lighting idea: THE HERO OBJECT IS A LIGHT SOURCE THAT OPENS.** From lf11 the seam cracks into a gold hairline; from lf14 the trough emits — warm bounce thrown UP onto the case underside, and an elongated gold ellipse cast FORWARD across the counter with the register's own shadow inverted inside it. Pool under every trough. A cold cyan-white RING LIGHT deep in the shop back-lights Flossula and throws his raking shadow FORWARD across the tiled wall — **that cast shadow, authored 300×100 (wider than his 268px body), is his grounding, because his floor contact is hidden behind the counter face (`<Actor shadow={0}>` deliberately).** Bloom on the bell dome glint lf20-26 only. By lf70 that single point source has become a **1012px-wide bottom-lit warm band**: the block is physically warmer than at lf0, serving the escalating-warmth law without one added prop.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P1 FG silhouette blur 5px 0.20× (shot A Flossula's caped shoulder x[-70,258] full height, sweeping 8px/f as his arm extends; shot B **SPARKY** crossing at y[600,780] 260×180 plus the counter's chrome nosing bar and a hanging lamp corner) · P2 counter/ground 0.55× (oak slab surface y612, front face running off the panel bottom, chrome nosing y[606,616], a steaming cup at (322,560), the sliding banknote lane y660) · P3 HERO 1.0× (the register x[186,734] y[172,612] becoming the ONE CONTINUOUS CHROME RAIL y[546,566] spanning x[-40,1052], with **four cabinets riding it at scale 1.00 / 0.78 / 0.60 / 0.46 receding up-street**; ~58% of panel) · P4 mid actors 1.34× groundY 470-546 (the reclined patient, BULK curling at cabinet 2, BATBRO's SOLD sign flipping out of trough 3, PEPPI shrugging at cabinet 4) · P5 shopfront band 0.55× (Smile Depot's white subway tile with a textless tooth-chart poster and a buzzing neon molar; in shot B the sliding facades of PLANET SWOLE, RE/MIX, PEPPI and the laundromat) · P6 **DEDICATED VITO LANE** 0.34× low-contrast slate, band y[300,420], z BELOW the hero, never crosses the rail (Bloatcorp's glass box, lit grid, one designer face-down asleep, and Vito at the diner counter with his concertina contract) · P7 far 0.20× (sky gradient, clock-tower silhouette, hazy skyline, one god-ray from camera-right).

**ESCALATION:** S9 was ONE static cardboard object on ONE awning — a price tag clacking to its number while Vito's board smoked across the road. Nothing moved but a digit. S10 converts that displayed number into **MECHANICAL MOTION AND MULTIPLICATION**: the price stops being shown and starts being obeyed, by machines, four of them, across the whole block, with the money mass finally overtaking the camera. It climbs three times — one blind reach → one drawer firing open and becoming a light source → the drawer revealed as ONE rail with cabinets popping off it in depth faster than the camera can travel. It escalates the warmth law (one god-ray at the start, under-lit warm across all 1012px at the end) and the laziness spine (he touches NOTHING; the street opens itself). Intensity 7→7 held with rising kinetics, hooking S11 by ending on a heap of cash that visibly now needs weighing.

**GAG:** FOUR laughs in 2.37s, layered so one lands per beat. (1) **THE BLIND PAYER** — Flossula pays without ever breaking eye contact with his patient; keys ripple, drawer fires, and he recoils and HISSES at the gold light flooding out of his own cash drawer while his eyeline never moves a pixel. (2) **THE PATIENT — the button:** the till opens so casually that the man reclined in the chair, bib on, two cotton-wool pucks in his cheeks **so he physically CANNOT argue**, reflexively shoots one arm dead horizontal holding a second note. Nothing else about him moves. The arm is a lever. Nobody looks at him, nobody takes it, **and it is STILL hanging there when the whip carries us away — and Claude can't take it because his hands are in his pockets, which is the running gag paying off at the exact moment of payment.** (3) **BULK opens his register by BUTTING it with a dumbbell**, mid-rep, never breaking the set. (4) **PEPPI'S TROUGH already has a PIZZA SLICE in the coin tray** — he tried again, it's still in there, he shrugs at camera palms-up. Then Pidgey lifts one note straight out of that trough and Peppi shrugs at that too. **Nobody argues about anything on this street.**

**LIVING WORLD:** Bloatcorp's grid glows with one designer face-down asleep. VITO's concertina contract takes out the sugar shaker, which is still tumbling on the last frame. **CANVA** is pressed flat against the far-left shop window in shot A, holding up his one hand-drawn post and mouthing at it, ignored by every character on screen. The neon molar buzz-flickers at lf6 and the laundromat's OPEN flickers twice at lf48. A delivery truck's raking shadow bar (1012×300) sweeps left→right across the tiled back wall lf0-24. Steam curls off a paper cup. The dental drill's cord swings on its arm. Coins that spill the trough lip bounce and roll down the counter face. Sparky sprints the full panel width along the blurred FG at ~34px/f, appearing to LEAD the whip. String lights sway and warm.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **NONE.** Zero numerals, zero words, zero legible copy at any zoom. Spine containment is absolute: **$20 belongs to S9's cardboard tag ONLY**, so every banknote here is a clay-green rectangle with a portrait blob and guilloche, no digits; every register's amount flag carries a SHAPE, never a figure — **molar** (Smile Depot), **dumbbell** (Planet Swole), **house** (RE/MIX), **slice** (Pizza Peppi). ⭐ **DELIBERATE PLANT: this establishes shop = engraved icon so S14's nameplates land as RECOGNITION, not decoration — and S10 shows FOUR flags precisely so S14 can pay off three plus a fourth.** The S14 fourth nameplate is now the HOUSE, closing this plant.

**MUTE CHECK:** a shopkeeper reaches sideways without looking, a huge till BANGS open and floods gold light upward, a patient in a dental chair blindly sticks out more money that nobody takes, then the camera whips and the whole street's tills are revealed to be ONE long open drawer, cabinets popping off it up the street, filling with cash faster than the camera can travel. ✓ Everybody here is paying, instantly, and not one of them cares. **The transformation is legible purely as SILHOUETTE** — one closed slab → one open trough spanning the frame — which is the test that matters. Sprite discipline: every shot-B character is on open pavement at groundY 546 with contact shadows WIDER than the sprite, size-ramped 250 near → 180 down the block, spacing ≥0.85×(rA+rB), at most 4 sprites at readable scale.

**TRANSITION IN:** S9 ended whip-panned ACROSS the road onto Vito's jammed, smoking board, so S10 has to come back to the money side. **Inherit that exact motion rather than cutting:** lf0-5 carries a decaying horizontal smear easing out, plus a grey smoke wisp still drifting through the top-left corner (dissipated by lf9, replaced in the same corner by the register's chrome specular). The cut reads as the same whip RETURNING, motivated by the first ka-ching pulling the camera back — one continuous walk down the same pavement. No black, no wipe, no dissolve.

**SFX:** emit as **L[9] + local** (L[9]=32.73) — scene bodies are NOT Sequence-wrapped, so a scene-local `at` typechecks, renders and is SILENT. 32.73 whip-tail smear inherited from S9 (v0.14) · 32.95 paper slide on wood (v0.18) · 33.10 chrome tick as the seam cracks (v0.16) · **33.20 THE HERO CUE — cash-register KA-CHING: bell ding + wooden slam-out thunk + coin jingle layered (v0.42)** · 33.30 coin rattle in tray (v0.20) · 33.42 Flossula hiss, short and comic (v0.16) · 33.55 note-clip snap (v0.18) · 33.53 WHIP swoosh + short riser (v0.28) · 33.83 chunk 2 + dumbbell clank (v0.30) · 34.10 chunk 3 + SOLD-sign boing (v0.27) · 34.46 chunk 4 + wet slap (v0.24) — **each chunk a semitone up and ~2.5dB down so the chain reads as receding DEPTH rather than lateral distance** · 34.46-35.10 rising coin cascade, crescendo (v0.26) · 34.60 pigeon flap (v0.12) · 34.40 glass sugar-shaker rattle (v0.10). **TAIL DISCIPLINE:** measure before setting any `dur` — the ka-ching bell tails ~1.4s and the cascade ~1.6s; set dur+rel ≥ 90% or they chop. **Deliberately let the cascade tail RUN PAST 35.10 into S11** — it bridges straight into the scale. The ka-ching lands under "nothing to a business", so duck it hard and let the bell tail BLOOM into the comma gap before "and it's the number". Solo-render `--codec=wav`, never mix-minus.

**WHY THIS:** the line makes two claims and a till drawer carries both. "That's nothing to a business" is proved by **BLOCKING**, not by size comparison: Flossula reaches sideways and pays without breaking eye contact, and the patient — cotton wool in both cheeks, physically unable to argue — reflexively offers a second note into empty air that nobody takes. That is the most literal staging of "the number nobody argues with", and it's funny because it's unsolicited and unclaimed. "Nobody argues" is then proved **MECHANICALLY**: a drawer opening IS a yes, so four drawers opening is four yeses with no dialogue and no text. The idea that makes this a scene rather than a prop shot is the rail — the lip smears into a line on the whip and stays pinned, so four separate shops resolve into ONE drawer. That single trick does five jobs: it welds the two shots into one unbroken transformation with no fragile cut; it makes the camera move legible as street progress; it gives the scene a base silhouette nothing else owns; it satisfies the does-less spine (he touches nothing, the street opens itself); and it makes the hero object a **LIGHT SOURCE THAT OPENS**, so the payoff of the morph is also the lighting of the payoff. Running the cabinet chain in DEPTH rather than laterally is what keeps S8 and S12's waves distinct — three lateral sweeps in five scenes was the reel's single biggest repeat, and this is the one that gives way, because a receding chain also sells "the whole street" better than a queue does.

**REJECTED:** a GIANT BANKNOTE folding itself into a paper airplane that flies down the street into each till. Closest call — a clean single-object morph that flies the lateral move for free. Killed on three counts: (1) the plane only reads as MONEY if the note carries "$20", which violates spine containment — S9 owns that figure and a second appearance turns a spine into wallpaper; (2) a note that folds itself is magic, and the world's law is a RECOGNISABLE REAL situation with characters; (3) it makes the money the active agent and the shop owners passive scenery, which INVERTS the VO — the point is that businesses hand it over without arguing, so the transformation must happen in THEIR machines, at THEIR counters. Also rejected: a camera push INSIDE the drawer through a coin tunnel (leaves the street, and burns 24 of 71 frames on one static reveal); a giant coin or piggy bank (money-about-money, and a big round object collides head-on with S11's scale one scene later); a rubber-stamped APPROVED (a text solution to a visual problem, colliding with S12's wax seal); and staging it at Claude's cart with owners queuing to hand him twenties (the cart is S3's base object, it puts his hands ON the money and kills the pockets gag at the one moment it pays, and it makes the scene about him receiving when the VO is entirely about the business not caring — whoever holds the money owns the frame).

---

### S11 · MATH — "THE BEAM THAT BECOMES THE HAND"
**LINE + DUR:** 35.10–38.05 (2.95s, 89 frames, lf 0-88). *"50 posts a month is $1,000, and it's an hour of your week at most."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** Fifty posts and a thousand dollars weigh EXACTLY the same — the trade is visibly, physically fair — and the clock says it cost him one day out of seven.

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE NICKEL-SILVER MARKET SCALE** — a monumental twin-pan balance on the pavement, shot from kerb level so it towers. Cast three-toed plinth (x391-621, y666-706), fluted column tapering 76w→46w (y666→y300), fulcrum knuckle d=56 at (506,292) with a bubble level and a pointer needle over a **three-tick index arc with NO numerals**, a 620×24 swan-neck beam (x196-816), 150-long triple chains, and two 260×46 **copper** dishes (left pan cx246, right pan cx766). Silhouette 780×412. ⛔ **Nickel silver `#DCE2E8`/`#8A96A4` with copper pans, NOT brass** (material ledger — an all-brass monument filling the panel is what destroyed the plaque's rarity). **Its beam is the only element that survives the tilt into the far plane, because the beam IS the transition.**

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, ~72% down the block — one shopfront right of the tills, on the near pavement outside the LAUNDROMAT (mint tile x0-330, two tumbling porthole drums) with the EMPTY LOT hoarding running right (x340-1012, alternating clay/amber/slate boards, torn poster corners, **a deliberate gap at x620-700**) and Peppi's neon clipped at the extreme left edge. The scale stands in the open market stretch. Across the road, cool and dull, Bloatcorp with Vito's own broken scale on the forecourt. **The CLOCK TOWER SHAFT is planted from frame 0 in the hoarding gap at x620-700 (visible y300-500) so the tilt pays off something already present** rather than parachuting in a new object. Near ground GY=706, deep lane y660, far pavement y596. Sun rakes from camera-right; string lights lit since S8. All background layers authored at `inset:-200`.

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-8 (A) LOW KERB DOLLY.** camZ 1.030→1.024, origin (506,602), horizon high at y~250 so we look UP at the beam. The scale stands with **both pans EMPTY and dead still** (beam level at y280, pans at y450). **BULK** (x250, groundY706, size286) performs a full powerlifting ritual over the 300×180 fifty-brick at (250,646): spits on both paws (two 4px specks arc away), CLAPS them together (14px chalk puff), plants a wide stance, compresses translateY+7/scaleY 0.95, three clay effort-arcs pop above his head. **COUNT FLOSSULA** (**x940**, groundY706, size236, flipped, cape folded, tiny hand mirror, banded cash brick) watches — **re-grounded from x790 per the occlusion fix.** The delivery van is already crossing; the drums tumble.
- **lf8-14 THE OVER-LIFT.** Bulk explodes upward and **the brick WEIGHS NOTHING.** It flies clean past his head to y230 by lf13 (416px in 5 frames = 83px/f, 300×180 = 54,000px² certified large mover), his arms fly up with it, he staggers back 20px, stern→shock 0.4. **The brick is the countable 50:** 5 stacked 36px bands × 10 card ends at 30px pitch, each end a different desaturated palette tint, kraft strap with a wax dot, top card fanned back 14° showing one abstract colour-block face (clay circle + two amber bars + paper block, **ZERO legible text at any zoom**).
- **lf14-24 THE THUMP + THE VAMPIRE FLINCH.** The brick arcs over and SLAMS into the left pan. Beam overshoots to -14° about the fulcrum (half-span 310 → left pan drops 75px to y525, right rises to y375), chains snap taut with visible whip, pan bounces at lf18/21/24, the column flexes 3px, two loose cards puff off and flutter. At lf18 the swinging metal throws a 90px three-point GLINT: **FLOSSULA RECOILS** (use 4 of 5 in the ledger order — the mirror beat), cape thrown across his own face, one step back x940→x972, **and his hand mirror spins out of his paw 520° to the pavement by lf23.** Sparky sleeps on under the deck chair. Across the road a forklift lowers a pallet onto Vito's scale.
- **lf24-30 (B) CUT — 6-frame CutWipe, own authored layout.** camZ 1.150→1.128, origin (526,333). Reframed tight at beam height: beam left90 top330 w832 h30 pivoting (506,345); left pan x84 y470 w300 h58; right pan x638 y470 w300 h58; column x448 y358 w116 running off the bottom. Only the crowns of Bulk's and Flossula's heads sit in the bottom corners. Scale still LEFT-HEAVY at -12°. Flossula steps to the right pan and — one last time — checks his fangs in a pocket mirror, pockets it, then drops the cash with the flourish of a vampire dropping a cape.
- **lf30-36 THE $1,000 LANDS.** The cash brick (200×100, 12 laminated green-tinted note edges) falls y300→470 at 28px/f; pan compresses scaleY 1→0.88→1 over 3 frames. **The CLAY paper band across it carries the scene's only numeral: $1,000, Fraunces 900 / 38px / INK.** The god-ray cuts down-left across the fulcrum with dust riding it.
- **lf36-46 DEAD LEVEL (the money frame).** Damped oscillation: -12 → +5 (the money overshoots) → -2.2 → +0.8 → 0.0. At lf44 the pointer drops onto the CENTRE tick and the bubble bead slides to dead centre in the same frame; two detents CLICK home; a 2-frame white-hot flash bursts on the fulcrum pin; two rings expand r20→90; the specular runs the full 832 of the beam outward along both arms. **The scale does not move again until lf62.**
- **lf46-52 THE VERDICT + PIDGEY.** Both heads TURN inward across the level beam (gaze 5 → -5 over 4 frames), then Bulk and Flossula give **ONE slow nod in perfect unison.** Fair deal, no words — the scene's only line of dialogue. Simultaneously **PIDGEY** lands on the RIGHT pan rim, tugs exactly one note out of the brick's edge (band and numeral untouched) and flies out top-right. **The beam does not move a millimetre.** Bulk, without looking, reaches up out of frame-bottom and slaps a replacement card onto the fifty-stack.
- **lf52-62 BATBRO ARRIVES.** camZ 1.128→1.150. **BATBRO** grapples in from frame-right and lands badly on the plinth (his 3rd of 4 appearances — the one that ESCALATES), x690→560, feet skidding, arms windmilling, and his cape hem flops UNDER the plinth base. He extends a paw for the handshake over the level beam. Bulk reaches. Paws 40px apart. Flossula leans in approvingly. At lf60 Batbro's own grapple RETRACTS on him — a 3-frame twang on the cord — and he is yanked backwards mid-handshake. **His paw misses Bulk's by 8px.**
- **lf62-70 (C) SNAP TILT + THE MATCH-MORPH.** No cut — the retracting grapple motivates the move. Content translates DOWN 300px in 8 frames (37px/f, the whole frame is the mover) with a short directional blur, camZ 1.150, origin (587,238). **The level beam HOLDS its exact screen position and orientation through the translate while the CLOCK TOWER dial arrives around it:** d=300 cream stone face, chrome bezel, bevel highlight, hairline crack, 12 ticks and **NO NUMERALS**, centred (726,200). Over lf66-70 the beam crossfades and retapers — 832→396 long, 30→10 at the tip, growing a counterweight tail — and a stubby hour hand fades in so the dial reads CLOCK, not pie. **The beam IS the minute hand.** Warm bloom ramp as the camera clears the roofline; 5-ghost lens flare at lf74.
- **⭐ lf70-80 THE HOUR, TOLD AS SEVEN COUNTABLE PLATES (fix — the wedge fraction is DELETED).** A 3.4° sweep inside a 51.4° wedge is a 9px arc on a phone and is not a mute read. Instead: **SEVEN identical nickel day-plates sit in an arc across the tower face** (the 12 ticks stay, still no numerals). **Six are dark slate; ONE lights warm clay** as the minute hand crosses it, over 6 frames, with the shadow sliver falling only across that one plate. At lf72-78 the dial face drops 30% in luminance and **ONLY the lit plate is lit, so the eye has exactly one target for 6 frames.** Seven objects, one lit — a stranger reads "one out of a week" instantly and countably, exactly as they read three empty slots in S1. No numeral is added and the beat gains a light event. **lf82 (moved from lf76 so it cannot compete with the plate): PIDGEY, still carrying the stolen note, lands on the minute hand, which sags 2° under him.** Far below, Canva is still face-down asleep on shop one's window.
- **lf80-88 THE SHADE, THE DRAG, THE HOOK OUT.** The clay light PEELS OFF the dial and hardens into a shadow bar (1140×150, rotated -9°, `rgba(24,18,40,0.42)` with a 6px warm-lit leading edge) raking down the tower and across the street at ~70px/f while the camera cranes back down (translateY -300 → -40, camZ 1.150→1.040) riding its edge. **It lands across the foreground deck chair, revealing YOU-CLAUDE (x300, groundY780, size230, cropped by the panel edge — cap, shades, cold drink, feet up), the SHRINK-WRAPPED tablet still a ring-stained coaster on the chair arm. He lifts one finger off the drink in a lazy salute and lets it drop. That was his hour.** Simultaneously the grapple snaps taut at lf84 and **the ENTIRE SCALE goes with Batbro: the group rotates 0→-24° and translates (+420,-680) in 5 frames (~78px/f, ~283,800px² bounding area), sparking off the kerb.** ⚠️ **Occlusion solved:** the vector is (+420,-680) not (+520,-560), so both pans clear y=300 before crossing x=700; Flossula is grounded at x940 and at lf84-86 **drops scaleY 1.0→0.72 with `transformOrigin: bottom`, cape over his face**, so the pass reads as him ducking under it; the departing group is authored at z=30, strictly above his z=14. Verify the swept bbox against his sprite box at lf84/86/88. **The $1,000 brick tumbles OUT of the pan mid-flight and flops back onto the pavement — the apparatus leaves, the deal stays.** Bulk's paw is still stuck out, unshaken; Sparky wakes and bolts x250→x1060. **Revealed in the bare gap where the plinth stood: the first PARKING METER of the row, flag still RED — the plant for S12.**

**CAMERA:** three distinct moves. (A) LOW KERB DOLLY at pavement level — camZ 1.030→1.012, origin (506,602), 2px handheld sin drift. **First time in the reel the camera looks UP at a hero object.** (B) BEAM MACRO, own layout, hard 6-frame CutWipe in at lf24: camZ 1.150→1.090, origin (526,333) — a settling pull rather than a push, so DEAD LEVEL lands calm. (C) SNAP TILT UP then CRANE BACK DOWN, **motivated entirely by Batbro's grapple firing into frame**: translateY 0→-300 over lf62-70 (37px/f, out-cubic), hold to lf80, then -300→-40 over lf80-88 riding the shadow bar's leading edge down; camZ 1.150→1.040, origin (587,238). **Bleed solved backwards at peak camZ, not on a static frame:** Shot A @1.030/origin506 → band x[16,996] y[18,786] (scale right pan edge 896 ✓). Shot B @1.150/origin(526,333) → x[70,946] y[45,730] (macro beam 90-922 ✓, $1,000 band 700-900 ✓). Shot C @1.150/origin(587,238) → x[78,954] y[32,718] (clock dial 576-876, top 50 ✓). **Sample the overflow audit at lf30 and lf64.**

**LIGHT:** hard low golden key from camera-RIGHT (`#FFE2A6`) with a cool sky fill (`#8FB0D0`) on every left-facing plane — **the two-source contrast is what makes nickel read as round metal rather than a flat shape.** Every shadow lays LEFT across clay-warmed tarmac (`#6A5F5A` + clay wash, never neutral grey). A long travelling specular runs the beam and moves outward along the arms with every swing; a 26px radial bloom sits at each hot point. SHOT B adds a volumetric god-ray cutting down-left across the fulcrum (200px `rgba(231,178,76,0.14)` at -22°) with dust motes, and a 2-frame white-hot flash on the fulcrum pin at the level clunk. **SHOT C is a deliberate cool-to-warm REVERSAL:** the pavement at lf60 sits in the buildings' shadow (slate), and as the camera rises into the sunline at lf68 the frame floods gold, peaking on a 5-ghost flare off the tower bezel at lf74 — then drops the dial 30% at lf72-78 so the ONE lit day-plate owns the frame. Across the road Bloatcorp is deliberately cool, dim and low-contrast — **the temperature split is the argument.** Warm Pools under the market awning, the laundromat portholes and the cubicle grid; string lights glow as warm bokeh along the near roofline.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P0 FG blur 5-7px 1.6× (parked scooter rear wheel + mudguard bottom-left, cast-iron bollard bottom-right, market-awning corner sliver top-left; drift ~7px/f) · P1 HERO z20 (the scale — plinth, fluted column, fulcrum knuckle with bubble level and three-tick arc, 620×24 beam, triple chains, two copper dishes — plus the 300×180 fifty-brick and the 200×100 $1,000 brick) · P2 street actors near ground GY=706 (**BULK x250 size286 shadow w206; FLOSSULA x940 size236 shadow w170**; BATBRO entering from the right edge on a 32° line from lf52; PIDGEY on the right pan rim; a gawping pedestrian with a shopping bag crossing the deep lane at groundY660 size108 BEHIND the column) · P3 shopfronts z6-9 (the LAUNDROMAT with two d=110 portholes tumbling all 88 frames, the empty-lot hoarding with **the deliberate gap at x620-700 for god-rays and the tower shaft**, Peppi's neon clipped left; across the road, cool and desaturated, Bloatcorp with its 6×3 grid, one grey designer face-down, revolving door, Vito's own scale + forklift in the far-left sixth) · P4 far z2-4 (hazy gold skyline, stilted water tower at x130, **and the CLOCK TOWER SHAFT planted from frame 0**; dial authored off-panel at (726,-100) and brought in only by the tilt) · P5 atmosphere (three god-ray parallelograms at -18° falling through the hoarding gap across the chains, dust motes at 0.4px/f; a 340×140 cream+clay delivery van crossing x-340→1080 over lf0-34; the shadow bar in the last 10 frames).

**ESCALATION:** S10 was lateral and horizontal — four tills chunking open in depth, money simply ARRIVING, uncounted. **S11 turns the panel vertical and monumental and asks the only question that can follow it: is that fair?** The camera drops to pavement level and looks UP at the biggest object on the block so far, and the money stops arriving and starts being **WEIGHED** — the first scene where the output is proved rather than shown. The machine escalates from "it pays" to "it pays PROVABLY", and the scene ends higher than it started twice: the dead-level clunk, then the entire proving apparatus getting ripped off the kerb at 78px/f while the money it proved stays on the pavement. The does-less ladder hits its lowest rung yet — he never stands up, never touches the cash, and contributes one raised finger. And **the camera itself escalates: this is the first move that leaves the street plane entirely, pre-loading S13's crane above the roofline.**

**GAG:** **THE DEADLIFT** — Bulk performs a full powerlifting ritual (spit, chalk clap, wide stance, three effort-arcs, compression) for an object that weighs nothing, and OVER-LIFTS it clean over his own head, staggering back with his arms flying up. **Maximum effort, trivial load: that mismatch IS "an hour of your week at most", told with a body instead of a figure.** **THE DRAG** — Batbro grapples in for the closing handshake, lands badly, his cape hem flops UNDER the plinth, and his own grapple retracts on him: he is yanked out of frame **taking THE ENTIRE SCALE with him**, missing Bulk's paw by 8px and leaving Bulk standing with his hand out. The $1,000 brick tumbles out mid-flight and flops back onto the pavement: **the apparatus leaves, the deal stays.** RUNNING BEATS: Flossula recoils from the beam glint mid-thump and drops his hand mirror (520° spin to the pavement); Pidgey lands on the pan rim at the exact moment of level, tugs one note out of the fifty-brick and leaves, **and the beam does not move a millimetre**; and far down the block Captain Canva is asleep face-down on shop one's window, ruler still gripped — the only man still designing post number one, motionless while a scale weighs fifty.

**LIVING WORLD:** both porthole drums tumble across all 88 frames (free perpetual motion, no cue needed, and it guarantees no bucket goes static). A 340×140 delivery van crosses lf0-34. A pedestrian with a shopping bag slows to gawp at the scale. Torn poster corners flutter; the market awning canvas breathes. Bloatcorp's revolving door turns with nobody in it and one designer stays face-down all scene. Across the road at lf18 a forklift lowers a pallet of paperwork onto Vito's own scale and at lf26 his side SLAMS to the road and **catapults a 90px grey intern up into the market awning, where he sticks with his legs swinging for the rest of the scene** — ribbon ledger **460px**, and he stays in the far-left deep plane. Canva asleep face-down against a lit window (x44, groundY592, size84), cape rising and falling, T-square sliding 1px/f down the glass; the tilt simply leaves him there and he never wakes. Two loose cards flutter down after the thump. Sparky sleeps curled under the deck chair until lf84, then bolts x250→x1060 after the departing scale.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **$1,000** — one numeral, Fraunces 900 / 38px / INK, on the clay band wrapping the cash brick. Nothing else. **The 50 is a countable 5×10 grid of printed card ends** and is never written as a figure. **The hour is SEVEN day-plates with ONE lit** — the dial is deliberately built with 12 ticks and NO NUMERALS precisely so a second numeral cannot leak in under build pressure. The index arc has three ticks and no numerals. Post faces are abstract colour blocks with zero legible text at any zoom. Shopfront marks are far-plane shapes only.

**MUTE CHECK:** a strongman theatrically deadlifts a small stack of printed cards and it flies over his head; it thumps into one pan of a giant market scale; a vampire drops a brick of banded cash into the other; the pans clunk DEAD LEVEL and the two of them nod at each other once; a caped guy grapples in for the handshake, gets yanked out and takes the entire scale with him while the money stays on the pavement; the camera rises and that same level beam becomes a town clock's minute hand, which crosses **one of seven plates and lights it**; the shadow it drops rakes down the street and lands across a man asleep in a deck chair, who raises one finger. ✓ "This much work equals this much money, it was fair, and it cost him one day in seven." The only thing the words add is the exact figure.

**TRANSITION IN:** match-cut on metal. S10's last till drawer chunks open on its rectangle of banknotes and its chrome trim; on the cut that trim wipes into the scale's fluted column at the same screen height, and the drawer's KA-CHING becomes the low swell under the dolly (S10's cascade tail is deliberately allowed to ring through). The left→right walk never breaks — one shopfront right, from the row of tills to the pavement outside the laundromat — and the clock tower shaft is already standing in the hoarding gap. Internally: one 6-frame CutWipe at lf24 timed to land on the pan's bounce; then **NO cut into Shot C** — the grapple fires and motivates the tilt, so the last third is one unbroken move.

**SFX:** emit as **L[10] + local** (L[10]=35.10) — a bare scene-local `at` typechecks, renders and is SILENT. lf2 spit_tick v0.22 · lf4 chalk_clap dry thud v0.34 (close, no reverb) · lf10 whoosh_rise v0.30 on the over-lift · lf18 THUD_HEAVY v0.48 + chain_rattle v0.32, with two softening bounce echoes at lf21/lf24 · lf19 metal_ring — **do NOT clamp `dur`, this tail needs ≥90% of its true file length** · lf24 cutwipe_swish v0.24 · lf32 cash_fwump v0.36 · lf36-42 metal_seesaw_groan v0.26, two decaying cycles · **lf44 PING v0.52, a clean tuned tap — this is the scene's money sound and it must land in the measured VO gap after "$1,000,"** · lf48 pigeon_flap v0.22 + note_flick v0.18 · lf52 grapple_chunk v0.34 · lf60 line_twang v0.36 · lf62 riser_short v0.26 under the tilt · lf74 warm_chime v0.20 on the flare · **lf76 TICK_HEAVY v0.46 — EXACTLY ONE tick as the hand crosses the lit plate; never a ticking loop, the joke is that there is only one** · lf82 whoosh_wide v0.22 on the shadow rake · **lf84 metal_skid v0.38 with a long doppler-away rattle, dur+rel ≥ 1.2s — measure the source first, truncating this tail kills the landing** · lf86 paws v0.20. All ride the VO duck (1.0 in gaps → 0.38 on speech), ROOT-indexed, −12 to −17dB.

**WHY THIS:** "50 posts a month is $1,000" is not a number, it is an **EQUALS SIGN** — and the real-world object that means equals is a market balance. But a balance alone is a graphic, so the arithmetic is PERFORMED by the two people who have to believe it: casting Bulk as the loader puts the whole argument in one body (a strongman's full ritual for an object that weighs nothing is exactly what "an hour of your week at most" means, told through effort mismatch instead of a figure), and casting Flossula opposite him puts payer and worker on opposite pans, so the level beam is a two-character verdict and the unison nod is the scene's only dialogue. The beam→minute-hand match-morph is what makes the scene structural rather than decorative: it is the only way to say "an hour of your week" without a second numeral — and the seven-plates fix is what makes that claim actually READABLE, because countability is the reel's proven idiom (three empty slots, fifty card ends, nine meters) and a wedge fraction is not. It is also the first time an object crosses a parallax plane, which earns S13's crane above the roofline. Batbro's yank then solves three problems at once: it supplies the required ≥40,000px² mover in a small-prop bucket (283,800px² at 78px/f), it MOTIVATES the tilt so the camera move is caused rather than decreed, and it makes the point that the apparatus was never the asset — the scale leaves, the $1,000 stays, and the man who never stood up gets the last frame. It also keeps the three-nameplate cast physically together in one frame three scenes before S14 pays them off, and clears the kerb so the S12 meter is revealed rather than introduced.

**REJECTED:** staging it as a HAGGLE — Flossula lowballs, Bulk backs him up, the pans fight until they settle. It contradicts S10, which has just established this is "the number nobody argues with"; putting an argument on screen 2.4 seconds later un-sells the previous scene. It forces Claude into active negotiation, breaking both the does-less ladder and the hands-in-pockets law — and once he stands up, S13's hammock has nowhere left to go. And a contested balance means the beam oscillates for most of a 2.95s scene, burying the one beat that matters: the single clean clunk into level. Also dropped: a giant HOURGLASS (says TIME only, so it cannot carry the equation half of the VO and would have forced a second numeral; and "stuff pouring into a container" is already owned by S12's crates and S15's postbox); a nickel ABACUS (a counting frame can show 50 and can show $1,000 but cannot show they are the SAME THING, and a grid of small beads is exactly the small-motion failure the motion audit is blind to); and a wall-sized calculator or ledger, which is a text-and-digits card.

---

### S12 · RETAINER — "THE METER SEALS ITSELF SHUT"
**LINE + DUR:** 38.05–40.59 (2.54s, 76 frames, lf 0-75). *"Then you stop selling posts, and you start selling monthly retainers."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** You stop feeding it coin by coin and pay once — the slot SEALS itself shut, a flower grows out of the machine, and then the whole street flips green on its own, faster than the camera can follow, while he never takes a hand out of his pockets.

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE KERBSIDE PARKING METER.** One object in two states, never two objects. SHOT A: a single monumental cast-iron/chrome meter head at macro — head x[236,766] y[96,540] (530×444 = 52% of panel width), post x[438,564] y[540,700], plinth x[410,592] y[700,726] — red enamel tongue up, draining sliver, open **copper** coin throat (⛔ not brass — material ledger). SHOT B: revealed as a receding row of **EIGHT** identical meters (**not nine — nine was never countable**), each converting to green flag + bolted throat + flower. Meter faces are **PICTORIAL ONLY** — red tongue / green flag / chevron. Never a digit, never a dial number.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST at golden hour, the laundromat / empty-lot stretch — the next block right of S11's scale, exactly where the walk has arrived. Fixed vertical stack: sky y[0,210] · facades y[210,612] · awning row y[400,470] · **PAVEMENT y=700** (every sprite and meter uses groundY 700, upstage 660, far kerb 596) · cream kerb stone y[700,726] with a hot gold sun-lit top edge · tarmac gutter y[726,792] in slate-tinted asphalt, never neutral grey. World left→right (translating -880px through the panel across SHOT B): the tail of PIZZA PEPPI's frontage w[300,560] with **CANVA asleep face-down on its glass at w300** — the LAUNDROMAT w[560,900], mint tile, three porthole drums turning, **HERO METER #1** at its kerb — the EMPTY LOT w[900,1180], chain-link fence, and seen THROUGH it in the deep plane **BLOATCORP**, one designer face-down asleep — a warm awning row w[1180,1520] where the clay cargo trike is parked at w1250 with four crates — an ALLEY MOUTH at w1520 throwing one hard god-ray — Vito's rusted decommissioned wall-meter at w1560 — last shopfronts to w1900, and above the roofline at w1850 **the bottom-left scaffold corner of the GURU BILLBOARD**, sliding into the top-right at lf70 as the hook into S13. ⛔ **The closed roller shutter is NOT in this scene at any point** (S1 and S14 own it).

**SHOT LIST**

He does not look up. That is the entire amount of work he does in this scene.**

Each conversion is the same 4-frame unit (tongue flip, endless-green snap, throat seal, flower sproing 3 frames later, green Pool).
