SHOT B: a **SNAP PULL-BACK** handing into the reel's only sustained **LATERAL TRACKING DOLLY** — camZ 1.42→1.00 then →1.045, origin `19% 66%` = (192,523), with the world div translating -880px. **The pull-back is what sells "it keeps going past what we can see": the frame opens up at exactly the moment the wave outruns it.** BLEED SOLVED BACKWARDS before any coordinate was authored, audited at PEAK camScale. SHOT A peak 1.090 about (466,349): content inside x[53,953] y[51,741] — head x[236,766] ✓, Bulk's fist to x830 ✓, coin apex (884,150) → 922/132 ✓. **SHOT B's single peak frame is lf38 at 1.42 about (192,523): x ≤ 758, y ≤ 701 — therefore ONLY meter #1 and its pool may be load-bearing on that frame; M2-M8, the trike, Peppi, Vito, the far kerb and the billboard corner all fade/parallax in from lf44 onward, by which point camZ ≤ 1.08 and the box has opened to x[52,960].** Final beat at 1.045: key content x ≤ 961.

**LIGHT:** golden hour, hard low key from CAMERA-RIGHT. Warm key on every right-facing surface, cool slate sky-bounce rim on every left-facing one — the post carries a 2px gold rim right, slate rim left; each dome gets a cream sheen crescent top-left and a hot 6px gold specular at its right shoulder. Every vertical throws a LONG shadow raking LEFT: each meter's is a sheared quad 210px at 22°, and because they all slide with the world **they give the pavement a continuously moving light-texture that carries the motion audit on its own.** Sky 178° `#2E4260`→`#6B7FA0`→`#C58D5A` at roofline → a hot band at y300; sun bloom disc at (905,96) r120 with three god-ray wedges. VOLUMETRIC beat: the alley shaft sweeping the frame at lf60-70. **THE SCENE INVENTS ITS OWN SECOND LIGHT SOURCE:** before, the meter glows sickly red and throws a red pool; after, each conversion ignites a warm-edged green Pool at meter height, so across lf40-67 the kerb builds from one warm-only street to a continuous strip of eight green emissives — the street is measurably brighter and richer at the end than at the start, holding the S2→S15 warming curve. Deliberate cold contrast: **BLOATCORP's glass box behind the lot fence is the only cold thing on the street** (`#566274`, cyan cubicles), which makes the green read warmer. Final 6 frames: a hard-edged shadow from off-frame top-right desaturates the flower row 32% — the billboard arriving.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P1 FG 1.35× blur 5-6px silhouette **at 0.85 opacity minimum** (tarmac gutter y[726,792]; bottom-left a parked scooter rear wheel, mudguard and a clay planter that Sparky bursts out from behind; bottom-right the hydrant shoulder plus Sparky's snapping jaw in SHOT A; a striped awning valance corner intruding top-right and sliding left at 11px/f; a paper bag tumbling in the gutter; a dark vignette bar y[762,792]) · P2 HERO 1.00× (meter #1 in macro, then the row of **eight** converted meters along the kerb edge at scale 1.00→0.42, head bottoms all inside y[498,540]; plus the cream kerb stone with its gold top edge, every left-raking shadow, every green Pool and every flower; **this plane holds 54%+ of the frame in both shots**) · P3 street actors 1.00× / upstage 0.94× (**Bulk's forearm entering right; YOU-Claude sitting hands-in-pockets against meter #4's post, size 150; Sparky galloping the near tarmac at 47px/f authored in SCREEN space so he is a character not scenery; Peppi and his crate.** Size-ramped hero 190 → mid 148 → upstage 108; spacing ≥0.85×(rA+rB); contact shadows WIDER than each sprite) · P4 shopfronts 0.82× (Peppi's frontage with **CANVA asleep face-down on its glass — a green flag pops beside his ear at lf46 and he does not wake, just a snore-puff, then he slides off the left edge at lf50**; the laundromat's three drums turning with one red sock; the lot hoarding; the awning row; two catenary string-light swags with a bulb every 46px swaying ±3px) · P5 far side 0.55× desaturated 40% (a driverless cargo trike of four wax-sealed crates rolling the far carriageway at 10.5px/f; **BLOATCORP seen THROUGH the lot fence, one designer face-down asleep**; Vito + Pidgey on the far kerb) · P6 sky 0.18× (the golden gradient, the sun bloom, hazy skyline silhouettes **colour-mixed toward the sky rather than alpha-faded**, three cloud slabs, and — a deliberate callback — S11's clock tower still small at x[836,904], the object we just cut away from, now receding behind us).

**ESCALATION:** S11 was EQUILIBRIUM: one client weighed once on a static balance, everything settling dead level and stopping, the biggest camera move a tilt. **S12 inverts the physics — one input triggers a RUNAWAY CHAIN that accelerates until it physically outruns the camera.** The escalation is measurable, not asserted: the flip gaps shrink 4,4,3,3, the front hits ~3.5× camera speed, and the last conversions happen off-panel as flashes we only catch peripherally. It is also **the reel's first IRREVERSIBLE state** — every earlier win could be undone (a price re-tagged, a till closed, a scale re-tipped) but the coin throat is physically bolted shut and a flower is growing out of it. It covers more literal street in 2.54s than any scene before it, so the camera-as-progress-bar jumps. And the does-less ladder steps up on schedule: S11 he was in a deck chair watching; S12 he does not even pay — Bulk seats the plate — and he sits on the pavement with both hands in his pockets and does not look up when a flower erupts over his head. **The street has now saturated at ground level, which is precisely why S13 must go up to the roofline to find something bigger to destroy.**

**GAG:** **BULK TRIES TO FEED A FLAT PLATE IN EDGEWAYS LIKE A COIN, THREE TIMES, THEN SHOULDER-BARGES THE WHOLE METER — seating it by accident and leaving his meter crooked at 6° forever** while every other meter on the block flips green dead upright. Payoff gag: the sealed meter sprouts a FLOWER and one bee arrives thrilled; then the pull-back reveals EIGHT flowering meters igniting outward and **the bee stalls dead in the air, spins once, and cannot choose.** Contrast gag across the road, in his dedicated lane: **VITO** — eleven scenes of producing invoices — is on BOTH KNEES using a rolled invoice as a **FUNNEL**, pouring coins two-handed into a rusted meter that will never turn green, ribbon snaking off the right edge with no end, **while PIDGEY in a tiny traffic-warden cap tickets him and he cannot stop feeding it long enough to look up.** Third: **PIZZA PEPPI**, refused all reel, climbs INTO the MONTHLY crate to post himself and gets wax-stamped on the shell, legs kicking happily. Fourth, quiet: a green flag CLACKS on right beside Captain Canva's ear and **he sleeps straight through it**, then slides off the left edge — one shop behind, forever.

**LIVING WORLD:** the laundromat's middle drum spins the entire scene with one red sock tumbling in it (circular motion rhyming with the meter domes, guaranteeing no bucket goes static). Peppi's neon flickers once. String lights sway ±3px with a warm pool each. Tarmac centre-line dashes slide at 12px/f. A driverless trike of four wax-sealed crates rolls the far carriageway. Every flower keeps wobbling after it pops. A gust at lf64 ripples the full length of Vito's ribbon. Pidgey is working a second job as a traffic warden. Sparky sprints the whole panel chasing a light he cannot catch. Behind the lot fence one Bloatcorp designer is face-down asleep, as always. A paper bag tumbles in the gutter. One bee has a small personal crisis. **S11's clock tower is still on the skyline behind us, receding.**

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **MONTHLY — exactly ONCE**, debossed on Bulk's copper pass plate in SHOT A macro (⛔ the crate stencil is DELETED per the one-label rule; the other crates carry a wax-seal blob and a chevron). **NO NUMERALS ANYWHERE** — meter faces are a red tongue and a green flag with a chevron, never a dial with digits, never the word EXPIRED. Spine containment holds absolutely. Quantity here is COUNTABLE (eight meters), permanence is a SEALED slot, growth is a FLOWER. Shop signage is far-plane, small, part-cropped, and reads as shape.

**MUTE CHECK:** a coin is dropped into a parking meter and a small printed card falls out of its side; three times. Then a coin is flicked OUT and a brass card is pushed IN — with a shoulder, because it won't fit. The red tab turns over to green, shutters slide shut across the coin slot so nothing can ever be fed into it again, and a flower grows out of the top. Pull back: eight meters down the street do the same thing **spreading outward from the middle** until the whole kerb is green and flowering, a corgi sprinting after a light he can't catch, while across the road one man kneels pouring a fistful of coins into a meter that stays red, and a pigeon in a warden cap tickets him. ✓ **"He paid once and now it's on permanently, everywhere — that guy is still paying by the coin."** The two states are distinguishable on COLOUR ALONE at thumbnail scale, which is what makes the wave legible at 0.42 down the block.

**TRANSITION IN:** **MATCH CUT ON A CIRCLE**, no wipe. S11 ends on a snap tilt UP to the clock tower's pale cream face — a lit disc with a dark needle, upper-centre. S12 opens on meter #1's cream dial glass at the same screen position (centre (501,268), d250) and roughly the same diameter, re-lit warm from the right, with its own dark needle already draining. **One rented-time dial becomes another rented-time dial, and the theme is done for free before a word lands.** A wipe would kill the match, so the join is a 5-frame warm exposure bloom reading as a lens adjustment. ROBUSTNESS: if S11 leaves its clock centred at 506 rather than 501 it still matches; if it drifts further, add a 6-frame transformOrigin drift (50%→46%) over lf0-6. **TRANSITION OUT: the frame ends LEVEL and wide with the billboard's scaffold corner intruding top-right as an OBJECT and a shadow, never as a tilt** — S13's heroic crane must remain the reel's first up-move.

**SFX:** emit as **L[11] + local** where **L[11] = 38.05** (a bare panel-local `at` here typechecks, renders and is SILENT). 0.00 low mechanical hum bed + a dry ratchet tick every 0.22s (the drain) · 0.07/0.23/0.40 three thin, tinny, deliberately unsatisfying coin pings, **each followed 3 frames later by a soft paper flutter as its card ejects**, plus a sprung-tongue tick · 0.60 bright spring PING + coin ring with a doppler tail on the eject (v0.28) · 0.93 coin clatter on tarmac + a wooden jaw SNAP (v0.22) · 1.00/1.10/1.20 three dry metallic SCRAPES as the plate refuses to go in edgeways (v0.18) · **1.23 heavy shoulder-BARGE thud + a copper CLUNK as the plate seats — MEASURE the source and give it dur+rel ≥ 90%, never cap it at 0.4s** · 1.27 cut whoosh (v0.20) plus a solid KA-CHUNK and a bright ascending chime for meter #1's morph (v0.34, the anchor hit) · 1.35 soft organic sproing for the flower (v0.16) · 1.47 a soft whoosh riding the green light band · **1.47-2.23 SEVEN more KA-CHUNKs on the outward wave, emitted in PAIRS panned symmetrically L and R from centre and pitched up ~1 semitone per pair, so the ear hears the propagation spreading from the middle rather than sweeping across** (v0.26→0.34) · 1.60 trike bell ting · 1.67 paw scrabble, 2.00 one happy bark · **2.05 warm sustained chord swell as the row lands green — let this ring under the button** · 2.10 coin cascade on tarmac (Vito) · 2.27 ticket paper rip · 2.40 heavy WAX-STAMP THUNK with a wet squelch tail on Peppi's shell · 2.30/2.37/2.43 three off-panel clacks at −6dB with reverb to sell the block continuing past frame. **The VO runs almost continuously across these 2.54s so expect the duck near 0.38 throughout and set cue levels hot enough to survive it; only the 1.23 BARGE and the 1.27 KA-CHUNK should peek, and both must sit in the VO's measured micro-gaps — verify by RMS scan of the WAV, never by whisper word times.**

**WHY THIS:** "stop selling posts, start selling monthly retainers" is a change of STATE, and the parking meter is the only everyday object that already means "you paid, and the clock immediately starts running out." Converting it plays the whole VO as one continuous gesture: coin OUT on "stop", plate IN on "start", then four sub-morphs on a single body that the eye can follow without a cut. Three properties nothing else has. **It renders BOTH states in the same body, as pure colour, with zero numerals** — mandatory, since S12 sits between S11 and S14 and the spine forbids it a single digit. It is inherently RECURRING, so bolting one permanently is a visible violation of what everyone already knows the object does. And it exists natively as a ROW along a kerb, which hands the scene its wave, its two-shot structure and its guaranteed large movers for free (a 72,000px² light band at 45px/f, a ~330,000px² shopfront plane, Sparky crossing the full panel) — in the geography the world already runs on, built from street furniture the chassis already owns. **The coin→card ejection is the fix that makes the mute read true:** without it the audience sees money coming back rather than an exchange ending. Running the flip outward from mid-block is what keeps this from reading as S8 in green. It gates the how completely, and it hands S13 energy that is still accelerating.

**REJECTED:** a WALL CALENDAR whose highlighted day swells until it swallows the month grid while a drift of per-post receipts folds into one bound monthly ledger. The most literal transformation available, dying three times: (1) a calendar is a grid of numbers and a ledger is a page of words, so it fails the mute test the instant you strip the text — and S12 is explicitly barred from carrying any numeral, which leaves it nothing to say; (2) it is an interior paper object and would break the "camera never leaves the street" law that makes the walk function as the progress bar; (3) its silhouette is flat rectangular paper, colliding with S11's brick of printed posts and S7's fanned deck — three paper-stack heroes in five scenes. Also killed: a coin-op SUBSCRIPTION VENDING MACHINE (same base silhouette as S4's claw cabinet, and a vending machine says "buy a thing", not "keep paying", so recurrence would have to be carried by a counter, i.e. a numeral); a season-pass TURNSTILE (a turnstile lets people IN, which is S14's door, and pre-spends the payoff); and staging the contrast as Claude and Vito face-to-face at the same meter (forces Vito into the hero's arc and out of his dedicated lane, breaking the no-occlusion discipline that has to hold across his ~12 appearances). ⚠️ **BUILD WATCH-OUT:** if the green wave still reads as an S8 repeat in the wireframe, change the direction of attention further (start it at meter 5 and let the LEFT branch run visibly faster than the right) rather than softening the clack.

### S13 · TWIST — "HIS ARM BECOMES THE ARROW"
**LINE + DUR:** 40.59–43.58 (2.99s, 90 frames, lf 0-89). *"And here's what the gurus won't tell you. Making the posts is the easiest part."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** The guru's pitch is a 0.4mm sheet of vinyl stretched over a wall — peel it and his outstretched pointing arm literally BECOMES a faded painted arrow that was there first, pointing down the block at the one door still shut.

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE ROOFTOP GURU BILLBOARD** — glossy vinyl on a rusted steel frame bolted to a brick gable. Vinyl FACE x104..904 y132..504 (800×372 = 297,600px²); rusted box-section frame x88..920 y116..520; 12 **galvanised-steel** grommets on turnbuckles (⛔ not brass); grated catwalk x88..924 y504..528; 5-head floodlight bar x150..870 y96..116; rung ladder x78..104 y120..640. Full assembly ≈ 836×524 = 438,064px² = 55% of the panel. **It is the hero in ALL THREE of its states — intact SKIN, airborne SAIL, bare BRICK-AND-ARROW — so the camera never has to cut away from its own morph.** The reel's ONLY destroyed object and its only above-roofline object.

**SETTING:** MAPLE ST, golden hour, ~86% down the block — one step right of S12's meters, one step short of S14's shuttered door. **For the first and ONLY time in the reel the camera leaves street level:** the brick gable flank above PIZZA PEPPI and the laundromat, three storeys up. Low sun authored at (930,118), bloomed, its edge wobbling in the air-con unit's heat shimmer; every shadow falls left. Below: pavement ground y726, kerb y742, road y742..792. Far right at the end of the block, the small dark shuttered frontage x800..960 / y600..726 with its **brass plaque x856..888 / y588..604** — unlit, the only thing on the street the sun never reaches. Across the road far-left, Bloatcorp's grey glass box with its lit grid. Chassis idiom throughout: absolute layered divs, BRICK/PLASTER repeating-linear-gradients, an overflow-hidden shrinking wipe layer for the peel, a gradient curl tube, warm Pools, `<Actor>` grounding. ⛔ **Because SHOT C1 drops camZ to 0.92 with camY -120, every bleeding background plane must be authored out to x[-90,1105] y[-90,995] or the pull-back exposes an edge.**

**SHOT LIST**

A 260×372 hot specular glare band slides down the sheet at 9px/f and throws a warm bounce onto the roof deck. 4 real white doves orbit the top rail. **NO WORDS ON THE VINYL EVER.**

Glossy becomes matte in the same beat that the lie becomes the truth.** Three god-ray wedges cut the alley gap at 22° with rooftop dust; the sail's shadow sweeps the whole street in C1 as one huge travelling darkness. A full-panel warm flash is permitted at lf56 **capped at opacity 0.44 for 3 frames** — bloom, never a white-out. The shuttered frontage is the single element the sun never reaches, except the brass plaque, which glints. ⛔ **NO NEUTRAL GREY ANYWHERE:** every "empty" fill is brick, oxide, clay or slate.

**DEPTH PLANES:** P1 FG blur 5-6px 1.35× near-black with a warm right rim (a taut power cable arcing (-70,96)→(506,148)→(1080,84) with two pigeon silhouettes; a TV-aerial silhouette bottom-left; **and for the first 11 frames only, the underside of the occupied HAMMOCK with one dangling paw, the shrink-wrapped tablet coaster and a ring-stained cup**) · P2 HERO 1.0× (the billboard face in its three states — glossy SKIN, the 26→92px CURL and airborne SAIL, then BARE BRICK carrying the painted arrow (shaft (200,246)→(784,272); head tip (846,276), wings (782,238)/(782,314)) and the crude painted supercar x150..330 y300..400 with its raised paint edge and drip runs) · P3 structure/mid 0.9× revealed only after the tear (the 22px rusted I-beam frame with cross-members at x270/504/738, the catwalk with handrail, the ladder, the brick gable x40..960 y90..640 with damp stains and half-peeled paste-up remnants, the parapet, air-con unit, a slowly rotating satellite dish, pigeon coop, a forgotten deckchair) · P4 street actors 0.75× grounded with `<Actor>` (VITO x210 groundY726 size150 with his 640px ribbon; SPARKY groundY770 size118; two backlit pedestrian silhouettes; the cart and a queue silhouette far right) · P5 far 0.45× (**Maple St rooftops stepping DOWN to the right in five boxes (heights 190→90) so the eye is led toward the shutter**; the clock tower with its minute hand still barely moved; Bloatcorp's lit grid far-left with one always face-down; the alley god-rays; hazy skyline) · P6 sky 0.15× (dusk-gold gradient, three warm clouds, one contrail, the sun disc).

**ESCALATION:** S12 was a kerb-height wave of 40px meter flags with everything going right — camera locked to the pavement, and every scene for twelve scenes has BUILT something. S13 breaks all of it at once and stacks four escalations: **AXIS** (horizontal → vertical for the first time in 13 scenes, and the camera leaves the ground for the only time in the reel), **SCALE** (a 40px meter flag → a 297,600px² vinyl sheet, the largest object in the video), **CONSEQUENCE** (the first and only DESTRUCTION — every prior scene added something to the street, this one tears something off it), and **SPEED** (the sail is 211,200px² at 25px/f with a street-wide sweeping shadow, the fastest large mover in the reel). Tone flips too: twelve scenes of the machine getting better, then the first scene that says the machine was never the point. It also escalates by SUBTRACTION on the hero side — Claude is asleep in the blurred FG for 0.37s and then absent entirely; **the world argues his case while he sleeps**, which is the top rung of the does-less ladder and exactly what makes S14's hard cut to him knocking in the rain read as a real reversal. And Vito's running gag hits its terminal state: the ribbon that has grown every scene finally trips him. Intensity 8→9. **This is the spike.**

**GAG:** **GURU MAXX SAILS AWAY DOWN MAPLE ST CLINGING TO HIS OWN TORN BILLBOARD — BUT THE SUPERCAR STAYS ON THE WALL, BECAUSE IT WAS ONLY EVER PAINT.** Flat, wonky, house-paint red, wheels as black circles, its own painted shadow, a peeling rental sticker curling off the rear quarter, still parked there on the brick while the camera slides absurdly past it. **It is a reaction shot performed by an object.** Second beat, one frame later: **the DOVES, being real, simply leave** — up-right in tidy indifferent formation while the man screams. **The only things that follow Guru Maxx off-frame are the things that were never real.** Third, riding underneath: VITO trips over his own invoice ribbon, is SLAPPED flat under the vinyl into a lump with one polished shoe out, and pops an invoice open as a parachute a full second late — then a gloved hand emerges, gives a small flat resigned thumbs-up, and withdraws.

**LIVING WORLD:** PIDGEY in his traffic-warden cap is the one who yanks the corner and starts the entire disaster, then flaps off unbothered — and lands on the arrow at the end to point at the answer. Four doves orbit and exit in indifferent formation; two more pigeons bail out of the coop and the door swings. The air-con unit rattles and its heat shimmer wobbles the sun's edge. The satellite dish rotates slowly through the whole scene. String lights swing hard in the sail's wake and the laundromat neon stutters as the shadow crosses it, then steadies. SPARKY chases the sail's SHADOW rather than the sail and skids to a stop with a dust plume. **CAPTAIN CANVA is one shop behind as always — only a cyan cape corner flutters up from behind the far-left parapet and gets sucked along after the sheet.** Across the road one designer is face-down asleep and never moves, while on the final beat exactly ONE head lifts and turns toward the shutter. The clock tower's minute hand has still barely moved.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **NONE** — zero words and zero numerals anywhere. The captions already speak "gurus", so putting GURU on the vinyl would repeat them; **the guru is identified purely by iconography** — aviators, open shirt, gold chain, arms-wide pose, painted sunburst rays, supercar, doves. The vinyl's "wordmark" is a wordless gold squiggle-signature and a knockoff dollar-crest emblem; the rental sticker is a wordless barcode-and-key-tag; the brass plaque never carries text in any scene. Spine containment holds perfectly — not one figure leaks into S13.

**MUTE CHECK:** a huge glossy poster of a smug rich guy peeling off a wall in one sweep; his outstretched pointing arm turning, **in the same pixels**, into an old faded painted arrow; the sheet tearing free and sailing off down the street with him still clinging to it while his supercar stays stuck flat on the brick; a pinstriped man flattened into a lump with one shoe out and a tiny parachute opening far too late; and the arrow pointing straight down the block at the one door that is dark, shut and bolted. ✓ Read in well under 2s with no text. **The three empty slots are small, in shadow, countable, glinted once, and deliberately NOT resolved.**

**TRANSITION IN:** a **MATCH CUT ON A VERTICAL STEEL POST**, authored as lf0-4 of this scene so S12 loses nothing. S12 ends on the last parking meter's pole at screen x~300 with a warm right-side rim; S13 opens on the billboard's ladder rail at the same x, the same 26px thickness, the same rim — then the camera rises off it and the world drops away in 4 frames of vertical motion blur. The street stays geographically continuous, the camera has still only ever travelled left to right, and the crane simply continues the motion the cut started, so there is no dead frame. Audio bridge: S12's final meter ding pitch-bends upward and dissolves into the rooftop wind riser.

**SFX:** ⛔ **BUILDER TRAP: `<Sfx at>` is ROOT seconds. EVERY cue below is panel-local and MUST be emitted as L[12] + local, where L[12] = 40.59. Delete any inherited root cue map from the cloned chassis.** 0.00 rooftop wind bed + street murmur + a rising whoosh under the crane (v0.30, pitch-bridged from S12's ding) · 0.27 vinyl/flag flutter loop (v0.22) · 0.27 floodlight contactor thunk + ballast hum (dur 0.9, v0.30) · 0.60 pigeon flutter (v0.26) · 0.70 metallic grommet TINK (v0.34) · **0.73-1.73 THE PEEL — one long vinyl/velcro RIP pitching upward as the peel accelerates; MEASURE the library file before setting `dur`, this tail must not be chopped: dur+rel ≥ 90%, budget dur 1.2s minimum (v0.52 falling to a v0.30 tail). This is the scene's money cue.** Grommet POPs at 0.90/1.10/1.23 and a run of four at 1.73 (v0.44) · **1.03 one small bright gold CHIME on the arrowhead glint (v0.30) — the clue motif** · **1.80 huge canvas FWOOMP as the sheet unfurls + a sub-bass drop (v0.55, FULL TAIL, no dur cap)** · 1.87-2.27 doppler sail flap, a low whumping loop panning and pitch-dropping (v0.34) · 1.93 dove wing flurry, bright (v0.28) · 2.07 WHUMP — the flatten, heavy soft impact + dust-ring puff (v0.55, full tail) · 2.13 a tiny muffled squeak from under the lump (v0.24) · 2.23 comedy parachute POMF + a short sad slide-whistle (v0.26) · 2.27-2.40 whip swoosh (v0.36) · 2.60 low brick-dust reveal swell rising into the arrow ride (v0.28) · **2.80 one bright metallic GLINT ting on the plaque (v0.30) — SAME TIMBRE as the 1.03 chime, a fifth higher, so the arrowhead and the plaque are audibly the same clue.** ⛔ **PLACEMENT: the VO's only real gap is between "won't tell you." and "Making the posts" at roughly local 1.65-1.85s — the FWOOMP and the sub-drop land THERE, in real silence, and can run hot; everything else sits under the duck.** Onset density here (rip + fwoomp + doppler + whump inside 0.9s) is the highest in the reel, so the backing WILL measure hot; re-level against the VO after a solo WAV render, never as mix-minus.

**WHY THIS:** the transformation is answered literally and with ONE object: POSTER becomes ROLL becomes SAIL, and the negative space it vacates becomes the ARROW. The eye follows it unbroken for the full 2.99s because the camera never leaves the surface — **the three shots are three moves on the same morph, not three tableaux**, which is how a 2.99s scene gets three real camera moves without feeling cut up. The strongest frame is lf29-33, where the peel front crosses the guru's outstretched hand and the arrowhead appears in the identical 64px band at the identical angle and thickness: **his arm literally becomes the arrow, and what he was pointing at (a painted sparkle) becomes what it was always pointing at (a real shut door).** That single gesture converts the reel's biggest destruction beat into its open-loop re-arm — one gesture instead of two, which is what buys room for the sail, the flatten, the parachute and the ride in under three seconds. It earns the VO exactly: the sentence about gurus ends as the sheet lets go, and "making the posts is the easiest part" plays over a camera riding an arrow AWAY from the billboard toward the door. Load-bearing supports: the underside-through-the-lens sweep, which is simultaneously the best frame and the B→C transition, paid for once; the raised paint edge on the supercar so the joke has a physical reveal rather than a stated one; Claude's hammock for 0.37s then total absence, the top rung of the ladder and the setup that makes S14's rain reversal land; the four doves leaving because they are the only real things on the board; and the single Bloatcorp head turning as the seed of S14's unison turn.

**REJECTED:** hard-cut to Guru Maxx as a live sprite posing on his rooftop, then reveal the car is rented by peeling a sticker off the windscreen. (1) It makes a CHARACTER the base object, failing the 15-distinct-silhouettes crop test against S14's crowd scene and S4's plush claw machine. (2) Peeling a sticker is a ~4,000px² mover **in the reel's designated spike bucket** — the motion audit flags it instantly, where the billboard sail delivers 211,200px² at 25px/f plus a street-wide travelling shadow. (3) It spends the peak's intensity on mocking the guru rather than on re-arming the open loop, leaving S14's payoff unpointed. (4) "The supercar is rented" is the single most-run anti-guru beat in this format and dies on the rerun test by itself. Also rejected: a toppling GURU STATUE — a statue has no INSIDE, so nothing is revealed when it falls and the arrow would have to be bolted on as an unmotivated element instead of being the thing the object was covering; and it reads as "cancel the guru" rather than "the promise was a skin". Also rejected: VITO yanking the billboard down as deliberate sabotage — motivated, but it makes Vito competent for the first and only time, which flattens his S14 ejection from the revolving door. **The pigeon keeps the destruction accidental, physical and funnier, and lets the same pigeon land on the arrow at the end.**

---

### S14 · GRIND — "THE THREE SLOTS WERE ALWAYS THE FIRST THREE CLIENTS" · **THE PEAK**
**LINE + DUR:** 43.58–48.04 (4.46s, 134 frames, lf 0-133). *"Getting the first three clients is a couple of weeks of messaging local businesses. After that, they start referring each other."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** The three empty slots were always the first three clients. **The machine was never the lock — the DOOR was**, and it only opens after two weeks of getting your paper handed back to you in the rain. Once it opens it never has to be opened again.

**HERO OBJECT:** **THE BRASS PLAQUE WITH THREE EMPTY SLOTS**, bolted above the roller shutter at the dead end of Maple St. **Brass is legal here** (ledger — this is the object the whole ledger exists to protect). Panel-local: backplate left 126, top 96, w 760, h 330, radius 6, milled brass `linear-gradient(168deg,#E8C878,#B98E38 46%,#8A6420)`, verdigris wash, four countersunk screws r16 at the corners. **THREE RECESSED SLOTS: left 176, w 660, h 84, tops 126 / 226 / 326** — each `linear-gradient(180deg,#38290C,#12100A)` with `inset 0 6px 12px rgba(0,0,0,0.78)` and a bright bottom lip `inset 0 -3px 0 rgba(247,226,168,0.30)`, plus two retaining clips per slot so the eye reads "a plate slides in here". Hero ASSEMBLY = plaque + 3-bolt rail (y448; bolts 110×44 at x 206/506/806) + shutter head. 33% of panel at rest, **58% at peak camZ 1.18.**

**SETTING:** the far-right dead end of MAPLE ST — the closed, unlit roller shutter S1 planted and S13's arrow just pointed at. **SHOT A is the SAME geography two weeks earlier in rain:** identical brick, a row of twelve shut roller doors (190w on a 236 pitch, groundY 700), wet tarmac carrying smeared reflections, world graded `filter: saturate(0.22) brightness(0.72) contrast(1.06)`, **and the camera running the street BACKWARDS.** SHOTS B–C are the present: shutter left 132 / top 508 / w 748 / h 260, ten 22px slats, drum housing at y486. Behind it, a warm lamp-lit BACK ROOM — one round table (ellipse cx 506, cy 618, 300×92), a swinging pendant cone from y330, steam off three cups. Across the road in the far-left dedicated lane (x0-170, z8) sits BLOATCORP with its 30-sprite grid and revolving door. **Golden hour is physically restored across the scene as a `saturate()` ramp 0.22 → 1.00, quantised to the three clacks.**

**SHOT LIST — budget: A rain lf0-38 (1.27s) / B clack-and-reveal lf39-92 (1.80s) / C cascade lf93-133 (1.37s). ⭐ Rain compressed from 1.50s per the fix; the recovered 6 frames go to the shutter-reveal hold, which is the most important hold in the reel.**
- **lf0-4 THE CUT.** Match-cut on S13's painted arrow TIP: the golden brick under the arrow point is swapped in ONE frame for the rain-slick edge of a shuttered door at the identical x/y. Two-frame cold-blue flash `rgba(176,200,224,0.35)`, then the world locks to `saturate(0.22) brightness(0.72)`. Rain wall: 90 streaks 2×46px falling 26px/f, three stacked `repeating-linear-gradient(74deg)` sheets scrolling 34/22/13 px/f for parallax, haze plane 0.18. FG plane: black umbrella dome silhouette x-40..260 y430..792, blur 3px, rain sheeting off its rim, **right edge stopping 20px clear of the hero.** ⭐ **CAMERA DIRECTION REVERSES for the only time in the reel — the world slides LEFT-TO-RIGHT at 9px/f — so the flashback reads as a rewind without one word.** camZ eases out of S13's push, 1.06→1.01.
- **lf4-15 DOOR 1, TWO KNOCKS.** YOU-CLAUDE holds at x430, groundY 700, size 240, **FLIPPED (walking against the travel)**, beanie + tote, soaked, shoulders down. Door A slides 250→655. lf6 and lf9: paw strikes, two concentric ripple rings on the corrugation (30→110px). lf10: he posts **FLYER #1** under the door — a wet paper rect 96×62, `#B9B3A6`, radius 5, one clay smudge. lf12: **THE LETTERBOX FLAP SNAPS SHUT ON IT** (flap rotates -70→0 in 2f) and the flyer sticks out bent; lf14 he tugs it free, now dog-eared. **RECT #1 IS TAGGED AND TRACKED FROM HERE.**
- **lf15-24 DOOR 2, ONE KNOCK.** Door B enters. **He knocks ONCE now** (lf19) — the effort is visibly draining, and that decay is the shot's whole arc. lf21: posts **FLYER #2**. lf23: a grey hand behind the glass shoves it straight back out into a puddle; 9 droplets burst radially, the flyer lands face-down in water. **RECT #2 tagged.** Sparky (soaked, ears flat, size 150, groundY 748) starts a full-panel crossing in the near lane. lf16: **PIDGEY** lands on Claude's shoulder, feathers flat, **sheltering under a tiny umbrella made from one of the rejected flyers — the only company on the street** (traffic-warden cap still on, carried from S12).
- **lf18-34 DOOR 3 + THE CALENDAR GUST (overlapped per the compression fix).** Door C: he does not knock at all, just posts **FLYER #3** at lf26 (**RECT #3 tagged**). lf28: door C's shutter **DROPS ANOTHER SIX INCHES — it gets more shut.** lf30: a small red diagonal CLOSED bar on the glass flips over by itself. **LARGE MOVER lf18-30 (started 10 frames earlier so the beats overlap):** the lamppost calendar (x872, y300, 150×200) is stripped by a gust — **14 tear-off pages rip loose in one go and blow left-to-right across the entire panel at 24px/f in a high lane y[130,280]** (never below 280, so nothing crosses the hero's head at y500). ~420,000px² travelling. **NO NUMERALS on any page: two weeks is fourteen countable sheets.** Sparky shakes off at lf32.
- **lf34-38 THE MORPH BEGINS (the bridge across the cut).** The three flyers peel off the wet tarmac and are lifted toward the lens, rising, rotating, scaling 1.0→2.2, water beading off them. At lf36 they cross a shaft of light and the LEADING EDGE of each starts to turn brass — a gold wipe runs 0%→18% across each rect. **THE CUT TO SHOT B HAPPENS ON THESE THREE RECTANGLES MID-FLIGHT so the eye carries them over it. Rejection is literally becoming the client.**
- **lf39-46 PAPER BECOMES BRASS + FOUR FRAMES OF NEAR-SILENCE.** SHOT B: snap MACRO PUSH into a lock-off, camZ 1.02→1.18 by lf64 with a 4px overshoot settle, origin (506,396), 3-frame motion-blur streak. The plaque fills the frame; **its three empty slots are readable and countable for the first time.** The three rects arrive from upper-right depth (x1090, y-60, scale 2.4, rot 40°) arcing toward their slots at ~60px/f. TRANSFORMATION, 7 frames each: gold wipe leading-edge to trailing-edge (18%→100%), paper fibre becomes milled brass, corners square off (radius 5→2), a bevel highlight appears, 6 water beads flick off each and evaporate to steam. **lf43-46: FOUR FRAMES OF NEAR-SILENCE before the first clack — the negative space is the punctuation and it is the single most important audio decision in the scene.**
- **lf47 CLACK 1 — MOLAR.** **COUNT FLOSSULA** rises in from bottom-left (x=250, groundY=690, size=210, cape, stern, fangs) and HAMMERS nameplate 1 home into slot 0 — **engraved MOLAR in relief (ivory crown + two roots, dark engrave line), image never a word.** Plate scale 1.15→1.00 over 3f, dust ring, 3-frame white spark ring, whole-panel shake ±5px for 4f. **Then he catches his own reflection in the polished brass, stops dead mid-gesture, and checks his fangs in it — recoiling from his own shine** (recoil use 5 of 5, the button). WORLD STATE STEPS: **saturate 0.22 → 0.48, rain density 100% → 66%.**
- **lf56 CLACK 2 — DUMBBELL.** **BULK** enters from the right at 24px/f (x=770, groundY=690, size=215, headphones, ripped vest) and SLAMS nameplate 2 into slot 1 — **and it ARRIVES ALREADY TAPED BACK TOGETHER**, a strip of tape across it fluttering, because Bulk rips everything. He over-tightens; **the brass rail visibly BENDS, and he bends it back with two fingers, sheepish.** Flossula flinches bodily away from him. Shake ±6px. **saturate 0.48 → 0.74, rain 66% → 33%.**
- **⭐ lf60-68 THE LIFT + CLACK 3 — PIZZA SLICE. THE PEAK FRAME OF THE REEL.** **PIZZA PEPPI** (size 195, apron + shell) can't reach slot 2 — he is the shortest on the street — and he jumps for it twice. **BULK PICKS HIM UP BY THE SHELL in one clean 8-frame lift, legs pedalling**, and holds him at slot height. Peppi presses his SLICE nameplate in with both hands, delicately, tongue out. **CLACK at lf68 (abs ≈ 46.05s ≈ 88.7% through the reel — the peak).** Flossula gives one slow, reluctant nod. Shake ±8px; **saturate snaps to 1.00 in ONE frame; the RAIN STOPS DEAD** (last streaks fall out of the bottom over 4f and convert to rising steam); a hard gold shaft enters from camera-right and a specular GLINT travels the full 760px of brass in 6 frames. **THIS IS THE POINT: the first time in the reel any character helps another, and it plays "they start referring each other" a full second before the VO says it.** Three filled slots, three clients, zero numerals.
- **lf71 / lf74 / lf77 THREE BOLTS THROW.** The three chromed deadbolts on the rail at y448 (x 206/506/806, 110×44) fire left to right, each retracting 44px into its keeper with a 7-spark burst and a dust puff. Camera eases back and tilts, camZ 1.18→1.12, translateY +58, bringing the shutter into frame. **The count is repeated in a second physical register — three slots, three plates, three bolts — so it cannot be missed at mute.**
- **lf78-92 THE SHUTTER RATTLES UP ON A MEETING ALREADY IN PROGRESS.** Ten 22px slats accordion into the drum; visible shutter height 260→30 over 9 frames at ~26px/f **with a 3px judder at 4Hz so it reads as rattling steel, not a wipe** — 748×260 = a 194,000px² mover. Warm amber FLOODS out in three hard volumetric shafts and **the WET PAVEMENT, which was the misery of shot A, becomes the mirror that carries the win.** Behind the door is NOT a shop: ⭐ **FLOSSULA, BULK and PEPPI are ALREADY SEATED around the round table — the same three whose nameplates just went in** (fix: Batbro is no longer in the room; the three slots and the three people are now the same three people). They are at **x=356 / 506 / 656, sizes 132 / 150 / 132, groundY staggered 656/650/656** (gaps 150px vs a 119.85px merge floor — 30px of headroom, and contact shadows at 1.15× each sprite width). They pass cards hand to hand in a continuous loop and **do not look up.** Sparky is asleep under the table using the tote of flyers as a pillow. **The door he knocked on for two weeks was never locked from the inside — the party had started without him.** ⭐ **HOLD lf85-92 (8 frames, extended with the compression savings), camera locked.**
- **lf93-102 (C) THE FOURTH.** BIG CRANE UP AND PULL BACK, camZ 1.12→0.84 with translateY 0→-70, origin (506,560), opening from one doorway to the whole block. **lf95: PIZZA PEPPI leans OUT of the doorway and hands a FOURTH nameplate — a HOUSE glyph — to BATBRO, who is outside.** ⭐ This closes S10's four-flag plant (molar / dumbbell / house / slice) and pays off the only owner who has been paying since S10 without a slot. **Referral is shown as an object physically changing hands — never an arrow, never a diagram.**
- **lf102-112 THE HANDOFF CHAIN ACCELERATES.** The fourth plate passes right along the block: handoffs at lf103, 105, 107, 108, 110, 111, 112 — **3-frame gaps compressing to 1-frame gaps until the chain stops reading as events and becomes a streak.** The same rectangle, still traceable, now multiplying. **lf106: BATBRO — his 4th and final appearance, and the SUBVERSION — does not grapple. He simply walks to the front of the forming queue at x760, and SPARKY body-checks him** (corgi crosses 400px in 8 frames at groundY 748) and he tumbles clean off frame-right, cape over his head.
- **lf112-128 ⭐ THE CASCADE — VERTICAL, TOP-DOWN (fix: the fourth L→R wave is deleted).** A solid front of ~170 nameplates and business cards (58×36 each, ~354,000px²) **arrives from ABOVE and stacks DOWN each facade**, matching the crane the scene is already doing, **confined to the band y[120,430] ONLY** — never below y430, so nothing crosses a sprite head (tallest head top = 507). They CLACK flat onto all six shopfronts, each facade filling over 6 frames staggered by i×2, **and the plates STACK UPWARD PAST THE TOP OF THE PANEL** (top row deliberately cut at y=0). lf118: **across the road, all 30 BLOATCORP sprites turn their heads 90° toward the shutter IN UNISON in a single 4-frame rotation — all except the one still face-down asleep, who never wakes, in any scene.** lf122: **the revolving door spins to a blur with defecting clients and FLINGS VITO out pinstripes-first onto the pavement**; he slides 200px on his face and his invoice ribbon — ledger **760px**, the longest of the reel — whips out and drapes a parked scooter.
- **lf126-133 THE GAG AND THE HOLD.** Far-left dedicated lane (x=105, groundY=700, size=145, z=10): **CAPTAIN CANVA finally holds up his ONE finished handmade post, beaming, arms fully extended, at the exact instant two hundred referral cards sweep past him.** lf130: ⭐ **a single card sticks flat to his face and he topples backwards stiff as a plank — authored on a DEDICATED LANE, breaking the cascade band at x300 and descending x300→236 / y430→470, with no other card below y430 in those six frames**, so the one card that must cross an active figure is the only thing in that airspace. lf131-133 held wide — the whole block gold and plated wall to wall, one last card landing on the plaque's top edge with a single glint sweep. Camera settles at camZ 0.84 and hands the fully-lit street straight to S15's postbox.

**CAMERA:** three distinct moves, one per shot, none reused from a neighbour. **A: LATERAL TRACKING RUN IN REVERSE** — the world slides left-to-right at 9px/f, **the opposite of every other scene in the reel, so the flashback reads as a rewind without a word**; camZ 1.06→1.01, 1.5px handheld jitter. **B: SNAP MACRO PUSH INTO A LOCK-OFF** — camZ 1.02→1.18 by lf64 with a 4px overshoot settle, origin (506,396), then **dead still while the plaque does all the work — the only true lock-off in the reel; it is the payoff object, so the camera stops moving and lets it land** — then an ease back/tilt to 1.12 + translateY +58 at lf71 to bring the shutter in. **C: BIG CRANE UP AND PULL BACK** — camZ 1.12→0.84 with translateY 0→-70, origin (506,560). **Bleed solved backwards at maxScale 1.18 about (506,396):** plaque left 126→57.6, right 886→954.4, top 96→42, bolt-rail bottom 492→509. All inside [16,996]; audited at PEAK camZ. Global safe zone holds: plaque right edge local 886 = global 920, inside the 940 line.

**LIGHT:** a three-act lighting story that IS the emotional arc, with the key quantised to the client count. **A:** flat cool overcast `#3E4756`, `saturate(0.22) brightness(0.72)`, **NO key — the only unlit scene in the reel** — one weak sodium pool under the lamppost buzzing and flickering twice, wet specular sheen on tarmac and corrugations, rain-haze at 0.18. **B: the key BUILDS IN THREE COUNTABLE STEPS driven by the clacks — saturate 0.22 → 0.48 (lf47) → 0.74 (lf56) → 1.00 (lf68) — so the colour of the world is literally paid for by the third client**; at lf68 a hard directional gold shaft enters from camera-right, the remaining rain converts to rising steam inside the beam (volumetric), and a 6-frame specular glint travels the full 760px of brass; at lf78 warm amber spills out of the opening shutter in three hard shafts and bounces off the still-wet pavement in a long trapezoid with visible dust. **C:** full golden hour, god-rays through the alley gaps, bloom on the new plated wall, warm bounce onto every sprite, **and a deliberate COOL GREY HOLDOUT on BLOATCORP so the defection reads.** Running continuously: a 210×330 skewed specular bar sweeping the plaque face x-240→1060 at 24px/f — **both the glint pass that makes the EMPTY slots readable and the guaranteed large mover during the locked-off macro.**

**DEPTH PLANES:** P0 FG silhouette z60 blur 3px fastest parallax (shot A the black umbrella dome x-40..260 y430..792 dripping a chain of drops, **right edge stopping 20px clear of the hero**; shot B a blurred bolt head bottom-left and a wet scooter wheel; shot C a lamppost shaft and an awning corner) · P1 HERO z40 (the three tagged rectangles → the plaque, its three slots, the three nameplates, the 3-bolt rail → the plated wall. **One traceable shape across all 134 frames**) · P2 street actors z24 (Claude 240 beanie+tote, Flossula 210, Bulk 215, Peppi 195, Batbro 190, Sparky 150, Canva 145, Pidgey — **max 5 at readable scale at once, and the five in the peak frame are named: Flossula, Bulk, Peppi, Sparky and Claude**; spacing ≥0.85×(rA+rB) enforced; contact shadows 1.12× wider than each sprite) · P3 shopfronts z12 (the twelve-door shut row on a 236 pitch in A, the roller shutter and drum housing in B, the six facades that take the cascade in C, the lamppost carrying the calendar pad) · P4 across the road z8, **dedicated far-left lane x0-170** (Bloatcorp's glass box, its 30-sprite grid rendered as a silhouetted depth plane not individual actors, the revolving door, and Vito — **Vito never leaves this lane**) · P5 far/sky (rain-haze gradient becoming a god-ray gradient, hazy golden skyline and the clock tower by C).

**ESCALATION:** S13 was the intensity SPIKE (9): the largest object in the reel torn free, with a painted arrow uncovered pointing at this door. S14 cannot out-shout that, so it steps to 10 by **REVERSING FIRST** — it is the only tonal drop in the back half and the only tonal reversal in the whole reel. Colour, warmth, the golden street, the machine and the money are all taken away in one hard cut to grey rain, and the hero visibly runs out of energy (two knocks, then one, then none). **That trough is what buys the climb, and after fourteen scenes of monotone ascent the reversal itself is the escalation.** Then it inverts S13's scale logic: S13's LARGEST object was destroyed; **S14's SMALLEST object — an 84px empty slot — is the one that pays off**, three times in twenty-one frames, with the saturation of the entire world stepping up in thirds behind each clack. And it escalates in payload: it is the only scene that answers a question the viewer has carried since 0:05. S13 destroyed one thing; **S14 transforms the whole street** — one plate becomes three, three become a handoff chain, the chain becomes a front of two hundred that plates every shopfront and stacks off the top of the frame.

**GAG:** **BULK PICKS PEPPI UP BY THE SHELL so he can reach the third slot** — legs pedalling, tongue out, pressing his slice nameplate in with both hands. **It is the first time in the reel any character helps another, and it lands "they start referring each other" a full second before the VO says it.** The payoff gag sits right underneath: when the shutter rattles up, **the three of them are ALREADY INSIDE at a table handing each other cards and they do not look up** — the door he knocked on for two weeks was never locked from the inside. Closing gag: **CANVA, one shop behind for 48 seconds, finally holds up his ONE finished handmade post, beaming with total professional pride, at the exact instant two hundred referral cards blow past him**; one card sticks flat to his face and he topples backwards stiff as a plank. Supporting laughs: the letterbox flap BITES flyer #1 and he has to tug it free; nameplate 2 arrives already taped back together because Bulk rips everything; Bulk over-tightens and bends the brass rail, then bends it back sheepishly; Flossula stops mid-hammer to check his own fangs in the polished plate; **Sparky body-checks Batbro out of the queue he just walked into**; Bloatcorp's revolving door spins so fast with defecting clients it fires Vito out pinstripes-first with the longest ribbon of the reel trailing him.

**LIVING WORLD:** **Shot A:** Pidgey soaked flat on his shoulder sheltering under an umbrella made from one of the rejected flyers — **the only company on a dead street**; a blocked drain overflowing in a steady rope at the kerb; a bus-stop poster peeling wet off its frame; a laundromat window across the road with one drum still spinning; a shop cat watching dry from a ledge; the sodium lamp buzzing and flickering twice; Sparky trotting the full panel width with ears flat and shaking off at lf32. **Shot B:** a moth flies straight into the newly lit doorway at lf80; Pidgey lands on the plaque's top edge at lf82 and is shrugged off by the shutter rattle; the back room's pendant lamp swinging slightly as if someone just stood up; steam off three cups; Sparky asleep under the table on the tote of flyers. **Shot C:** the 30 Bloatcorp designers turning their heads in unison at lf118, **one still face-down asleep and missing it entirely**; Vito flung onto the pavement at lf122, ribbon draping a parked scooter; string lights and neon coming on shop by shop behind the cascade so the street is visibly richer and warmer than the same block was in S2; a bus crossing behind the card band.

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **NONE** — zero words and zero numerals in the entire scene, deliberately. **This is the scene that must not leak a figure:** per containment, 3 exists in this reel ONLY as the S14 nameplates, and here it exists as objects rather than as a numeral. "Three" is carried by **three countable empty slots, three engraved images (molar / dumbbell / slice, never words) and three bolts.** "A couple of weeks" is carried by **14 countable tear-off calendar pages with nothing printed on them.** Nothing is ever written on the plaque. The fourth plate is a HOUSE glyph.

**MUTE CHECK:** there is nothing to cover — the scene has no words. A small soaked figure knocks on doors that stay shut, posting three papers that get bitten, spat back and ignored → the three papers lift and turn gold in mid-air → they slam into three empty holes in a big brass plate, one, two, three, **the last one pressed in by a little guy being held up by a big guy** → three bolts fire → a steel shutter rattles up on a warm room where **those same three people** are already handing each other cards → gold plates bury the entire street. ✓ **The three empty slots are the mute test's load-bearing element and are engineered for it:** dark recessed insets with a hard bright bottom lip on bright brass, at macro scale, with a dedicated glint pass, so EMPTY and exactly THREE are both readable at a glance — which is what makes S1's glint pay off instead of reading as decoration.

**TRANSITION IN:** **match-cut on the arrow.** S13's final move pushes along the faded painted arrow on bare brick; S14 opens on the arrow's TIP and in one frame the golden brick under that point is swapped for the rain-slick edge of a shuttered door at the identical x/y. **Three stamps fire together so it reads as a deliberate jump backwards in time and not a different video:** (1) an instant `saturate(1.0)`→`saturate(0.22)` snap with a two-frame cold-blue flash at 0.35, (2) rain streaks and the umbrella silhouette arriving in the FG plane, (3) **the tracking direction REVERSING.** Same door, same brick pitch, weeks earlier, no colour. A hard cut, never a dissolve. **The exit is the opposite — no cut at all into S15:** the crane simply keeps settling and the last card glints, handing the fully-lit street straight to the postbox.

**SFX:** ALL cues emitted as **L[13] + local** (L[13] = 43.58). **A:** rain_bed +0.00 (v0.30) under everything; knock ×2 +0.20/+0.30 (full), knock ×1 +0.63 (weaker), **NO knock at door 3 — the absence is the sound design's joke**; letterbox_snap +0.40; flyer_wet_slap +0.77; shutter_drop +0.93; leaf_gust + 14-page paper flutter +0.60 (started early with the overlap); a low reversed whoosh under the backwards track; sodium buzz. **THEN FOUR FRAMES OF NEAR-SILENCE +1.43→1.57 before the first clack — the negative space is the punctuation and the most important audio decision in the scene.** **B:** **CLACK_brass ×3 at +1.57 / +1.87 / +2.27, each pitched up a semitone, the third loudest with a brass bloom and a sub-boom under it**; bolt_kachunk ×3 at +2.37 / +2.47 / +2.57; **shutter_rattle_up +2.60 — MEASURE the library file and give it dur+rel ≥ 90% of true length; a long tail at a lower level reads bigger than a short loud chop**; warm room-tone swell +2.83. **C:** card-chain clicks +3.40→3.73 accelerating; cascade_whoosh +3.73; **~40 nameplate clacks +3.80→4.30 pitched into a chord, not a mush**; revolving-door whir +4.13; one dog bark; one final single clack + glint ting +4.40. **Per-cue volumes must be RE-DERIVED — this is the densest scene in the reel.** VO duck baked per-frame from the waveform, ROOT-indexed; **the only place SFX may sit forward is lf68-78, which lands inside the VO gap between "local businesses" and "After that".** Rain bed at −17dB under speech.

**WHY THIS:** the lens is TRANSFORMATION, and this scene has the reel's only true material state-change, so the whole design follows **ONE rectangle for 134 unbroken frames.** The three wet flyers pushed under three doors that shut in his face ARE the three brass nameplates — the same objects, tracked across the cut, converting mid-air via a gold wipe while water beads off and evaporates. **Rejection literally becomes the client**, which is exactly what the VO's two halves say. That morph then compounds without ever changing shape: one rectangle → three in the slots → a fourth handed out → an accelerating chain → a front of two hundred plating the block. A second channel rides the same driver so the eye gets the state-change twice: **world saturation steps 0.22 → 0.48 → 0.74 → 1.00 on the three clacks, so the colour of the world is quantised to the client count and the rain stops on the third one.** The payoff must be PERFORMED, not delivered: each plate is driven home by its own owner with a distinct reaction (Flossula vain, Bulk over-strong, Peppi too short), **and the third requires two of them to cooperate — that single lift does the VO's second half visually, early, and gets the scene's biggest laugh.** Then the reveal inverts the effort: the door opens on a meeting already running, so the joke and the lesson are the same object. **And with the room now seating exactly the three whose plates just went in, and the fourth plate being the HOUSE that S10 planted and never resolved, the payoff frame finally says the same thing in every register at once.** It fixes the arc's biggest identified weakness — the open loop is now a countable physical object, so S1's glint and S14's clack are the same thing at two ends of the reel, landing at 46.05s, 88.7% in.

**REJECTED:** a GRIND MONTAGE built on a tally — a burning-down wall calendar plus chalk knock-marks accumulating on a door, ticking off two weeks and three yeses. (1) It makes the hero a NUMBER: the viewer reads instead of watching, failing the mute test and breaching containment, where 3 may exist only as the nameplates. (2) A tally is a static prop — it accumulates but never BECOMES anything, so there is nothing continuous for the eye to follow and the transformation lens has no subject. (3) It resolves the open loop in the abstract (a count) rather than in the exact physical object S1 planted, which is the one substitution the payoff cannot afford. Also rejected: a giant DEAD-LETTER MAILBAG the hero wades through — more literal to "messaging" and a cheap huge mover, but **S15's base object is the clay postbox and a river of letters, so paper mail would be the base of two consecutive scenes at the worst possible moment** — and you cannot see "three" in a heap of envelopes: a pile is a quantity, three milled slots are a count, and this scene turns on a count. Third: having Claude mount the three nameplates himself — cheaper to build, but it makes him the agent of his own referral, killing both the gag and the point of the line.

---

### S15 · CTA — "ONE SEALED ENVELOPE, HELD OUT TO YOU"
**LINE + DUR:** 48.04–51.92 (3.88s, 116 frames, lf 0-115). *"The carousel workflow is in my bio. Comment POSTS and I'll send you the exact pitch that gets the yes."*

**THE ONE TAKEAWAY:** Everything this street sent me compresses down to ONE sealed thing — and I will hand that exact thing to you. **Comment POSTS.**

**HERO OBJECT:** the clay-orange **CORNER PILLAR POSTBOX** at epic scale — the 15th and final distinct base silhouette, appearing in no other scene. Plinth x330-770 y700-756; cylindrical body x362-738 y240-702; domed cap x342-758 y156-252; **cast-iron finial painted clay** to y110 (⛔ not brass — only the WAX STAMP is brass in this scene). A 428×646 silhouette covering ~57% of the panel at rest. Body gradient authored at 264° (`#EFA070`→`#D2724E`→`#98462A`→`#6E3320`) so the light edge is genuinely on the right, a 26px vertical sheen at x=690, a cool sky rim down x362-368, a rivet seam at x=402 with 9 rivet dots, chipped paint showing dark iron, a rust streak under the mouth, and **a half-torn knockoff BLOATCORP sticker peeling off the lower-left.** **FOUR PARTS DO ALL THE ACTING:** (a) the **SLOT** — recessed x400-700 y308-372, cavity `#060A10`, hinged flap `transformOrigin "50% 0%"`, rotateX 0 → -62° on every hit; (b) the **COLLECTION PLATE** x430-670 y400-472 carrying the word POSTS as raised cast letters; (c) the **BELLY SEAM** x400-700 y560-592 which splits open at lf57 and glows amber THROUGH the metal; (d) the **COLLECTION DOOR** x416-684 y508-690, hinged LEFT on two hinges, keyhole escutcheon, a drawn 10px thickness edge so it reads solid when it swings, plus a small parcel HATCH on the left flank with a chute lip. **Contact shadow ellipse x320-780 y702-752 — deliberately WIDER than the plinth and offset LEFT, or the 646px object floats.**

**SETTING:** the far-RIGHT end of MAPLE ST at deep golden hour — the walk has arrived at the last thing on the block, **so the street reads back down to the LEFT and the whole reel's geography is visible in one frame.** This is the richest, warmest, most-lit version of the street: every string light on, every neon alive, awnings down, bunting up, **and every shopfront now carrying a small brass nameplate glinting by its door (silent S14 callback).** Kerb GY 706, far kerb BGY 646, postbox base y756. Shopfronts recede left in the fixed order: laundromat x0-120, PIZZA PEPPI x120-290, RE/MIX x290-420, PLANET SWOLE x420-520, SMILE DEPOT x520-620, facades y330-646. **The CAROUSEL RIG from S6 turns at canopy cx 190 cy 330 r118, pole to y646, 12 rim bulbs chasing, the luggage tag with the link charm swinging at (190,470) — on screen for the entire "carousel workflow is in my bio" line.** At the vanishing point the **S14 SHUTTER stands OPEN**, x596-664 y420-600, throwing a warm gold rectangle onto the pavement with two tiny silhouettes inside still handing each other cards; it rim-lights Claude's left side. Across the road Bloatcorp sits dim and grey, one lit window, **one designer still face-down asleep** (consistent with every scene — he never woke, and S8's head-lift was a different sprite), revolving door turning with nobody in it.

**SHOT LIST**
- **lf0-10 TRANSITION (match-cut on paper).** NO hard cut. S14's referral cards are still airborne across the top of frame; the crane's rise continues down-and-right and over 10 frames the eight topmost cards SETTLE into identical paper stock as ordinary letters — **the previous scene's debris becomes this scene's raw material.** camZ carries straight through 1.30→1.24 with no reset, origin (506,470). First authored frame is the postbox's clay BELLY filling the lens, so the dominant hue and mass land where S14's brass-and-clay plaque wall left them.
- **lf10-26 (A) THE SOURCE.** Crane back and up: camZ 1.24→1.08, translateY +48→+16. The finished street unfolds. **26 string-light bulbs IGNITE in a left-to-right wave, one per 1.1 frames, each firing its own warm Pool** — a full-panel-width luminance mover. **Five shopfront mail slots FIRE in sequence** (SMILE DEPOT lf12, PLANET SWOLE lf15, RE/MIX lf18, PEPPI lf21, laundromat lf24), each ejecting 2-3 letters that arc UP into a dedicated air-lane y[120,340], **well clear of every sprite head (~y500+), so nothing ever crosses a figure.**
- **lf6-44 PEPPI'S BRACED APPROACH (the gag armed).** PIZZA PEPPI walks in from panel-left, cx 120→250, groundY 742, size 236: **shell hunched, moustache trembling, eyes SHUT (shock 0.55), body leaned back 6°, pizza box held at full arm's length** (prop 130×34 at y486, PAPER lid + clay stripe, wobbling ±2°). **He is braced for the fourth refusal.** The door does not open. Dread beat, held under everything else.
- **lf12 THE COASTER GAG CLOSES.** Foreground-left, Claude sets his coffee cup down on the **STILL-SHRINK-WRAPPED design tablet** on the scooter rack (x54-146, y598-640). A hard glint travels across the plastic; fresh coffee ring. **52 seconds after S1 established it, he has still never opened a design tool.**
- **⭐ lf18-30 THE ARTIFACT, ON THE WORD.** The carousel rig's **LUGGAGE TAG — authored IDENTICAL to S6's fixed version: 140×96, same two-oval chain charm, same swing amplitude** — hangs at (190,470) and **GLINTS once, specular sweep lf24-28, exactly on the word "bio", with nothing else in that pocket of frame moving.** Two matched isolated reads of one object (S6 lf80-85 and here) beats one unreadable read. Whole lit block readable at once, postbox in full silhouette, 18 letters drifting in the air-lane. **LARGE MOVER: Sparky sprints the full panel width along the kerb, x 1060→-60 over lf10-34, size 96, groundY 706 (28px/f).** The S12 cargo trike crosses L→R along y700. **The beat ends the instant the LEAD LETTER tips its nose down toward the slot — that tip is the hook into B.**
- **lf38-52 (B) THE FUNNEL FORMS.** camZ 1.00→1.04, origin migrating to (550,340) — **the lens funnels as the letters do.** All 28 letters (96×66, PAPER `#F7F3EA`, `#D6D0C2` flap, clay corner stripe) bank out of the drift into **ONE descending helix centred on x=550, radius shrinking 300→60 as y runs 160→320, each scaling 1.0→0.52 so the eye reads depth, not just travel.** The vertical corridor x[480,720] is kept sprite-free for the whole shot. - **lf50-70 TWO SIDE GAGS, DEDICATED LANES.** **CANVA** on his fire-escape landing (platform x150-330 y470, Canva cx 236 gY 470 size 120 — **elevated specifically so no near-plane body can ever collide with him, 58px head clearance**) receives his own letter on a dedicated vector from (-80,200) over lf50-58: **it is HIS OWN MOOD BOARD from S1, returned, visibly patched with three strips of tape. He hugs it.** ⛔ **He does NOT raise his finished post — that beat belongs to S14 and is deleted here; his arc closes on being remembered, not on being ignored a second time.** **VITO**, foreground-left behind his own sun-faded hot-dog cart (cart x-16..134 y628..782; Vito cx 66 gY 782 size 168), catches a letter lf56-64 from (-80,540), opens it sheepishly one eye squinted — **the pitch card — and WRAPS A HOT DOG IN IT** (wrong-tool #7, the last and stupidest). **One 40px scrap of invoice ribbon still stuck to his shoe: the empire, reduced to litter.**

Peppi is STILL braced, eyes still shut. Accepted, at last, after four refusals.**
- **lf90-102 THE SEAL.** camZ 1.14→1.20. The envelope rotates three-quarter → face-on (skewX 14°→0, scaleX 0.86→1), now 420×282 at (500,470). lf92: a blob of molten clay wax (`#D2724E`, emissive) drops from top of frame. lf94: it splats on the flap junction, **lighting the underside of Claude's paw from below.** lf94-98: **a BRASS stamp** (the ledger's fourth and final legal brass object) descends and presses **THUNK at lf98** — the wax spreads outward into the 12-point Claude starburst and **POSTS letterpresses INTO the face**, rim cooling `#F0A882`→`#C0603A` over 8 frames. **That cooling is the last colour change in the reel.**
