He looks at the envelope. **Full double-take: head snaps 12° and back, shock 1.0 → cheer 1.0, moustache flies up, shell-HOP 14px off the ground with a dust puff on landing. It lands on the VO word "yes" — Peppi's yes and the viewer's yes are the same beat.** Vito, mid-hot-dog-wrap, looks over and drops the hot dog.
- **lf100-115 HANDED OUT (the only fourth-wall break in 52 seconds).** camZ 1.20→1.26, origin locked (560,520). **Claude turns to LENS (gaze 0, cheer 0.95) — the first time in the reel any character addresses camera — and extends the second sealed envelope toward it** on an ArmProp sibling div (forearm x700 y500 w150 h44 r22, clay, -8°). The envelope travels 240×162 → 340×230, centre drifting (500,470)→(470,470), rotate -14°→-3°, soft radial bloom behind it, hard sun glint sweeping the seal lf108-112. **Its right edge sits at x640 against his body edge at x720 — an 80px clearance gap, so it NEVER occludes him**; the postbox recedes behind it (2px blur + 12% darken) — transformed depth, not occlusion. Behind, at 24% defocus, the whole street throws a rolling thumbs-up wave, 8 sprites 2 frames apart, L→R.
- **lf104-115 BUTTON / LAST FRAME.** **PIDGEY** (traffic-warden cap still on, carried unbroken from S12) snatches an envelope off the postbox DOME at (700,200) and launches up-right toward (930,270) — **routed deliberately outside the hero envelope's box (max x 640).** **SPARKY** launches vertically off the plinth at (860,700) and intercepts at lf114. **FREEZE on lf115: both mid-air in silhouette against the sky, feathers, one envelope spinning free, and the wax-sealed envelope blooming in the foreground filling the lower-centre of the panel.**

**CAMERA:** three moves, each a different family from S13's heroic crane and S14's rainy track → macro lock-off → big crane. **SHOT A CRANE-BACK PULL-BACK:** camZ 1.30→1.00, origin (506,470), whole-scene translateY +48→0, out-cubic; opens cropped on the postbox's clay belly and ends on the whole finished block. **SHOT B NEAR LOCK-OFF WITH A CREEP:** camZ 1.00→1.08, origin (550,340) aimed at the slot, plus a 2px handheld punch on each of the six hero thunks; **the camera holds still so the 28 converging letters carry all the motion.** **SHOT C SLOW MACRO PUSH:** camZ 1.08→1.26, origin locked (560,520), onto the opening door then the sealed envelope. **Bleed solved backwards and audited at PEAK (lf114):** at 1.26 with origin (560,520) the authored-safe box is x∈[128,906], y∈[107,736]. Final envelope 340×230 centred (470,470) → x300-640 y355-585 → maps to 232-712 / 293-650 ✓. Claude's body right edge 880 → 963 ≤996 ✓, left edge 720 → 762 ✓. POSTS plate x430-670 → 396-699 ✓. Peppi at cx 250 → 169 ≥16 ✓. Kerb y756 and the plinth base intentionally crop in Shot C (foreground plane only). Shot A at 1.30 crops the finial and plinth base at lf0 — deliberate, it is the colour-matched belly.

**LIGHT:** low golden-hour sun from CAMERA-RIGHT (world-consistent since S1, and the direction the camera has walked all reel): a disc at (952,224) r58 with a 200px bloom, so every shadow on Maple St rakes LEFT including the postbox's own offset ellipse. Warm key `#FFD9A0`/`#E7B24C` on all right-facing surfaces, cool slate rim `#3A5C84`/`#7FA0C8` on all left-facing edges. Two volumetric god-ray wedges cut down-left at -18° through the alley gaps at x140 and x700, dust in both. Warm Pools under every source: 26 string-light bulbs, all 12 carousel rim bulbs, each neon, the collection plate. **THE LIGHT ARC IS THE SCENE'S ARC, in three stages:** (1) Shot A the postbox is **LIT BY** the street — 26 bulbs ignite in a wave, a full-panel-width luminance mover; (2) lf57 it becomes **SELF-LIT** — the belly seam splits and an amber Pool glows out through the iron, pulsing through the compression hold; (3) lf80 it **FLOODS** the street — a 520×180 gold Pool spills from the open door across the tarmac. **Fourth and final source:** in Shot C the molten wax is its own light, a clay emissive lighting the underside of Claude's paw and the envelope's inner flap from below, then COOLING `#F0A882`→`#C0603A` over 8 frames. **Sky is the warmest of the whole 52s.** Bloatcorp stays deliberately cold and under-lit so the warmth reads as earned.

**DEPTH PLANES:** 1 FOREGROUND BLUR 1.16× blur 3px unlit near-silhouette (the clay scooter's rear wheel + rack bottom-left with the **STILL-SHRINK-WRAPPED tablet**, coffee ring on the plastic; an awning corner top-right; a fire hydrant) · 2 FOREGROUND ACTOR LANE 1.05× **z below hero, never crosses the hero arc** (VITO's sun-faded hot-dog cart far-left x-16..134 y628..782 with Vito at cx 66 groundY 782 size 168, one 40px scrap of ribbon stuck to his shoe) · 3 HERO PLANE 1.0× (the POSTBOX x342..770 y110..756 plus its light spill and the 28 converging letters, which read as one mass with it; **YOU-CLAUDE at cx 800 groundY 736 size 250; PIZZA PEPPI at cx 250 groundY 742 size 236** — spacing: Peppi to postbox left edge 342 = 92px gap at his radius 118, so he is authored 6px in front on z, **contact shadows 0.72 body-width, WIDER than each sprite**) · 4 MID/ELEVATED 0.62× (**CANVA on a fire-escape landing, platform x150..330 y470..488, Canva cx 236 groundY 470 size 120 — elevated specifically so no near-plane body can ever collide with him, 58px clearance above every head below**) · 5 SHOPFRONTS 0.35× (the five facades receding LEFT, **each now carrying a small brass nameplate glinting by its door**; the CAROUSEL RIG canopy cx 190 cy 330 r118 with 12 chasing bulbs and the 140×96 luggage tag at (190,470); two string-light catenaries; **BLOATCORP dark x0..190 y300..470, one lit window, the designer still face-down asleep**, revolving door turning slowly with nobody in it) · 6 FAR 0.15× **blue-haze overlay via colour-mix, not alpha** (clock tower with a lit face, water tower, hazy golden skyline band, two god-ray shafts, a seagull crossing lf28-36).

**ESCALATION:** S14 was the peak (10) — hundreds of nameplates burying a block, resolved on a big crane up. S15 must not repeat that volume and must not fall off a cliff (target 7, warm). It steps up on two axes untouched in 48 seconds. **(1) COMPRESSION, NOT ADDITION:** S14's hundreds of cards fly AT a wall and stick; S15's 28 letters fly INTO one object and change its physical state — it swells, the belly seam splits and glows through the metal, two rivets pop — **and then the sky EMPTIES completely at lf70 for a held breath, the only stillness in the reel, so that many visibly becomes one.** **(2) ADDRESS:** for fourteen scenes the camera has watched Maple St from outside it; at lf100 a character looks down the lens for the first and only time and pushes a sealed envelope at the viewer. Light escalates monotonically too: LIT BY the street → SELF-LIT → FLOODING the street. Scale collapses from a whole block to one object in one paw — **intimacy after spectacle is the only direction left that is not a repeat.**

**GAG:** **THE PIZZA IS FINALLY ACCEPTED** — the reel's oldest running gag, refused four times (the satchel flap in S1, Bloatcorp in S2, the cart flap in S7, red-X-stamped in S9, wax-stamped on his own shell in S12), closed here as a performance rather than a punchline. Peppi enters at lf6 already braced for the next refusal: **eyes SHUT**, shell hunched, leaned back 6°, box at full arm's length, wobbling, **while a storm of mail ignores him completely for two full shots.** At lf86 the little door opens, Claude takes the box with one paw and puts a wax-sealed envelope in his claw. **He is still braced when the trade is already done.** lf96 one eye opens. lf98 full 12° double-take. lf100 shell-HOP 14px off the pavement. **It lands on the VO word "yes".** SUPPORTING, each in its own lane: **VITO**, demoted to a hot-dog cart, receives exactly ONE letter, opens it sheepishly — it is the pitch card — and immediately wraps a hot dog in it, his last and stupidest use of a piece of paper. **CANVA** opens his one letter: **it is HIS OWN MOOD BOARD from S1, returned and taped back together; he hugs it.** **AND THE FIRST GAG CLOSES LAST: the design tablet is STILL shrink-wrapped, fresh coffee ring on the plastic, 52 seconds after S1.** BUTTON: Pidgey steals an envelope and Sparky tackles him mid-air on the final frames.

**LIVING WORLD:** Bloatcorp is dark across the road except one lit window where **the same designer is still face-down asleep — he never woke up for any of it** — and its revolving door turns slowly with one grey figure trapped going round and round. Two tiny silhouettes are still visible deep in the open shutter's glow at the vanishing point, handing each other cards. The laundromat drum tumbles for all 116 frames. Peppi's OPEN neon buzzes and flickers twice. A seagull's shadow crosses the tarmac. **Leftover S8 confetti is still settling onto the postbox dome.** The clock tower's minute hand ticks once at lf66 (S11 callback). One string-light bulb pops out at lf72 and a moth immediately starts circling the dead glass. Bunting lifts on a gust. Two rivets ping off the swelling box and roll into the gutter. The S12 cargo trike crosses left-to-right along y700 with its stamped MONTHLY crates. **Every shopfront down the block now carries a small brass nameplate by its door catching the low sun — the S14 payoff, never pointed at, never explained.**

**ON-SCREEN TEXT:** **POSTS** — one word, in exactly two physical states, never as floating type. (1) On the postbox: **cast into the collection plate x430-670 y400-472 as RAISED metal letters**, Fraunces 900 / 46px / letterSpacing 9 / fill `#6B4A16` / `textShadow "0 1.5px 0 rgba(255,240,200,0.75)"` so it reads embossed, struck by a hard specular glint at lf56 **on the exact frame the VO says the word.** (2) On the envelope: **letterpressed INTO the wax seal at lf98** (circle r46, `#C44A3A`→`#8E2A20`, INK letters 22px/900/ls2, 1px cream highlight — pressed in, never printed on top), blooming as the envelope fills the lens at lf114. **NOTHING ELSE IS LEGIBLE:** shopfront fascias are colour blocks at ≤14px and low contrast, the brass nameplates are pure glint with no text, the envelopes are plain paper with a flap triangle and a clay corner stripe, and the carousel rig shows exterior and its swinging link-charm tag only — no dials, no readable settings, no mechanism (GATE THE HOW). **ZERO NUMERALS: the spine is fully spent by S14 and nothing may leak back in.**

**MUTE CHECK:** a giant clay postbox on a warm, fully-lit street; every shopfront spits letters into the air; they spiral down into its mouth until the box visibly bulges and glows from inside; the sky empties; the little door swings open and instead of a flood, **ONE envelope comes out, gets a wax seal stamped on it, is handed to a braced turtle who explodes with joy — and a second one is pushed straight at YOU.** ✓ Reads as "send this thing a message and it sends you back one specific thing, personally" in well under 2 seconds, with no words at all. **The compression IS the sentence, and the turtle's double-take proves it works even to a viewer who never hears a syllable.**

**TRANSITION IN:** a **MATCH-CUT ON PAPER**, not a cut on camera — the only transition the compression logic permits. S14 ends craned up on a wall of nameplates with a cascade still in the air. S15 opens with those exact cards still airborne — same paper stock, same size, same tumble rate — and over lf0-10 the crane simply continues down and to the right while the eight topmost cards SETTLE into the drift of ordinary letters S15 will spend three seconds funnelling. **No whip, no wipe, no black, no camZ reset: 1.30 carries straight through to 1.24.** The previous scene's payoff object physically becomes this scene's raw material, S14's rising crane resolves into S15's pull-back rather than reversing it, and the first bucket's large-mover coverage survives the boundary intact. It is also geographically honest: the camera has walked strictly left-to-right for fifteen scenes and this is the far-right corner, the last thing on the block.

**SFX:** **EMISSION RULE, non-negotiable: every cue is emitted as L[14] + local seconds (L[14] = 48.04). Scene bodies are NOT Sequence-wrapped, so a scene-local `at` here typechecks, renders and is SILENT. Any cloned root cue map must be DELETED.** Every `dur` is set from a MEASURED source length with dur+rel ≥ 90%. **CUES:** 48.04 warm crowd ambience + golden-hour bed lift (dur 3.9, v0.15) · 48.37 rising 26-step light-ignite shimmer tracking the bulb wave (v0.19) · 48.44 soft coaster clink (coffee cup on the shrink-wrap, v0.12) · 48.68 carousel bulb chime + soft rig hum (v0.16) · **48.92 tag glint ting on the word "bio" (v0.12)** · 49.44/49.54/49.64/49.74/49.84 five shopfront slot-clacks (v0.18) · 49.31-50.31 layered paper flutter-swarm bed, rising in pitch and density (v0.13 ×3) · slot THUNK + flap-snap doubles at 49.61/49.75/49.88/49.99/50.09/50.17, accelerating (v 0.22→0.32) · **49.91 bright metallic glint ding on the POSTS plate (v0.32)** · 49.94 low pneumatic WHUMPF as the belly seam splits (v0.20) · 49.97 + 50.14 two detuned rivet pings (v0.24) · 50.11 comedy pizza-box creak + chute pop · **50.37-50.50 HARD SILENCE, 4 frames, cut everything including the music bed; release the sidechain duck to 1.0 here — the emptiness is the beat** · 50.57 heavy iron hinge creak (dur 1.2, v0.28) · 50.71 warm bloom riser as light floods out · **51.31 WET WAX THUNK, the heaviest cue in the scene and a deliberate rhyme with S12's shell stamp and S14's three nameplates, long warm tail, dur ≥ 0.9× measured (v0.32)** · 51.44 cartoon boing (Peppi's double-take, v0.20) · 51.51 shell-hop thump + dust (v0.16) · **51.64 rising bell shimmer on the seal glint + warm chord swell (v0.30) — the peak-end cue** · 51.79 pigeon squawk (v0.16) + dog bark (v0.15) · 51.86 tackle thump + feather flutter (v0.18). **VO SIDECHAIN:** bake a per-frame duck from the waveform (1.0 in gaps → 0.38 on speech), ROOT-indexed, −12 to −17dB. This VO is near-continuous so set the bed LOW rather than relying on the duck. **Verify by SOLO-RENDERING the SFX stem `--codec=wav` — never by subtracting stems.**

**WHY THIS:** the CTA is where reels go flat, because they stop being a story and start being a card. **Here the CTA IS the last physical event of the story.** "Comment a keyword and I'll send you something" is, physically, a postbox: you put a message in a slot and one specific object comes back to one specific person. That maps with zero abstraction — the comment is the letter, the DM is the sealed envelope, **and the keyword is CAST INTO THE IRON of the thing you post into**, so the one word the viewer must type is a physical property of the hero rather than a caption pasted over it. Structurally it is the cleanest close available: a postbox is the fifteenth genuinely distinct silhouette, it lives natively on the corner of a street we have already built so no new world is invented, and it is a real device, so its slot, plate, seam and door are legitimate chrome rather than a gratuitous container. It gives the scene a three-stage **LIGHT ARC** — lit by the street, then self-lit, then flooding the street — which is exactly the "resolution feels warmer and richer than the setup" beat the arc demands. It gives four running gags a physical mechanism to close on (a parcel trade for Peppi's pizza, a tray for Vito's hot-dog wrapper, one letter for Canva's returned mood board, a coaster for the shrink-wrapped tablet). And **the viewer watches somebody else receive the thing they are being asked to comment for, and then it is offered to them.** That is the peak-end image: a single wax-sealed envelope, glinting, held out to you, impossible to read as anything other than "this gets sent to me if I comment."

**REJECTED:** a giant glowing **PHONE / DM INBOX** as the hero — the obvious CTA object, notification bubbles pouring into a screen. (1) It is a screen, so the scene becomes a container full of UI, exactly what rules 1 and 6 exist to stop, and every DM-inbox CTA in the category looks identical, which costs the reel its ending. (2) It has no material state to change — an inbox cannot swell, split a seam, pop rivets or emit volumetric light, so both the light arc and the physical escalation collapse and the scene goes static under a 3.88s VO. (3) It has no geography: a floating phone belongs nowhere, throwing away the reel's strongest structural idea in the final frame. (4) **It cannot hand you anything, and the whole line is that a specific object comes back to a specific person.** Also rejected: **THE ORIGAMI MORPH** — the postbox unfolding and refolding into a giant envelope. It is the more literal read of "transformation" but **the postbox must REMAIN a postbox for "I'll send you this" to be literal**; a building that turns into paper reads as magic, and the reel's whole argument is that none of this is magic. Also: a MAILBAG/satchel hero, which collides head-on with S1's courier satchel at the exact moment the reel needs to feel finished; and a post-office COUNTER INTERIOR with all six owners queueing — funnier per character, but it breaks the world rule that the camera never leaves Maple St, reuses the queue silhouette already spent in S3 and S14, and cannot show the street being visibly warmer and more lit than it was in S2, **which is the reel's entire visual argument.**

## CONTINUITY TABLE — 15 DISTINCT BASE OBJECTS

Crop every frame to its hero and confirm 15 different silhouettes. A small recurring accent (the shrink-wrapped tablet, the Claude starburst) is an identity thread, not a base.

| # | scene | BASE OBJECT | silhouette class | verb it owns | material (post-ledger) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | HOOK | courier **SATCHEL** on the scooter rack | soft leather bag with a hinged jaw | CHOMP / SWALLOW / REFUSE | leather + **BRASS** counter *(ledger 1 of 4)* |
| S2 | PROBLEM | the **TRIPOD TOTEM** | built junk tower, 550px tall | COLLAPSE | mixed junk + grey gaffer tape |
| S3 | PROMISE | the clay **VENDOR CART** | mushroom-topped barrow, **smooth round dome, stub pole** | UNFOLD | clay-orange steel + cream beadboard |
| S4 | STEP1 | the **GRABCO CLAW MACHINE** | tall glass-fronted coin cabinet | GRAB / LOCK OUT | chrome + slate enamel + glass |
| S5 | REUSE | the **STENCIL PLATE** | flat milled hand tool with an aperture | PRINT / REPEAT | **BRASS** *(ledger 2 of 4)* |
| S6 | STEP2 | the **CAROUSEL RIG** | **faceted 8-gore cone** on a full vertical pole | ERECT ITSELF | chrome pole + clay/paper canopy |
| S7 | LOAD | the **NAPKIN → CARD DECK** | paper stock, fanned ribbon | UPGRADE | paper *(cowl demoted to 240×175 **copper**)* |
| S8 | BUILT | the **SCISSORS + RIBBON** | crossed blades / a red line | CUT / RELEASE | chrome + red satin |
| S9 | STEP3 | the **PRICE TAG** | hanging slab → hanging card | SHRINK | slate steel → cream cardboard |
| S10 | EASYYES | the **TILL DRAWER** | horizontal open trough on a rail | OPEN / ACCEPT | **chrome** + oak |
| S11 | MATH | the **MARKET SCALE** | twin-pan balance on a column | BALANCE | **nickel silver** + copper pans |
| S12 | RETAINER | the **PARKING METERS** | domed head on a post, in a row | FLIP / SEAL | cast iron + **copper** plate |
| S13 | TWIST | the **GURU BILLBOARD** | rooftop rectangle above the roofline | TEAR / UNCOVER | vinyl + rusted steel + galv grommets |
| S14 | GRIND | the **BRASS PLAQUE** | wall plate with three recessed slots | FILL / UNLOCK | **BRASS** *(ledger 3 of 4)* |
| S15 | CTA | the **POSTBOX** | fat cylinder with a domed cap | COMPRESS / SEND | cast iron painted clay + **BRASS** stamp *(ledger 4 of 4)* |

**Adjacency checks that previously failed and are now resolved:**
- **S3 vs S6** (both canopies): S3 = smooth stretched dome, single clay colour, no visible ribs, stub pole below the hem, lit from the tarmac up. S6 = faceted 8-gore cone, gold seam piping, 12-tab valance, 20-bulb rim, full architectural pole, lit by its own bulbs. **The luggage tag is removed from S3 entirely.**
- **S6 vs S7** (rig reused): the cowl is shrunk to 240×175 and pushed to the upper-right third; the napkin holds the lower-left two-thirds. Crop-test lf46/52/62.
- **S1 vs S15** (both containers that swallow paper and pop a fastener): differentiated by verb — S1 CHOMPS and REFUSES (a jaw, animate, four times), S15 COMPRESSES many into one and then SENDS. S1's fastener pop is a buckle under strain; S15's is rivets under internal pressure that then becomes light.
- **S7 vs S15** (macro mouth swallowing paper): S7's cowl is copper, off-centre, and 240px; S15's slot is a recessed letterbox in a 646px cylinder that becomes a light source.
- **S9 vs S12** (kerb-height readouts): S9 is a HANGING slab on chains under an awning that shrinks; S12 is a POSTED head on a plinth in a receding row that seals. S9 ends legible ($20); S12 is pictorial only.
- **S1 vs S14** (the shutter): S1's base is the satchel; the door is a **0.20s terminal beat with residual camera drift and no dead stop**. S14's base is the plaque in macro lock-off.
- **The four waves:** S8 lateral · S10 Z-axis depth · S12 outward from mid-block · S14 vertical top-down.

## FIXES APPLIED FROM THE THREE CRITIQUES

**Structural / continuity**
1. **MATERIAL LEDGER published.** Brass restricted to four objects (S1 counter, S5 stencil, S14 plaque + nameplates, S15 wax stamp); S6→chrome, S7→copper, S10→chrome, S11→nickel silver + copper, S12→copper, S13→galvanised, S15 finial→painted cast iron. The travelling-glint + high-chime SFX pair is reserved for the plaque and the arrowhead only. *(Critique 1 — the loop's payoff metal was the reel's default metal in 11 of 15 scenes.)*
2. **String-light embargo enforced.** Deleted from S5's light block and from S7's setting/light. **No street, string or festoon light ignites before 29.56s (S8 lf36).** S5's warmth evidence is now interior windows + widening god-rays.
3. **S14 back room reseated** as Flossula / Bulk / Peppi — the same three whose plates just went in. Batbro moved outside entirely (arrives on foot at lf106, body-checked by Sparky); his ceiling grapple line deleted.
4. **Fourth nameplate changed from a coffee cup to the HOUSE glyph**, handed to Batbro at lf95 — closing S10's four-flag plant (molar/dumbbell/house/slice) instead of introducing an unearned glyph at the payoff frame.
5. **The four block-wide waves re-axised** (S8 lateral / S10 depth / S12 outward-from-centre / S14 vertical), so the climax no longer reads as the fourth run of a house move.
6. **BLOATCORP pinned to a fixed world X**; scenes where it falls outside x[-300,1312] omit it rather than repositioning it (S2, S8, S11 flagged). **S8's head-lift reassigned to a different sprite** so the permanent sleeper never wakes in any scene — consistent with S15's claim.
7. **FACADE PALETTE LOCKED** in shared constants; **clay `#D2724E` banned from all facades, awnings and signage** (S5 had painted SMILE DEPOT in the hero clay).
8. **Vito's ribbon converted to a numeric ledger** (120 → 760 → a 40px scrap); every prose superlative deleted.
9. **Pidgey's traffic-warden cap dated to S12** and carried through S13/S14/S15 (S8's note said "not until S13"; it is introduced in S12).
10. **S2's "rainwater held since S1" replaced with a burst gutter joint dripping from frame zero** — the reel's only rain is S14's flashback.
11. **Sparky is no longer permanently orange** after S5 (no downstream scene could carry it) — he lands in a grey dust cloud and shakes out clean.

**Number spine / text**
12. **S9's `$2,000` and `$200` DELETED.** The false landing is a blurred four-module state with only the `$` resolved; `$20` at lf49 is the scene's first and only legible figure. Captions carry no numeral over lf14-30. Spine restored to seven figures in seven scenes.
13. **S12's MONTHLY reduced to ONE instance** (the copper plate); the crate stencil replaced with a wax blob + chevron.
14. **S3's OPEN reduced to 34px on the flap's lower edge, glint sweep deleted** — signage, not a title card.
15. **S4's GRABCO plate rendered at full opacity** with wear painted as flecks. **All alpha-fades on background planes replaced with colour-mixing toward the sky** (S12, S13, S15) — no content anywhere below 0.85 opacity.

**Occlusion / geometry (all previously declared clear and arithmetically false)**
16. **S4:** molar wall constrained to x430-796 / y300-510 with a 6px chrome mullion at x420 and Vito in a dedicated left bay — the payoff no longer buries the scene's only laugh. **The giant molar lands at cx700 and Claude moves to x965**, restoring 190px.

37. **S15's compression hold instrumented** with a pulsing belly-seam glow and a continuing scaleX creak, plus a mandatory `motion_audit.py` check across 50.0-51.0s.

**Pacing / scroll risk**
38. **S2's thesis morph pulled forward to 7.57s** (a 200px half-ribbon lands in Vito's hand at lf58) so the scene's only new idea arrives before the swipe decision, not 3.4s after it.
39. **S3's Sparky/Canva herding moved from lf34 to lf14** — a laugh inside the first half-second of the promise scene.
40. **S8 given 6 frames from S7's deck tail** (never a new element) and **the ribbon recolour deleted**, buying the whole 8-frame window for the load-bearing thread-and-sag beat. **A second laugh added** (the Bloatcorp copycat wave in dead fluorescent green) to fill f16-56, which previously had no gag.
41. **S10's shot A trimmed to 24 frames** and **the one-pixel-tolerance hard cut replaced with a 5-frame whip at a held camScale** — the morph is now renderer-enforced.
42. **S14's rain compressed to 1.27s** (calendar gust overlapped from lf18, door 3 cut to 6 frames) with the recovered frames given to the shutter-reveal hold (5f → 8f).
43. **S1's dead stop softened** — the halt is 6 frames with a 1.4px/frame residual drift, the "nothing else moves" clause deleted, Sparky's full-width sprint runs through it, and **the f138 audio duck changed from near-silence to a 6dB dip.**
44. **S8's decorative plaque glint DELETED** — the light wave dying one frontage short re-arms the loop by absence, and S13's arrowhead-and-plaque chime pair is no longer pre-spent.
45. **⭐ THE LOOP SIGHTING ADDED at S5 lf96-103** — a 7-frame, 60px, un-glinted glimpse of the one dark frontage. The loop was previously untouched from 5.0s to 43.0s; a loop nobody has thought about for 23 seconds gets *explained* rather than *paid off*.
46. **S6/S15's luggage tag enlarged to 140×96** with a 6-frame isolated glint in both scenes (nothing else moving) — the one thing the reel is selling was previously its least legible object.
47. **S7's pizza refusal restored** (the cart flap snaps shut on Peppi's box at lf30-35), so his acceptance arc is not pre-spent eight scenes early.
48. **S12's meter row cut from nine to eight with a scale floor of 0.42**, every meter beyond M4 given a 2px warm rim and a 26×18 minimum flag — anything that cannot carry them is cut from the row, not shrunk into it.

**Rejected fixes (and why)**
- *"S1 and S14 share the shutter, therefore S1 repeats S14's base object."* **Rejected as stated.** S1's base is unambiguously the satchel (62-86% of the panel for 130 of 167 frames); the shutter is set dressing in a terminal beat. The *mitigation* is accepted and applied (fix 43) — the halt is shortened and never fully stops — but the scene is not redesigned.
- *"Delete S10; it restates S9."* **Rejected.** S9 sets the price; S10 proves nobody argues with it, which is a different proposition and the direct setup for S11's fairness question. Its real defects were the fragile cut, the derivative wave and the missing gag — all fixed (41, 5, 28).
- *"Remove S7's egg timer; the deck already carries speed."* **Rejected.** The timer is the ONLY carrier of "under 1 minute", and the spine requires that figure to live somewhere. Instead it is enlarged to Ø200 in the C3 foreground and given a dedicated clack.
- *"S12's wave should run from both ends inward."* **Partially rejected.** Adopted the single-origin mid-block outward version instead — one origin reads as propagation; two origins read as two waves and re-introduce a scripted sweep.

## STANDING BUILD CONSTRAINTS (hand these to every scene author)
1. Scene bodies are **PANEL-LOCAL 0..1012 × 0..792**. Anything `top > 792` is clipped. Only the CTA chrome uses screen coords.
2. `over(f, start, dur)` takes **FRAMES** — always `over(lf, fr(2.4), fr(0.5))`, never a bare number.
3. `ramp(f,a,b)` maps f FROM [a,b] TO 0..1 — not a lerp. `a < b` strictly.
4. Valid easings only: quad, cubic, sin, circle, exp, poly, bounce, back, elastic, bezier, ease, linear, step0/1. ⛔ No `Easing.quart/quint/expo/circ` — **every "out-quint/out-quart" in these cards must be authored as `Easing.out(Easing.cubic)` or a bezier.**
5. Clamp every pop/flash: `lf < fr(X) ? 0 : Math.max(0, 1 - (lf-fr(X))/9)`.
6. Never `background:` + `backgroundImage:` in one style object (renders transparent).
7. Sprites use `<Actor x groundY size flip shadow>` — contact shadow WIDER than the body; spacing ≥ 0.85×(rA+rB).
8. ⛔ **`<Sfx at={}>` is ROOT-timeline seconds** — emit `L[i] + local`, never a scene-local number. **The cloned chassis's root cue map is DELETED and all cues re-authored.**
9. Every 1s bucket must read ≥4.0 on `tools/motion_audit.py`, cropped to the PANEL — ≥1 element of ≥40,000px² moving ≥6px/frame at all times.
10. **Solve `origin+(x-origin)*maxScale ≤ 996` BACKWARDS before authoring coordinates, and audit the overflow at PEAK camScale, never on a static frame.**
11. **Prefix every asset with the reel number** — `vo_66_posts.wav`, `words_66_posts.json`. `public/` is gitignored; a clobbered .wav has no backup.
12. VO sidechain duck baked per-frame from Alex's waveform (1.0 in gaps → 0.38 on speech), **indexed on the ROOT frame**, target −12 to −17dB. Verify by solo-rendering the stem `--codec=wav`, never as mix − vo − music.
13. First render is a **WIREFRAME, never delivered** — Gate A (hook interrupt) + Gate B (per-scene visual overhaul) must both pass, then a GLOBAL audit stage after any per-scene fan-out.

## ARBITRATION — S11 → S12 match cut (decided at build time, 2026-07-18)

The S11 card mandates a crane back **down** (`cTY -300 → -40`) to land the shadow rake, the deck-chair
reveal and Claude's one-finger salute. The S12 card asks S11 to land its clock face centred at panel
**(501, 268)** on its final frame for a match cut. These are geometrically incompatible: a d≈300 dial at
y268 puts the pavement at screen y≈1054.

**DECISION: keep the deck-chair payoff. The match cut is on X only.**
S11 now lands the dial at screen **x = 500.6** (fixed during the washout repair) but lets it exit the top of
frame as the crane descends. Rationale: the board's own standing rule is "a laugh or a surprise in EVERY
scene" — deleting S11's only gag to satisfy an internal continuity nicety is a bad trade, and an x-aligned
hard cut still reads as a match. Revisit only if the overhaul pass shows the S11→S12 join as a jump.

## KNOWN-OPEN (first pass, not yet addressed)

The full [[reel-overhaul-stage]] Gate A / Gate B pass has NOT been run. Only the six defects measured or
observed in the v1 render were repaired (S2 dead air · S5 empty quadrant · S8 hero blur · S9 hero-number
blur · S10 flat wash rebuild · S11 washout). Everything else is first-draft.
